-## 0. Facts we build on
-
-| Fact | Source |
-|---|---|
-| All 8 examples reproduce `examples/expected/*.json` rows and cycles exactly | [measured] |
-| `bin/minivm --schema examples/data.json` regenerates `examples/schema.json` exactly, so we can make schemas for our own data | [measured] |
-| One `bin/minivm` run takes ~42 ms (mostly process start); python start ~31 ms; 16 cores | [measured] |
-| Example cycles: q1 13766, q2 3617, q3 8562, q4 18442, q5 5518, q6 6338, q7 2596, q8 1524 | [measured] |
-| Hot spots: q4 GATHER 6672 + UPPER/RTRIM 5132; q1 CAT+LEFT 7124; q3/q6 self-HJOIN+GROUP ≈ 3200 each; redundant SORTs in q1/q4/q5 | [measured, prefix-diff profiler] |
-| Dead `DIV x,0` still errors, so dead-code removal can only remove errors (allowed: "if the input errors, anything goes") | [measured] |
-| NUM prints keep scale (`"2.0"` vs `"2.00"`); INT+NUM gives NUM; INT beyond ±2^63 prints as a string | [measured] |
-| HJOIN = NLJOIN results, ordered by (i, j); NULL keys never match unless `NULLEQ` | [measured] |
-| GROUP treats NULL = NULL, and `1` and `1.00` share a group; groups are numbered by first appearance | [measured] |
-| IDX drops FALSE and NULL; IF treats a NULL condition as FALSE; `IS` is never NULL | [measured] |
-| Type errors are per row, and never on empty or all-NULL columns, so whether a program fails depends on its data | [measured] |
-| Empty GALL: GCOUNT 0, GSUM/GMIN/GFIRST NULL; GATHER of a NULL index errors | [measured] |
-| VM limits: 20k instructions, 4M values per register, 64M total, ~300M work, 10k rows per table | docs |

-## 1. Requirements → how we meet them → how we prove it
+## 1. Requirements: how we meet each one, and how we prove it
 
 | # | Requirement (one-pager) | How we meet it | Proof |
 |---|---|---|---|
-| R1 | `./optimize program.mini schema.json` prints a program | Python 3 stdlib only, no deps; on any internal failure it prints the input unchanged | runner checks exit 0 and that `bin/minivm` parses the output |
-| R2 | Same results on any data satisfying the schema constraints | rewrites are justified only by semantics plus **constraints** (never stats); each rewrite has a written safety rule (§3) | evaluator over many generated datasets (§4) plus mutant-optimizer recall test |
-| R3 | Order doesn't matter unless the program specifies it | the optimizer always **preserves exact order** (strictest); the evaluator compares order only when the original specifies it (§2 Q1) | rubric O2 |
-| R4 | Numbers within 1e-6 relative | the optimizer avoids changing arithmetic, so values are usually bit-identical; the comparator uses a relative tolerance | rubric O3 |
-| R5 | Input errors → anything goes | the evaluator skips (program, data) pairs where the original errors | rubric O5 |
-| R6 | ≤1 s per program | linear-ish passes and a hard internal deadline (0.8 s) that falls back to the best result so far | runner times every call; p100 reported |
-| R7 | Fewer cycles, "noticeably faster" on unseen programs | general passes, not example-specific hacks; measured on held-out generated programs | perf report: speedup per program and geomean |
-| R8 | `./evaluate OPTIMIZER` exits 0 or non-zero; any optimizer with the same CLI | black-box: calls `OPTIMIZER prog schema` as a subprocess | identity optimizer → exit 0; mutant optimizers → non-zero |
-| R9 | Evaluator ≤2 min, may use `bin/minivm` | parallel pool (16 workers), 100 s wall-clock budget, deterministic seed | runner reports wall time |
-| R10 | Submission: code, prompts, logs, raw sessions, git history, no binaries, models and spend, "few days" plan | §10 | checklist before sending |
+| R1 | `./optimize program.mini schema.json` prints a program | Python 3 stdlib. On any internal error, or an estimate that isn't cheaper, it prints the input unchanged | runner: exit 0, `bin/minivm` parses the output |
+| R2 | Same results on any data satisfying the schema's constraints | rewrites justified only by semantics and **constraints** (never stats); safety rules in §5 | evaluator over generated data, plus meta-eval |
+| R3 | Order doesn't matter unless the program specifies it | the optimizer always preserves order; the evaluator compares in order when the original specifies it (§4.3) | `b_reverse` caught (172 defects) **[M]** |
+| R4 | Numbers within 1e-6 relative | the optimizer doesn't change arithmetic; the comparator compares Decimal values with relative tolerance | `h_tiny_drift` OK, `b_drift` caught **[M]** |
+| R5 | If the input program errors, anything goes | the evaluator skips pairs where the original errors | rubric O5 |
+| R6 | ≤ 1 s per program | linear passes; internal deadline at 0.8 s, then fall back | runner reports max optimizer time |
+| R7 | Noticeably faster, on unseen programs and data | general passes (§3), held-out generated programs | perf report: geomean speedup on examples and on held-out programs |
+| R8 | `./evaluate OPTIMIZER` exits 0 or non-zero; works for any optimizer with the same CLI | black box: subprocess call plus `bin/minivm` only | meta-eval verdict table |
+| R9 | Evaluator ≤ 2 min, may use `bin/minivm` | platform binary (8.3 ms/run), parallel, watchdog at 100 s | wall time in every report |
+| R10 | Submission: code, prompts, logs, raw sessions, git history, no binaries, models and spend, "few days" plan | §11 | checklist |

-## 2. Open questions → default we use
+## 2. What the examples were hiding (learnings from hand-optimizing)
 
-| # | Question | Default |
+Each row: the original program's cost, what made it slow, and what that means for the optimizer.
+
+| Example | Cycles orig → hand **[M]** | Hidden lesson |
 |---|---|---|
-| Q1 | When does the program "specify" order? | Order is specified iff the row positions reaching `OUT` come from a `SORT`/`TOPK` and no later reordering (join/group) loses that order. Otherwise compare as multisets. The **optimizer** preserves order regardless. |
-| Q2 | Is `"2.0"` vs `"2.00"` a difference? Or INT `2` vs NUM `"2.0"`? | Numbers are compared by value (rule R4), so no. Type is not compared for numbers; STR/DATE/BOOL/NULL must match exactly. |
-| Q3 | Does a NULL equal a NULL in output comparison? | Yes. NULL vs non-NULL is a defect. |
-| Q4 | Is a different `ncols` or column order a defect? | Yes, always. |
-| Q5 | Optimized program errors, times out (>1 s) or hits a VM limit where the original succeeds? | Defect. |
-| Q6 | Optimizer crashes or prints garbage? | Defect (counted as "optimizer failure"). |
-| Q7 | Can we rely on `stats`? | Only for **cost choices** (HJOIN vs NLJOIN, which side to filter), never for correctness. Stats describe one dataset. |
-| Q8 | "Faster" measured how? | `cycles` reported by `bin/minivm`, on the example data and on generated data. |
-| Q9 | Can the optimizer run `bin/minivm`? | Not required, and we don't depend on it (the grader may not provide it to `optimize`). |
-| Q10 | Hidden programs: same shape as the examples? | Assume "legacy" SQL-ish plans: wide GATHER fan-outs, late filters, self-joins, redundant sorts, dead columns. |
+| q0001 | 13766 → 1703 (8.1×) | 52% of the cycles are CAT and LEFT building a label for rows a later filter throws away. **Order of work matters more than the work itself.** `SORT` on `lines` is a no-op because of the `sorted` constraint. `GE l_qty,1` is always TRUE because of `not_null` + `check`. |
+| q0002 | 3617 → 2011 (1.8×) | The self-join's *counted* side must stay unfiltered. Filtering it too is the naive bug (`q0002.naive`, caught on 17 datasets). Only the outer side may shrink. |
+| q0003 | 8562 → 2817 (3.0×; 37× on large) | A "count of better rows" self-join is O(n²) per group. A sort plus per-group rank is linear. The HJOIN silently drops NULL and `''`→NULL keys, and the rewrite must reproduce that. |
+| q0004 | 18442 → 2179 (8.5×; 47× on large) | 36% of the cycles are GATHER fan-out across 4 joins. A SORT before GALL/GSUM is useless. **`customers` has no key**, so de-duplicating `c_id` is wrong (`q0004.naive`). |
+| q0005 | 5518 → 3294 (1.7×) | Join, then sort, then GROUP de-dup is really a **semi-join**. De-duplicate the small side first. |
+| q0006 | 6338 → 2091 (3.0×; 20× on large) | A self-join followed by GMAX is GROUP + GMAX + GID. GROUP puts NULLs in one group but HJOIN never matches them, so an explicit NULL filter is required (`q0006.naive`). |
+| q0007 | 2596 → 637 (4.1×) | Filter first, then TOPK instead of SORT, then build the label for 5 rows. The example data has only 3 July-2023 orders and no tied amounts, so `TOPK 4` and a flipped tie-break both **passed on the example data**. |
+| q0008 | 1524 → 1249 (1.2×) | Already tight. Dead loads are the only waste. Not every legacy program is slow. |
+
+**Cross-cutting.**
+1. The biggest wins are generic, and the clever rewrites are the riskiest:
+   - The generic wins are filter-first, late materialization, GATHER sinking, dead-code elimination, and TOPK instead of SORT.
+   - The self-join idioms give the largest gains on large data, and they are also the riskiest.
+2. Constraints carry the correctness argument:
+   - `not_null` + `refs` to a `key` means exactly one match.
+   - `sorted` means a SORT on those keys is a no-op.
+   - A table with **no key** can fan out a join.
+3. **Weak data hides bugs.**
+   - A q0004 baseline was missed until the generator planted the programs' own literals (`'KITCHEN'`).
+   - Every gain is relative to the data: on small datasets speedups shrink to 1.2–3× because the filter results are often empty.
 
 ---
 
 ## 3. Optimizer design
 
-**Pipeline:** parse → SSA IR (rename registers, def-use, value metadata: source table, row domain, type, nullability, sortedness) → passes to a fixpoint → cost check → emit.
-**Global guard:** if the optimized program's estimated cost ≥ the original's, or any pass throws, emit the original.
-
-### Rewrites ranked by (gain ÷ risk)
-
-| Rank | Rewrite | Gain [est] | Risk | Safety rule |
-|---|---|---|---|---|
-| 1 | **Dead-code elimination** (unused LOAD/GATHER/string ops; e.g. q7 `upper r14`, `dow r15`) | 5–25% | very low | Removing instructions only removes possible errors. Never remove `OUT`. |
-| 2 | **GATHER composition and sinking**: `GATHER(GATHER(s,i),j)` → `GATHER(s, GATHER(i,j))`; carry *index vectors* through joins and filters and gather payload columns only at the end, only for columns that reach `OUT` | 20–50% on wide plans (q1, q4, q5) | low | Identity of index composition. Indices are in range by construction (same as the original). |
-| 3 | **CSE**: duplicate LOADs and identical ops (q2 loads `l_disc` twice) | small | very low | Pure ops are deterministic. |
-| 4 | **Late materialization of element-wise ops**: compute `f(x)` after filters (`f(GATHER(x,i)) = GATHER(f(x),i)`), only on surviving rows | 20–40% (q1, q4, q7 string ops) | low | Evaluating on a *subset* of rows can only remove errors. **Never** move an op onto rows it wasn't evaluated on, and never onto a join-expanded domain larger than the original (cost check). |
-| 5 | **Constraint rewrites**: `SORT` keys = prefix of `sorted` ASC NF on an unpermuted table → identity; predicate always TRUE by `not_null` + `check` (q1 `GE l_qty,1`) → drop it; `key` uniqueness ⇒ joins are at most 1:1, so a GROUP on key cols is identity | 5–15% | low-med | Uses only declared constraints. Must also prove the column isn't permuted or filtered in between (sortedness survives IDX/GATHER-by-ascending-index only). |
-| 6 | **Filter pushdown below joins** (q4 filters after 4 joins) | 30–70% on q4-like plans | med | Filtering the left side with IDX preserves the (i, j) output order. Applies only when the predicate depends on one side. Cast-free: same ops, fewer rows. |
-| 7 | **Sort reduction**: `SORT` + slice-first-n → `TOPK`; sort after filters, not before; drop sorts whose order is later destroyed | 5–20% | med | TOPK = first n of the same stable SORT (doc). Ties are preserved because both are stable. |
-| 8 | **Join algorithm choice** HJOIN ↔ NLJOIN by cost model | small-med | very low | Identical results by spec; only cost differs. Stats are fine here. |
-| 9 | **Idiom rewrites** (pattern-matched, each behind a precise matcher): self-join + GROUP + GMAX → `GROUP/GMAX/GID/GATHER` (q6); "count of better rows" top-n per group → sort + rank (q3); join-then-dedup semi-join → dedup the right side first (q5) | 2–10× on those programs | **high** | Must match NULL semantics exactly: HJOIN drops NULL keys but GROUP keeps them, so filter out NULL keys explicitly; GMAX "first of equals" printing scale. Only enabled after the evaluator passes with 0 defects on targeted stress data. |
-
-**Rule: never trade correctness for cycles.** Each rewrite ships with a unit test (before/after program pair) plus targeted adversarial data.
-
-**VM-limit safety:** no rewrite may increase any register's maximum length or the total HJOIN/GROUP input (work limit). This is checked by the cost estimator.
+**Pipeline.**
+1. Parse into an SSA IR.
+2. Annotate each register: source table, row domain, type, nullability, sortedness, ordered/monotone.
+3. Run the passes to a fixpoint.
+4. Estimate cost with the `[doc: cycles.md#cost-table]` model.
+5. Emit.
+
+**Global guard:** if the estimated cost isn't lower, an intermediate result would grow, or any pass throws an exception, emit the input unchanged.
+
+| Rank | Rewrite | Gain | Risk | Safety rule (§5) | Prototype evidence |
+|---|---|---|---|---|---|
+| 1 | Dead-code elimination + CSE | 5–25% | very low | S1 | `h_dce` honest, 0 false alarms |
+| 2 | GATHER composition / sinking; gather only the columns that reach OUT | 20–50% on wide plans | low | S7 | q0001, q0004 |
+| 3 | Filter first; run element-wise ops after filters (late materialization) | 4–8× on q0001/q0004/q0007 | low | S2, S6 | q0007 2596→637 **[M]** |
+| 4 | Sort reduction: TOPK for SORT+slice, dead SORT before order-insensitive aggregates, SORT on `sorted` keys is identity | 5–20% | med | S4, S5 | q0001, q0004, q0007 |
+| 5 | Filter pushdown below joins (one-sided, total predicates) | 30–70% on q0004-like plans | med | S2, S3 | q0004 |
+| 6 | Constraint rewrites: always-TRUE predicates, exactly-one-match joins | small | med | S8 | q0001 |
+| 7 | Join algorithm choice (HJOIN↔NLJOIN by cost; stats allowed here) | small | very low | — | `h_join_swap` honest; NLJOIN is cheaper when one side has ≤ ~5 rows |
+| 8 | Idioms: self-join+aggregate → GROUP; rank via sort; semi-join de-dup | 2–47× | **high** | S3, S4, S9 | q0003, q0005, q0006 |
+
+**Skip entirely:** arithmetic re-association, join reordering, anything that uses stats for correctness.
+**Gate:** rank 8 is enabled only after 0 defects on NULL-, tie-, scale- and empty-table-heavy data.
 
 ---
 
 ## 4. Evaluator design (`./evaluate OPTIMIZER`)
 
-1. **Corpus (seeded, deterministic).**
-   - The 8 example programs.
-   - **Generated programs**: a template generator over the example schema that emits legacy-style plans: scan/filter/project, 1–4 joins along `refs`, group-aggregate, sort/topk/slice, string transforms (NULLIFE/RTRIM/UPPER/CAT/LEFT), dates, arithmetic, with deliberate bloat (wide fan-outs, late filters, dead columns, self-joins).
-   - **Mutants of the examples** (swap predicates or literals, change sort keys or directions).
-   - Programs are type-correct by construction, and are kept only if the original runs without error on at least one dataset.
-2. **Data (per schema).** A constraint-respecting generator: unique `key`s, `refs` drawn from the parent key (plus NULLs where nullable), `check` bounds including boundaries, `sorted` order, `not_null`. It is adversarial: NULLs, ties, `'Open '`/`'OPEN'`/`'open'`, empty strings, empty tables, 1-row tables, duplicate join keys, NUM scale variants (`1.0` vs `1.00`), boundary dates. Every dataset is validated by `bin/minivm --schema` (it checks constraints), and its schema file is produced the same way.
-3. **Run.** For each program: call `OPTIMIZER prog.mini schema.json` once (1 s timeout). Then run the original and optimized programs on K datasets (K≈8–12).
-4. **Compare** with the rubric in §5. Skip pairs where the original errors.
-5. **Report** defects with a minimal repro (program, optimized program, dataset, diff) saved to `eval_out/`. Exit 1 if there are any defects (or optimizer failures), else 0.
-6. **Budget** [est from measurements]: ~60 programs × (1 optimizer call + 2×10 VM runs) ≈ 1,300 runs × 42 ms ÷ 16 workers ≈ 4–8 s, plus optimizer time ≤ 60 s worst case in parallel. A hard watchdog at 100 s stops new work, prints the report, and exits non-zero if any defect was found so far.
-
-**Evaluator self-test (meta-eval):**
-- The identity optimizer (`cat`) must give 0 false alarms.
-- A set of **known-bad mutant optimizers** must each be caught: drop a filter, flip `ASC`/`DESC`, drop `NULLEQ`, drop a tie-break sort key, replace `IS` with `EQ`, reorder unsorted output only (must *not* be flagged when order isn't specified), change NUM rounding, introduce DIV-by-zero on filtered rows.
-- Recall = caught ÷ mutants. Target 100%.
+1. **Corpus.**
+   - Programs: the 8 examples, mutants of the examples, and generated programs from a type-checked template generator over the schema (scan/filter, 1–4 joins along `refs`, GROUP aggregates, SORT/TOPK/SLICE, string/date/arithmetic) with legacy bloat added. Keep a program only if the original succeeds and gives non-empty output on at least one dataset.
+   - Data: `gen_data.py` style, constraint-respecting, adversarial, with literals harvested from the programs, all validated by `--schema`. Plus datasets designed to make originals error.
+2. **Run.** One optimizer call per program, at most 4 in parallel so machine load can't cause a false "> 1 s". Then VM runs on all cores using the platform binary.
+3. **Compare** using the rubric in §6.
+   - Order: a data-flow pass over the original decides whether output order is specified (`eval-proto.py: output_ordered`).
+   - Ties: compare rows tied on the sort keys as a multiset, by running an *instrumented* original that also outputs the sort keys (still to build).
+4. **On a defect:** re-run once, save the repro (program, optimized program, dataset, diff) to `eval_out/`, then shrink it if time remains (stretch goal). Exit 1.
+5. **Budget [M]:** 8.3 ms per run means 320 checks take 1.7 s. A watchdog at 100 s stops new work and reports the verdict so far.
+
+### 4.1 Evaluation methods compared
+
+| Method | How | Proves | Catches | False-alarm risk | Cost within 2 min | Use |
+|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
+| **Run-and-compare (differential testing)** | run original and optimized on many datasets, compare outputs | that a defect exists (a counterexample); never that there is none | anything the data exercises: NULLs, ties, order, errors, scale | low, if the comparator rules are right | ~1.7 s per 320 checks **[M]** | **primary** |
+| **Normalize-and-compare (syntactic)** | canonicalize both programs (rename registers, DCE, sort commutative ops) and diff them | equivalence only when they normalize to identical text | nothing, if any real rewrite happened | none, but almost always "unknown" | ms | fast path: identical after normalizing ⇒ skip running the VM |
+| **Metamorphic testing (SQLancer TLP/NoREC, EMI)** | derive related programs or data, e.g. partition on `p` / `NOT p` / `p IS NULL`, add dead code, permute rows that are unsorted by the program | defects that need no reference output | NULL three-valued-logic bugs, order assumptions, stats-as-constraint bugs | low | moderate | data-side only: NULL partitions and row permutations in the generator |
+| **Property checks** | invariants without a reference: `ncols` same, row count same for pure projections, VM limits, optimizer time | gross failures | crashes, timeouts, limit blow-ups | none | trivial | always on |
+| **Mutation testing of the oracle** | seed bugs into fake optimizers; measure the kill rate | the *evaluator's* recall and precision | weak data, a lenient comparator | — | ~37 s for 19 fakes **[M]** | meta-eval gate on every evaluator change |
+| **Translation validation / math proof (Alive2, CompCert)** | encode both programs symbolically under the constraints and ask an SMT solver whether they are equivalent | **equivalence on all data** | everything, within the encoded semantics | none, if the encoding is right | days to build; may be slow per query; semantics are large (NUM rounding, Unicode UPPER, 3-valued logic) | out of scope for 2–3 h; first item in the "few days" plan |
+| **Bounded exhaustive (small-scope hypothesis)** | enumerate all tiny databases (e.g. ≤ 3 rows, small value domains) | equivalence on every database up to that size | most semantic bugs, which show up on tiny data | none | grows exponentially with columns and values; feasible for 1–2 tables | stretch goal: NULL/''/tie value domains on 0–3 rows |
+
+**Decision:** run-and-compare is the verdict. Normalize-and-compare is a fast path. Metamorphic ideas shape the data generator. Mutation testing gates the evaluator. Proofs are deferred.
 
 ---
 
-## 5. Eval rubric
+## 5. Safety rules: how a rewrite can produce wrong output (with doc citations)
 
-**Outputs (per program × dataset):**
-- **O1** `ncols` equal, and the optimized run succeeds whenever the original succeeds.
-- **O2** Rows equal as a list if order is specified (Q1), else as a multiset (sort both by canonical key).
-- **O3** Numbers (INT or NUM, parsed exactly via `Decimal`) satisfy `|a−b| ≤ 1e-6·max(|a|,|b|)` (`a==b` for 0). STR/DATE/BOOL are exact; NULL = NULL.
-- **O4** The optimized program doesn't exceed VM limits or the 1 s optimizer limit.
-- **O5** If the original errors, the pair is skipped (not a defect).
+| ID | Trap | Doc evidence | Probe **[M]** | Rule |
+|---|---|---|---|---|
+| S1 | **Adding an error.** Errors stop the program, and an optimized program that errors is a defect. Removing dead code can only remove errors, which is fine because "if the input errors, anything goes". | "Any error stops the program" `[doc: running.md#errors]`; dead `DIV x,0` still errors | F6, `b_dead_div0` caught (272) | Never add an op that may error. Deleting ops is always allowed. |
+| S2 | **Data-dependent errors.** Type errors and DIV by 0 fire only on rows actually used, never on empty or all-NULL columns. | "Types are checked row by row ... whether a program fails can depend on its data" `[doc: instructions/README.md#element-wise-instructions]`; "DIV of NULL by 0 is NULL" (same section) | `DIV` after a `d≠0` filter OK, before it errors | An op may run only on a **subset** of the rows it originally ran on. Never push DIV/IDIV/MOD/DATEADD/overflow-prone ops or comparisons of mixed types above a filter. |
+| S3 | **NULL keys: joins vs groups.** HJOIN never matches NULL keys (unless NULLEQ); GROUP puts NULLs together. | "A NULL key matches no key, unless ... NULLEQ" `[doc: instructions/joins.md]`; "NULL equals NULL" `[doc: instructions/grouping.md#group]` | `q0006.naive` caught (24), `b_nulleq` caught (56) | Any join↔group rewrite adds or keeps an explicit `NOT ISNULL(key)` filter. |
+| S4 | **Order and ties.** SORT is stable, HJOIN output is ordered by (i, j), and groups are numbered by first appearance. | "Ties keep their input order" `[doc: instructions/sorting.md#sort-keys]`; "ordered by i then j" `[doc: joins.md]`; "groups are numbered from 0 in order of first appearance" `[doc: grouping.md#group-sets]` | `b_reverse` (172), `b_no_tiebreak` (19) | Preserve exact output order. Never swap join sides or reorder the input to GROUP/GFIRST/GMIN/GMAX. |
+| S5 | **First-of-equals and NUM scale.** GMIN/GMAX return the first of equal values; GSUM uses the largest scale in the group; `1.0` and `1.00` print differently. | "the first of equals: of 1, 1.0 and 1.00, whichever comes first" `[doc: grouping.md#gmin-gmax]`; "1.0 and 1.00 are equal but print differently" `[doc: values.md#num-range]`; GSUM scale `[doc: grouping.md#gsum]` | F28, F5 | Keep the aggregate input order. Avoid identities that rescale (`x*1.000`, `x+0.0`): equal in value, but they change the printed text. |
+| S6 | **Unicode changes length.** UPPER can change the character count, so UPPER and LEFT/SUBSTR don't commute. | "UPPER('straße') is 'STRASSE'" `[doc: strings.md#upper]` | `ß` in generator | Never reorder string ops relative to each other; only move them across filters and gathers. |
+| S7 | **VM limits.** Join output counts toward limits; HJOIN/GROUP use up the work budget faster than their cycles suggest. | limits table `[doc: running.md#program-rules]` | 2M-row join takes 425 ms | No rewrite may grow any intermediate result beyond the original's. |
+| S8 | **Constraints vs stats.** Constraints hold for every dataset; stats describe one. `check` doesn't imply `not_null`. Tables without a `key` may have duplicates. | "The constraints hold for every table set with this schema; the stats describe only this one" `[doc: running.md#schema-files]`; constraint table `[doc: running.md#constraints]` | `q0004.naive` (no key on customers) | Correctness may use only key, not_null, refs, check and sorted. Stats are used only for cost. |
+| S9 | **`sorted` covers stored order only.** It survives IDX, ascending GATHER and the left side of a join, but not the right side of HJOIN, GROUP output, or a SORT on other keys. | "the rows are stored in ascending order ... as ASC NF sorts them" `[doc: running.md#constraints]` | q0001/q0005 identity SORT | Drop a SORT only if its keys are a prefix of `sorted` ASC NF *and* the index is proven ascending from LOAD. |
+| S10 | **Literal-only ops and OUT rules.** An element-wise op needs at least one register operand; OUT must be the last instruction and the only OUT. | `[doc: instructions/README.md#element-wise-instructions]`, `[doc: running.md#program-rules]` | F23, F25 | Constant folding must not produce a literal-only op; the emitter always ends with exactly one OUT. |
 
-**Programs (performance):**
-- **P1** Cycles of optimized vs original per dataset: speedup ratio, plus a geomean over the corpus.
-- **P2** Instruction count before and after.
-- **P3** Peak register size and VM-limit headroom (never worse than the original).
-- **P4** Optimizer wall time (max must be < 1 s).
-- **P5** Regression flag: optimized cycles > original cycles on any dataset (allowed but reported; target zero).
+---
 
-**Evaluator:**
-- **E1** Defects caught on mutant optimizers (recall, target 100%).
-- **E2** False alarms on the identity and correct optimizers (target 0).
-- **E3** Wall time (< 120 s; target < 60 s).
-- **E4** Determinism: the same seed gives the same verdict.
+## 6. Eval rubric
+
+```json
+{
+  "outputs": {
+    "O1_ncols": "equal; optimized must succeed whenever original succeeds",
+    "O2_rows": "in order if original specifies order (SORT/TOPK reaches OUT), else multiset",
+    "O3_numbers": "JSON int or string matching ^-?\\d+(\\.\\d+)?$ -> Decimal, |a-b| <= 1e-6*max(|a|,|b|)",
+    "O4_other_values": "NULL==NULL only; STR/DATE exact (no Unicode normalization); BOOL never equals INT",
+    "O5_skip": "original errors -> pair skipped, not a defect",
+    "O6_limits": "optimizer > 1 s, crash, empty output, or optimized VM error -> defect"
+  },
+  "programs": {
+    "P1_cycles": "speedup per (program, dataset) + geomean",
+    "P2_instructions": "count before/after",
+    "P3_peak_register": "never larger than original",
+    "P4_optimizer_time_max_s": "< 1.0",
+    "P5_regressions": "optimized cycles > original on any dataset (reported, target 0)"
+  },
+  "evaluator": {
+    "E1_recall": "caught / buggy fakes (seeded + held-out), target 100%",
+    "E2_false_alarms": "flagged honest fakes, target 0 over 10 seeds",
+    "E3_wall_time_s": "< 120 (target < 60 at -j4)",
+    "E4_determinism": "same seed -> identical report"
+  }
+}
+```
 
 ---
 
-## 6. Stopping criteria for the build ↔ evaluate loop
+## 7. Unclear rules and the assumptions we make
 
-- **Success, stop:** 0 defects on the full eval with 3 different seeds; E1 = 100% and E2 = 0; geomean speedup ≥ 2× on examples [target est]; all optimizer calls < 1 s; evaluator < 2 min.
-- **No more improvement, stop that rewrite:** two consecutive iterations where geomean cycles improve by < 3%, or a rewrite's gain doesn't justify its risk. Move to the next rank, or stop.
-- **Budget used up, stop:** user time hits 2h45m, or the AI token budget reaches its cap (we track per session). Ship the last green commit.
-- **Ask the user when:** a rewrite depends on an open-question interpretation (Q1/Q2); a defect can't be explained within 2 attempts; a fix requires dropping a rewrite that gives > 20% of the gain; or any destructive git action (history rewrite for binaries, §10).
-- **Hard rule:** any defect is a red build. Revert or disable the rewrite before doing anything else.
+How we handle each: pick the **default that can't produce a false alarm or a wrong optimization**, encode it as a check, log it, and escalate only if the choice changes more than 20% of results.
+
+| # | Unclear point | Options | Default | Why | Encoded in |
+|---|---|---|---|---|---|
+| Q1 | When does a program "specify" order? Do tied rows count? | (a) whenever SORT/TOPK reaches OUT, strict; (b) the same, but tied rows compared as a multiset; (c) never | **(b)**; today (a) | strict order flags legal reorders of ties | `output_ordered`; tie handling still to build |
+| Q2 | `"2.0"` vs `"2.00"`; INT `2` vs NUM `"2.0"` | text equal / numeric equal | numeric equal | "numbers within 1e-6" | `tricky_checks.py` CMP |
+| Q3 | STR `'1.0'` vs `'1.00'` print like numbers | infer column types / treat as numbers | treat as numbers (documented gap) | the output JSON has no types | known gap; fix by inferring types |
+| Q4 | Tolerance on INTs (`10000000` vs `10000001` passes) | apply to INTs / exact for INTs | apply (spec says "numbers") | follow the spec literally | CMP check |
+| Q5 | Near zero (`0` vs `1e-7`) | relative only / add an absolute epsilon | relative only | spec says relative; correct optimizers don't change arithmetic | CMP check |
+| Q6 | Ties at a TOPK/SLICE/GFIRST cut-off | fail / warn | warn once tie handling is built | a correct optimizer may choose another tied row | still to build |
+| Q7 | Optimizer may use `bin/minivm`? | yes / no | no | the grader may not ship it next to `./optimize` | optimizer design |
+| Q8 | Can stats be used? | correctness / cost only | cost only | `[doc: running.md#schema-files]` | S8 |
+| Q9 | Optimized program hits a VM limit where the original didn't | defect / skip | defect | the program "gives a different result" (none) | O6 |
+| Q10 | How is "faster" measured? | cycles / wall time | cycles | that's what the VM reports | P1 |
 
 ---
 
-## 7. Failure modes
+## 8. Working model: how the agents talk
 
-| Failure | Detection | Fix / prevention |
-|---|---|---|
-| Rewrite changes NULL semantics (HJOIN vs GROUP NULL keys) | targeted NULL-heavy data; mutant tests | explicit NULL filters in idiom rewrites; per-rewrite unit tests |
-| Rewrite introduces an error (DIV by 0, type error, out-of-range GATHER, DATEADD overflow) on rows the original skipped | O1 failures on adversarial data | the "subset of evaluated rows" rule (§3 #4) |
-| Lost tie order (unstable reorder) | O2 on tie-heavy data | always preserve order; only stable sorts and IDX/ascending composition |
-| Relying on stats instead of constraints | data generator deliberately violates stats (different ranges, distincts) | lint: the correctness path never reads `stats` |
-| Order-insensitive comparison hides a real defect, or order-sensitive gives false alarms | meta-eval with "reorder-only" mutant | Q1 detector unit-tested on all 8 examples |
-| NUM scale or rounding differences | O3 tolerance; DIV/MUL results compared via Decimal | don't reassociate arithmetic |
-| Optimized program blows VM limits (join expansion) | O4; P3 report | cost check: never increase peak register size or HJOIN/GROUP input |
-| Optimizer > 1 s on big programs (20k instructions) | timing in runner; a stress program with 20k lines | linear passes, iteration cap, 0.8 s deadline → fall back |
-| Evaluator > 2 min | wall-time report | watchdog at 100 s; parallel pool; cap on corpus size |
-| Generator makes invalid data | `bin/minivm --schema` rejects it | regenerate; count rejections in the report |
-| Overfitting to 8 examples | held-out generated programs scored separately | rewrites must be general passes; idioms gated |
+**Roles.**
+- **Orchestrator** (main session): owns the plan and writes task cards; never writes optimizer code.
+- **Builder** subagent: implements one rewrite per task.
+- **Red-team** subagent: sees only the spec, docs and rubric. It writes fake optimizers and adversarial data, and never sees the optimizer code.
+- **Checker:** the `./evaluate` script, not an LLM.
+- **Reviewer** subagent: fresh context, for rank ≥ 5 rewrites. Its job: "name the dataset that breaks this".
+- **Human:** approves at checkpoints and decides open questions.
 
----
+```
+human ──approve/decide──▶ ORCHESTRATOR ──task.json──▶ BUILDER ──commit + build.json──▶ CHECKER(./evaluate)
+  ▲                          │  ▲                                                        │
+  └────escalation.json───────┘  └──────verdict.json ◀── REVIEWER ◀── report.json ◀──────┘
+                                 RED-TEAM (spec only) ──fakes + data──▶ CHECKER (meta-eval)
+```
 
-## 8. Test suite and runners
+**Rules.**
+- Agents communicate only through **files**, never prose summaries.
+- Every message is a JSON document with a schema version.
+- Prose goes only in a `notes` field.
+- Files live under `runs/<iteration>/`.
+
+```json
+// task.json  (orchestrator -> builder)
+{"v":1,"task_id":"T07","goal":"sort reduction: SORT+IDX+SLICE -> IDX+TOPK",
+ "rewrite_rank":4,"safety_rules":["S4","S5"],"files_allowed":["optimizer/passes/sort.py","tests/cases/medium/*"],
+ "done_when":{"defects":0,"geomean_min":1.0,"opt_time_max_s":0.8},"budget":{"wall_min":15,"tokens_k":200}}
+```
+```json
+// build.json  (builder -> checker)
+{"v":1,"task_id":"T07","commit":"abc1234","pass":"sort_reduction","tests_added":["medium/topk_after_filter.mini"],
+ "claims":[{"rule":"S4","why":"TOPK = first n of the same stable SORT [doc: sorting.md#topk]"}],"notes":""}
+```
+```json
+// report.json  (checker -> everyone; also appended to notes/runs.jsonl)
+{"v":1,"commit":"abc1234","seed":0,"checked":320,"skipped":0,"defects":0,"wall_s":1.7,
+ "geomean_speedup":2.39,"per_program":[{"name":"q0007","speedup":4.08,"status":"OK"}],
+ "meta":{"buggy_caught":11,"buggy_total":11,"false_alarms":0},
+ "defect_repros":[]}
+```
+```json
+// verdict.json  (reviewer -> orchestrator)
+{"v":1,"commit":"abc1234","decision":"keep|revert|needs_test",
+ "counterexample":{"program":"...","dataset":"...","expected":"...","got":"..."},"rules_checked":["S4"]}
+```
+```json
+// escalation.json  (orchestrator -> human)
+{"v":1,"question_id":"Q1","options":["strict","tie-multiset"],"recommended":"tie-multiset",
+ "impact":"3/320 checks change verdict","blocking":true}
+```
 
-**Easy** (one pass each): DCE-only, CSE-only, SORT on a sorted table, always-true predicate, HJOIN↔NLJOIN choice, empty tables, all-NULL columns.
-**Medium:** examples q7, q8, q2; filter pushdown through one join; SORT+SLICE→TOPK; string op after filter.
-**Complex:** q1, q4, q5 (multi-join fan-outs); q3, q6 (self-join idioms); generated 3–4-join plans; 20k-instruction stress program for timing.
+**Git discipline.**
+- One rewrite per commit.
+- A red build gets `git revert`, never a reset or amend.
+- Tag green checkpoints `cp1`…`cp5`.
+- Each defect becomes a regression in `tests/regressions/`, replayed first on every run.
 
-Layout:
-```
-tests/cases/{easy,medium,complex}/NAME.mini     # + optional NAME.expect_opt (golden)
-tests/data/                                     # generated datasets + schemas
-tools/gen_data.py  tools/gen_prog.py  tools/compare.py  tools/profile.py
-run_tests.sh   # unit + suite: each case × each dataset, original vs optimized
-run_eval.sh    # ./evaluate ./optimize + meta-eval (identity + mutants)
+**Independence.** `evaluate/` never imports `optimizer/`; a grep check enforces this. Only the CLI and `bin/minivm` cross the boundary.
+
+---
+
+## 9. Stopping criteria
+
+```json
+{
+  "success": ["0 defects on 3 seeds", "meta-eval: 100% buggy caught (seeded + held-out), 0 false alarms",
+              "geomean >= 2x on examples, > 1x on held-out programs", "max optimizer time < 1 s", "evaluate < 60 s"],
+  "no_more_improvement": "2 consecutive iterations with < 3% geomean gain -> move to next rank or stop",
+  "budget": "user time 2h45m or token cap reached -> ship last green tag",
+  "ask_human": ["a rule depends on Q1-Q10", "a defect not explained in 2 attempts",
+                "a fix would drop > 20% of the gain", "any destructive git action"],
+  "hard_rule": "any defect = red build; revert or disable the rewrite before anything else"
+}
 ```
-Every run prints a report: `pass/fail per case | cycles orig→opt (×speedup) | instructions | max opt time | defects | geomean`. It also appends a JSON line to `notes/runs.jsonl` for trend tracking.

-## 9. Progression log
+## 10. Failure modes
 
-`notes/PROGRESSION_LOG.md` gets one entry per iteration: *date · approach tried · result (defects, geomean, timing) · what we learned · keep/revert*. The first entries are the facts in §0 and the probe results.
+| Failure | Detection | Prevention / fix |
+|---|---|---|
+| Rewrite breaks NULL semantics | NULL-heavy data; `b_nulleq`, `q0006.naive` | S3 |
+| Rewrite introduces an error | O6; `b_dead_div0` | S1, S2 |
+| Lost order or ties | order-aware comparator; `b_reverse`, `b_no_tiebreak` | S4 |
+| Evaluator too lenient (weak data) | meta-eval misses; `BASELINE_MISSED` in `check.py` | harvest literals; more rows than every TOPK limit; ties; empty tables |
+| Evaluator too strict (false alarm) | honest fakes (`h_*`) must pass | Q1–Q5 defaults; tie handling |
+| Comparator type confusion | `tricky_checks.py` CMP | strict numeric format; BOOL ≠ INT (both bugs found and fixed **[M]**) |
+| Using stats for correctness | generator deliberately violates stats | S8; lint check |
+| VM limit blow-up | P3, O6 | S7 cost check |
+| Optimizer > 1 s on 20k-line programs | stress test | linear passes; deadline then fall back |
+| Evaluator > 2 min | wall time in report | platform binary, watchdog, limits on corpus size |
+| Overfitting to the 8 examples | held-out generated programs | general passes; no matching on names |
+| Shared bug in optimizer and evaluator | independence grep check | separate parsers |

+# Tricky rules: what bin/minivm does and how the evaluator handles it
+
+Every item was checked with `bin/minivm`. They are encoded as 56 repeatable checks in
+`prototype_code/evaluator_agent/tricky_checks.py` (56/56 pass).
+
+## Parsing
+- **Case:** opcodes and keywords ignore case (`sort` = `SORT` = `SoRt`, `asc nf` = `ASC NF`). Register names are case-sensitive (`a` ≠ `A`, `s` ≠ `S`). The order detector upper-cases opcodes only.
+- **Literals:** `;` and `,` inside a string literal are data, not a comment or a separator. `''` is an escaped quote.
+- **Layout:** CRLF line endings, blank lines and comments after `OUT` are all fine. `OUT` with no operands is an error.
+
+## Output JSON has no types
+- **Text looks alike:** STR `"2.00"`, `"2024-01-31"` and `"1e5"` print exactly like NUM, DATE and plain text. Only BOOL is distinct, because it prints unquoted `true`/`false`.
+- **Number formats:** minivm prints numbers only as `-?\d+(\.\d+)?` (JSON integers, or strings beyond ±2^63). The comparator therefore treats only that exact text as a number. **Bug fixed:** it used to parse `'1.0 '` and `'1e5'` as numbers, which hid TRIM-style bugs.
+- **BOOL vs INT:** Python has `True == 1`. **Bug fixed:** `true` vs `1` and `false` vs `0` used to compare as equal.
+- **Negative zero:** `NEG 0.0` prints `"-0.0"` but `MUL 0.0, -1` prints `"0"`. The two are equal by value, so this is not a defect.
+- **Remaining ambiguity:** STR `'1.0'` vs STR `'1.00'` compares as equal numbers. This can't be fixed without column types (documented).
+
+## Tolerance (one-pager: numbers within 1e-6 relative)
+- **Large INTs:** this tolerance applies to INTs too. `10000000` vs `10000001` passes. That follows the spec, but it means off-by-one errors on very large counts go undetected.
+- **Zero:** the tolerance is purely relative, so `0` vs `0.0000001` is a defect. A correct optimizer doesn't change arithmetic, so this is fine.
+
+## Row order
+- **When order counts:** rows are compared **in order** whenever the original's output order comes from a SORT or TOPK, and as a multiset otherwise.
+- **Order detector:** decided by a data-flow pass over the original program. It covers filters, the right side of a join, GROUP numbering after a sort, register overwrites, sorts whose result is never output, and comments.
+- **Effect:** before this rule, the reversed-rows bug was missed and the sort-flip and dropped-tie-break bugs were barely caught (4 and 2 defects). Now all three are caught (172, 134 and 19 defects), with 0 false alarms on the 8 honest fakes.
+- **Known limit:** rows that tie on the sort keys are also compared in order. SORT is stable, so ties keep their input order. An optimizer that legally reorders ties would be flagged. None of the honest fakes do this. It's open question Q1 in the plan.
+
+## Empty and degenerate data
+- **Empty tables:** GROUP gives **0 rows**, but GALL gives **1 row `[0, NULL]`**. An optimizer that swaps them is wrong on empty data only.
+- **Errors on empty columns:** `DIV` by 0 and type errors never fire on empty or all-NULL columns, so whether a program errors depends on its data.
+- **Empty GALL:** GFIRST gives NULL, and GATHER with a NULL index is an error.
+
+## Speed
+- **Platform binary:** calling `bin/minivm-macos-aarch64` directly takes 8.3 ms per run, against 38.6 ms through the `bin/minivm` wrapper. The wrapper spawns a shell and runs `xattr` every time. Output is identical on all examples.
+- **Evaluator:** it now resolves the platform binary itself, the same way the wrapper does, and clears the quarantine flag once. A full evaluation dropped from 8.4 s to **1.7 s**. `MINIVM=...` overrides the choice.
+- **Determinism:** the same program and data give byte-identical output across runs.

+# prototype_code: quick experiments before the real build
+
+Throwaway-quality prototypes. They test the ideas in `../BUILD_PLAN.md` against real
+`bin/minivm` runs before we build `./optimize` and `./evaluate`. Python 3 standard library
+only. All paths are relative to each script, so they run from any directory.
+
+```sh
+prototype_code/run_all.sh        # regenerate data + run every check (~1 min)
+```
+
+Generated data (`optimizer_agent/data/`) is git-ignored. It is rebuilt byte-for-byte from
+fixed seeds by `run_all.sh`, or by the two generator scripts below.
+
+## Files
+
+### `optimizer_agent/`: hand-optimized programs and the data that tests them
+| File | What it does | Run |
+|---|---|---|
+| `qNNNN.opt.mini` | Hand-optimized version of each of the 8 example programs. A header comment says which constraint or semantic rule each rewrite relies on. | input to `cmp.py`, `check.py`, `hand_opt.sh` |
+| `qNNNN.naive.mini` | **Known-bad** fast version of each example: the mistake a naive optimizer would plausibly make, e.g. dropping NVL on a nullable column, de-duplicating a table with no key, or running TOPK before the filter. Used as baselines that the tests must catch. | input to `check.py`, `naive_opt.sh` |
+| `cmp.py` | Runs two programs on the same data. Prints cycles, speedup and MATCH / MISMATCH / SKIP / OPT_ERROR; exits 1 on a defect. | `cmp.py ORIG OPT [DATA...]` or `cmp.py --all [DATA...]` |
+| `check.py` | Runs every `qNNNN.opt.mini` against every dataset in parallel and reports matches and speedup by data size. On a failure it saves the program as `qNNNN.naive.mini` and writes `failures/qNNNN.txt`. It re-checks every naive baseline and fails if one stops being caught. | `check.py [qNNNN ...]` |
+| `gen_data.py` | Schema-driven generator for **small / medium / large** datasets (20 / 10 / 3 by default). It respects key, not_null, refs, check and sorted. It mixes in NULLs, duplicates and ties, empty and 1-row tables, odd strings (`ß`, `ﬁ`, emoji, accent marks, long strings), case and space variants, `1.0` vs `1.00`, and boundary dates. Literals taken from the programs (`'CARD'`, `750.00`, `#2023-01-01#`, ...) are planted with ±1 neighbours. Every file is validated with `bin/minivm --schema`. | `gen_data.py [--small N --medium N --large N --seed S]` |
+| `make_adv_data.py` | Six small variants of `examples/data.json`: case and space variants, NULLs, duplicate customer ids. | `make_adv_data.py [N] [OUTDIR]` |
+| `hand_opt.sh` / `naive_opt.sh` | Optimizers with the same command line as `./optimize`. They print the stored `.opt.mini` / `.naive.mini`, or the input if there is none. | `hand_opt.sh prog.mini schema.json` |
+
+### `evaluator_agent/`: prototype evaluator, and tests of the evaluator itself
+| File | What it does | Run |
+|---|---|---|
+| `eval-proto.py` | **Prototype evaluator.** It calls `OPTIMIZER prog.mini schema.json` for each example, then runs the original and the optimized program on all 40 datasets. A pair is SKIPPED if the original errors. It is a DEFECT if the optimizer fails or takes more than 1 s, if the optimized program errors, if ncols differ, or if the rows differ. Rows are compared in order when the original specifies order (SORT/TOPK reaching OUT), otherwise as a multiset. Numbers match within 1e-6 relative. Prints checked / skipped / defects / time; exits 1 on any defect. | `eval-proto.py OPTIMIZER [DATA...] [--quiet]` |
+| `fake_opt.py` | 19 fake optimizers as generic program transforms. **8 honest** (identity, dead-code removal, register renaming, reformatting, HJOIN↔NLJOIN, ±1e-10 drift, INT→NUM scale) must not be flagged. **11 buggy** (GT↔GE, no NULLIFE, no UPPER, RTRIM→TRIM, NULLEQ, LEFT n-1, sort flip, dropped tie-break, reversed rows, 1e-5 drift, dead DIV by 0) must be caught. | `fake_opt.py MODE prog.mini schema.json` |
+| `meta_eval.py` | Tests the tester: runs `eval-proto.py` on every fake. Reports CAUGHT / MISSED / OK / FALSE ALARM, with evidence for each miss: whether the fake changed the program, and on how many datasets the output was identical, order-only different, or different. Writes `meta_report.md`. | `meta_eval.py [MODE ...]` |
+| `meta_report.md` | Output of the last `meta_eval.py` run. | — |
+| `tricky_checks.py` | 56 checks covering comparator rules, order detection and VM facts. Details in `../notes/tricky_rules.md`. | `tricky_checks.py` |
+
+## Results (measured with `bin/minivm`)
+- **Hand-optimized speedups:** 3.14× geomean cycle reduction on `examples/data.json`. Larger datasets speed up much more, because the originals' quadratic self-joins grow with the data.
+
+  | Program | q0001 | q0002 | q0003 | q0004 | q0005 | q0006 | q0007 | q0008 |
+  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
+  | Speedup on example data | 8.1× | 1.8× | 3.0× | 8.5× | 1.7× | 3.0× | 4.1× | 1.2× |
+  | Speedup on large data | 4.5× | 1.8× | 37× | 47× | 2.2× | 20× | 8.3× | 1.3× |
+
+- **Correctness:** all 8 hand-optimized programs match the originals on all 40 datasets, and all 8 naive baselines are caught.
+- **Evaluator:** catches 11 of 11 buggy fakes with 0 false alarms on 8 honest ones. A full run takes 1.7 s.
+
+## What we learned
+1. **The biggest wins are generic, not clever.** Filter before joining and before building strings. Gather only the columns that reach `OUT`. Delete dead code. Use TOPK instead of SORT followed by a slice. These gave most of q0001, q0004 and q0007 (4–8.5×). Replacing a self-join with GROUP is the only big structural rewrite (q0003, q0006), and it is also the riskiest.
+2. **Rewrites must rely on constraints, never on stats.**
+   - Exactly one match is safe for `not_null` + `refs` to a key.
+   - A SORT is a no-op on a `sorted` table.
+   - `customers` has no key, so a join to it can multiply rows. The naive q0004, which assumes unique `c_id`, is wrong on data where `c_id` repeats.
+3. **NULL semantics are the main trap.** HJOIN never matches NULL keys, but GROUP puts NULLs together. NULLIFE turns `''` into NULL. IS is never NULL. Every self-join→GROUP rewrite needs an explicit NULL-key filter.
+4. **Safety rule for rewrites:** an operation may only run on a *subset* of the rows it originally ran on. Removing work can only remove errors, which is allowed because "if the input program errors, anything goes".
+5. **Weak data hides bugs.**
+   - Two broken q0007 variants (TOPK 4 instead of 5, flipped tie-break) passed the example data. It has only 3 July-2023 orders and no tied amounts.
+   - The q0004 naive baseline went uncaught until the generator planted the programs' own literals: no category was ever `'KITCHEN'`, so every result was empty.
+   - Fix: harvest literals and their ±1 neighbours, and generate more rows than every TOPK limit, plus ties.
+6. **Multiset comparison alone is too lenient.** It missed a reversed-rows bug and barely caught sort-key bugs (4 and 2 defects). The order rule raised these to 172, 134 and 19 defects with no new false alarms.
+7. **The output JSON has no types.**
+   - STR `"2.00"` prints like a NUM.
+   - Only text minivm actually prints as a number (`-?\d+(\.\d+)?`) may be compared numerically; otherwise a TRIM bug on `'1.0 '` would hide.
+   - JSON `true` must never equal `1`.
+   - Remaining ambiguity: STR `'1.0'` vs `'1.00'`.
+8. **Test the tester, and test it with evidence.** An evaluator that reports 0 defects proves nothing until it has caught seeded bugs and passed honest-but-different optimizers. Honest examples include renaming registers, INT→NUM scale changes, and 1e-10 drift.
+9. **Calling the platform binary directly is 4.6× faster than the `bin/minivm` wrapper** (8.3 ms vs 38.6 ms per run, identical output). This leaves the 2-minute evaluator budget roomy: about 14k VM runs fit in 2 minutes even sequentially, and many more in parallel on 16 cores.
+
+## Known gaps (next steps for the real build)
+- **Programs:** only the 8 example programs are tested. There is no random program generator yet, and only the example schema is used.
+- **Error paths:** no generated dataset makes an original program error, so the "anything goes" path is untested.
+- **Ties:** rows that tie on the sort keys are still compared in order, so an optimizer that legally reorders ties would be flagged (open question Q1).
+- **Tolerance:** the 1e-6 relative tolerance also applies to INTs, so `10000000` vs `10000001` passes.
diff --git a/prototype_code/evaluator_agent/eval-proto.py b/prototype_code/evaluator_agent/eval-proto.py
new file mode 100755
index 0000000..c5fa0d4

+++ b/prototype_code/evaluator_agent/eval-proto.py

+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Prototype evaluator.
+
+  eval-proto.py OPTIMIZER [--data DIR_OR_FILE ...] [--quiet]
+
+For each examples/programs/*.mini, runs `OPTIMIZER program.mini examples/schema.json`
+once, then runs the original and the optimized program with bin/minivm on every
+dataset (examples/data.json plus, by default, the generated table sets under
+prototype_code/optimizer_agent/data/), and compares the results.
+
+A (program, dataset) pair is:
+  SKIPPED  the original errors on that dataset (anything goes)
+  CHECKED  the original succeeds; then it is a DEFECT if
+             - the optimizer fails, prints nothing, or takes > 1 s, or
+             - the optimized program errors, or
+             - ncols differ, or
+             - the rows differ, where numbers (INT or NUM, compared exactly via
+               Decimal) match within 1e-6 relative, NULL == NULL, and every other
+               value must be equal. Rows are compared IN ORDER when the original
+               program specifies its output order, else as a multiset.
+
+Row-order rule: the original specifies order when a SORT/TOPK result reaches OUT.
+A forward data-flow pass marks registers ORDERED (rows in program-specified order):
+SORT/TOPK make one; GATHER keeps it through an ordered index, or through a
+monotone index (IDX, IOTA, a join's left positions, GFIRST) applied to an ordered
+column; joins keep the order of an ordered left key; GROUP/GALL over ordered keys
+number groups in that order, so their aggregates are ordered; element-wise ops,
+SLICE, FILL and IOTA keep their operand's order; LOAD is unordered (stored order
+is the data's, not the program's). Output order matters if any OUT operand is ORDERED.
+Known limit: ties on the sort keys are compared in order too (SORT is stable).
+
+Prints checked, skipped, defects and wall time; exits 1 if any defect, else 0.
+Independent of the optimizer code: only the CLI and bin/minivm cross the boundary.
+"""
+import glob, json, os, platform, re, subprocess, sys, tempfile, time
+from collections import Counter
+from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
+from decimal import Decimal
+
+HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
+REPO = os.path.normpath(os.path.join(HERE, "..", ".."))
+ROOT = os.path.join(REPO, "minivm-takehome")
+VM_WRAPPER = os.path.join(ROOT, "bin", "minivm")
+
+
+def native_vm():
+    """The platform binary bin/minivm would exec (same output, ~4.6x faster: no shell + xattr per run)."""
+    osn = {"darwin": "macos"}.get(platform.system().lower(), platform.system().lower())
+    arch = {"arm64": "aarch64", "amd64": "x86_64"}.get(platform.machine().lower(), platform.machine().lower())
+    ext = ".exe" if osn.startswith(("windows", "mingw", "msys", "cygwin")) else ""
+    b = os.path.join(ROOT, "bin", f"minivm-{'windows' if ext else osn}-{arch}{ext}")
+    if not os.access(b, os.X_OK):
+        return VM_WRAPPER
+    if osn == "macos":   # downloaded zips are quarantined; the wrapper clears this on every run
+        subprocess.run(["xattr", "-d", "com.apple.quarantine", b], capture_output=True)
+    return b
+
+
+VM = os.environ.get("MINIVM") or native_vm()
+PROGS = os.path.join(ROOT, "examples", "programs")
+SCHEMA = os.path.join(ROOT, "examples", "schema.json")
+DEFAULT_DATA = [os.path.join(ROOT, "examples", "data.json"),
+                os.path.join(REPO, "prototype_code", "optimizer_agent", "data")]
+OPT_LIMIT_S = 1.0
+REL_TOL = Decimal("1e-6")
+KEYWORDS = {"NULL", "TRUE", "FALSE", "ASC", "DESC", "NF", "NL", "NULLEQ"}
+
+
+# ---------------- does the program specify its output order? ----------------
+def parse_program(path):
+    prog = []
+    for line in open(path):
+        code, q = "", False
+        for ch in line:                      # strip ';' comments outside string literals
+            if ch == "'":
+                q = not q
+            if ch == ";" and not q:
+                break
+            code += ch
+        code = code.strip()
+        if not code:
+            continue
+        parts = code.split(None, 1)
+        ops, cur, q = [], "", False
+        for ch in parts[1] if len(parts) > 1 else "":
+            if ch == "'":
+                q = not q
+            if ch == "," and not q:
+                ops.append(cur.strip()); cur = ""
+            else:
+                cur += ch
+        if cur.strip():
+            ops.append(cur.strip())
+        prog.append((parts[0].upper(), ops))
+    return prog
+
+
+def reg(tok):
+    t = tok.split()[0] if tok.split() else ""
+    ok = t[:1].isalpha() or t[:1] == "_"
+    return t if ok and all(c.isalnum() or c == "_" for c in t) and t.upper() not in KEYWORDS else None
+
+
+def output_ordered(path):
+    ordered, mono = set(), set()
+    o = lambda t: reg(t) in ordered
+    for op, a in parse_program(path):
+        if op == "OUT":
+            return any(o(t) for t in a)
+        if op in ("HJOIN", "NLJOIN"):
+            li, ri, lk = a[0], a[1], a[2]
+            for d in (li, ri):
+                ordered.discard(d); mono.discard(d)
+            if o(lk):
+                ordered.update({li, ri})
+            if li != ri:
+                mono.add(li)
+            continue
+        d, src = a[0], a[1:]
+        if op in ("SORT", "TOPK"):
+            is_o, is_m = True, False
+        elif op == "GATHER":
+            s, i = reg(src[0]), reg(src[1])
+            is_o = i in ordered or (s in ordered and i in mono)
+            is_m = s in mono and i in mono
+        elif op == "LOAD":
+            is_o, is_m = False, False
+        elif op in ("IDX", "GFIRST"):
+            is_o, is_m = (op == "GFIRST" and o(src[0])), True
+        elif op == "IOTA":
+            is_o, is_m = o(src[0]), True
+        elif op == "SLICE":
+            is_o, is_m = o(src[0]), reg(src[0]) in mono
+        else:                                # element-wise, FILL, GROUP/GALL, aggregates, GID
+            is_o, is_m = any(o(t) for t in src), False
+        ordered.discard(d); mono.discard(d)
+        if is_o:
+            ordered.add(d)
+        if is_m:
+            mono.add(d)
+    return False
+
+
+# ---------------- comparison ----------------
+NUM_TEXT = re.compile(r"-?\d+(\.\d+)?")   # the only way minivm prints an INT or NUM
+
+
+def num(v):
+    if isinstance(v, bool) or v is None:
+        return None
+    if isinstance(v, int):
+        return Decimal(v)
+    if isinstance(v, str) and NUM_TEXT.fullmatch(v):
+        return Decimal(v)
+    return None
+
+
+def close(a, b):
+    if isinstance(a, bool) or isinstance(b, bool):   # JSON true/false never equal 1/0
+        return type(a) is type(b) and a == b
+    if a == b:
+        return True
+    na, nb = num(a), num(b)
+    if na is None or nb is None:
+        return False
+    return na == nb or abs(na - nb) <= REL_TOL * max(abs(na), abs(nb))
+
+
+def row_close(x, y):
+    return len(x) == len(y) and all(close(p, q) for p, q in zip(x, y))
+
+
+def canon(v):
+    n = num(v)
+    if n is not None:
+        return (1, str(n.normalize()))  # "2.0" and "2.00" -> same key
+    return (0, json.dumps(v, ensure_ascii=False))
+
+
+def same_multiset(ra, rb):
+    """Rows equal as a multiset, numbers within REL_TOL."""
+    if len(ra) != len(rb):
+        return False
+    # fast path: exact after numeric normalization
+    if Counter(tuple(map(canon, r)) for r in ra) == Counter(tuple(map(canon, r)) for r in rb):
+        return True
+    # tolerant path: sort, compare pairwise; then greedy matching as a fallback
+    key = lambda r: [canon(v) for v in r]
+    sa, sb = sorted(ra, key=key), sorted(rb, key=key)
+    if all(row_close(x, y) for x, y in zip(sa, sb)):
+        return True
+    if len(ra) > 3000:
+        return False
+    unused = list(rb)
+    for x in ra:
+        for i, y in enumerate(unused):
+            if row_close(x, y):
+                del unused[i]
+                break
+        else:
+            return False
+    return True
+
+
+# ---------------- running ----------------
+def vm(prog, data):
+    r = subprocess.run([VM, prog, data], capture_output=True, text=True)
+    if r.returncode != 0:
+        return None, (r.stderr.strip() or f"exit {r.returncode}")
+    return json.loads(r.stdout), None
+
+
+def optimize(optimizer, prog, outdir):
+    t = time.monotonic()
+    try:
+        r = subprocess.run([optimizer, prog, SCHEMA], capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10 * OPT_LIMIT_S)
+    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
+        return None, f"optimizer timed out (> {10 * OPT_LIMIT_S:.0f} s)", None
+    dt = time.monotonic() - t
+    if r.returncode != 0 or not r.stdout.strip():
+        return None, f"optimizer failed (exit {r.returncode}): {r.stderr.strip()[:200]}", dt
+    out = os.path.join(outdir, os.path.basename(prog))
+    with open(out, "w") as f:
+        f.write(r.stdout)
+    if dt > OPT_LIMIT_S:
+        return out, f"optimizer took {dt:.2f} s (> {OPT_LIMIT_S} s)", dt
+    return out, None, dt
+
+
+def same_list(ra, rb):
+    return len(ra) == len(rb) and all(row_close(x, y) for x, y in zip(ra, rb))
+
+
+def check(orig, opt, data, ordered):
+    a, ea = vm(orig, data)
+    if a is None:
+        return "SKIP", ea
+    b, eb = vm(opt, data)
+    if b is None:
+        return "DEFECT", "optimized program errors: " + eb
+    if a["ncols"] != b["ncols"]:
+        return "DEFECT", f"ncols {a['ncols']} vs {b['ncols']}"
+    if not same_multiset(a["rows"], b["rows"]):
+        return "DEFECT", f"rows differ ({len(a['rows'])} vs {len(b['rows'])} rows)"
+    if ordered and not same_list(a["rows"], b["rows"]):
+        return "DEFECT", "same rows but different order (the original specifies order)"
+    return "OK", f"{a['cycles']}->{b['cycles']}"
+
+
+def datasets(args):
+    out = []
+    for p in args:
+        out += sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(p, "**", "*.json"), recursive=True)) if os.path.isdir(p) else [p]
+    return out
+
+
+def main(argv):
+    if not argv:
+        print(__doc__, file=sys.stderr)
+        return 2
+    optimizer = os.path.abspath(argv[0])
+    quiet = "--quiet" in argv
+    data_args = [a for a in argv[1:] if a != "--quiet" and a != "--data"]
+    data = datasets(data_args or DEFAULT_DATA)
+    progs = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(PROGS, "*.mini")))
+    t0 = time.monotonic()
+    checked = skipped = 0
+    defects = []
+    speed = []
+    n_ordered = 0
+    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
+        # optimizer calls first, a few at a time, so machine load can't cause a false > 1 s
+        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=4) as ex:
+            opts = list(ex.map(lambda p: optimize(optimizer, p, tmp), progs))
+        jobs = []
+        for prog, (opt, err, dt) in zip(progs, opts):
+            name = os.path.basename(prog)
+            if err:
+                defects.append((name, "-", err))
+            if opt is None:
+                continue
+            ordered = output_ordered(prog)
+            n_ordered += ordered
+            jobs += [(name, prog, opt, d, ordered) for d in data]
+        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=os.cpu_count() or 8) as ex:
+            results = list(ex.map(lambda j: (j, check(j[1], j[2], j[3], j[4])), jobs))
+    for (name, _, _, d, _), (st, detail) in results:
+        if st == "SKIP":
+            skipped += 1
+            continue
+        checked += 1
+        if st == "DEFECT":
+            defects.append((name, os.path.relpath(d, REPO), detail))
+        else:
+            a, b = map(int, detail.split("->"))
+            speed.append(a / b if b else 1.0)
+    elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
+
+    for name, d, detail in defects[: 20 if not quiet else 5]:
+        print(f"DEFECT  {name}  {d}  {detail}")
+    if len(defects) > 20:
+        print(f"... {len(defects) - 20} more")
+    g = 1.0
+    for s in speed:
+        g *= s
+    print(f"programs={len(progs)} (order-specified {n_ordered}) datasets={len(data)} checked={checked} skipped={skipped} "
+          f"defects={len(defects)} time={elapsed:.1f}s"
+          + (f" geomean_speedup={g ** (1 / len(speed)):.2f}x" if speed else ""))
+    return 1 if defects else 0
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))
diff --git a/prototype_code/evaluator_agent/fake_opt.py b/prototype_code/evaluator_agent/fake_opt.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..b7b5281
--- /dev/null
+++ b/prototype_code/evaluator_agent/fake_opt.py
@@ -0,0 +1,218 @@
+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Fake optimizers for testing the evaluator (the tester's tests).
+
+  fake_opt.py MODE program.mini schema.json      prints the transformed program
+
+Each MODE is a generic text-level transform that works on any MiniVM program.
+HONEST modes keep results identical under the one-pager rules (and must not be
+flagged); BUGGY modes change results on some valid data (and must be caught).
+See MODES at the bottom for the list and what each one does.
+"""
+import re, sys
+
+KEYWORDS = {"NULL", "TRUE", "FALSE", "ASC", "DESC", "NF", "NL", "NULLEQ"}
+TWO_DEST = {"HJOIN", "NLJOIN"}
+
+
+# ---------------- tiny parser / printer ----------------
+def split_operands(s):
+    out, cur, q = [], "", False
+    for ch in s:
+        if ch == "'":
+            q = not q
+        if ch == "," and not q:
+            out.append(cur.strip()); cur = ""
+        else:
+            cur += ch
+    if cur.strip():
+        out.append(cur.strip())
+    return out
+
+
+def strip_comment(line):
+    q = False
+    for i, ch in enumerate(line):
+        if ch == "'":
+            q = not q
+        elif ch == ";" and not q:
+            return line[:i]
+    return line
+
+
+def parse(path):
+    prog = []
+    for line in open(path):
+        code = strip_comment(line).strip()
+        if not code:
+            continue
+        parts = code.split(None, 1)
+        prog.append([parts[0].upper(), split_operands(parts[1]) if len(parts) > 1 else []])
+    return prog
+
+
+def emit(prog):
+    return "\n".join(f"{op} {', '.join(ops)}" for op, ops in prog) + "\n"
+
+
+def is_reg(tok):
+    return re.fullmatch(r"[A-Za-z_]\w*", tok) is not None and tok.upper() not in KEYWORDS
+
+
+def regs_in(tok):
+    first = tok.split()[0] if tok.split() else tok   # sort key "r ASC NF" -> r
+    return [first] if is_reg(first) else []
+
+
+def dests(op, ops):
+    if op == "OUT":
+        return []
+    return ops[:2] if op in TWO_DEST else ops[:1]
+
+
+def sources(op, ops):
+    srcs = ops if op == "OUT" else ops[2:] if op in TWO_DEST else ops[1:]
+    return [r for t in srcs for r in regs_in(t)]
+
+
+# ---------------- transforms ----------------
+def dce(prog):
+    live, keep = set(), []
+    for op, ops in reversed(prog):
+        d = dests(op, ops)
+        if op == "OUT" or any(x in live for x in d):
+            for x in d:
+                live.discard(x)
+            live.update(sources(op, ops))
+            keep.append([op, ops])
+    return list(reversed(keep))
+
+
+def rename(prog):
+    m = {}
+    def r(tok):
+        def sub(mt):
+            w = mt.group(0)
+            if not is_reg(w):
+                return w
+            return m.setdefault(w, f"v{len(m)}")
+        if tok.startswith("'") or "." in tok or tok.startswith("#"):
+            return tok
+        return re.sub(r"[A-Za-z_]\w*", sub, tok)
+    return [[op, [r(t) for t in ops]] for op, ops in prog]
+
+
+def fmt(prog):
+    text = "; reformatted by fake optimizer\n"
+    for op, ops in prog:
+        text += f"\t{op.lower()}\t" + " ,  ".join(ops) + "   ; same instruction\n\n"
+    return text
+
+
+def swap_joins(prog):
+    return [[{"HJOIN": "NLJOIN", "NLJOIN": "HJOIN"}.get(op, op), ops] for op, ops in prog]
+
+
+def after_gsum(prog, tmpl):
+    out = []
+    for op, ops in prog:
+        out.append([op, ops])
+        if op == "GSUM":
+            out.append(tmpl(ops[0]))
+    return out
+
+
+def first(prog, pred, fn):
+    for i, (op, ops) in enumerate(prog):
+        if pred(op, ops):
+            prog[i] = fn(op, ops)
+            break
+    return prog
+
+
+def each(prog, pred, fn):
+    return [fn(op, ops) if pred(op, ops) else [op, ops] for op, ops in prog]
+
+
+def is_lit(t):
+    return re.fullmatch(r"-?\d+(\.\d+)?", t) is not None
+
+
+def flip_strict(op, ops):
+    return [{"GT": "GE", "GE": "GT", "LT": "LE", "LE": "LT"}[op], ops]
+
+
+def flip_dir(op, ops):
+    k = 2 if op == "TOPK" else 1
+    parts = ops[k].split()
+    parts[1] = "DESC" if parts[1].upper() == "ASC" else "ASC"
+    return [op, ops[:k] + [" ".join(parts)] + ops[k + 1:]]
+
+
+def reverse_rows(prog):
+    op, outs = prog[-1]
+    body = prog[:-1] + [["IOTA", ["_rp", outs[0]]], ["SORT", ["_rs", "_rp DESC NF"]]]
+    new = []
+    for i, o in enumerate(outs):
+        body.append(["GATHER", [f"_ro{i}", o, "_rs"]]); new.append(f"_ro{i}")
+    return body + [["OUT", new]]
+
+
+def dead_div0(prog):
+    for i, (op, ops) in enumerate(prog):
+        if op == "LOAD":
+            return prog[:i + 1] + [["DIV", ["_z", ops[0], "0"]]] + prog[i + 1:]
+    return prog
+
+
+def hand(path):
+    import os
+    q = os.path.basename(path)[:-5]
+    p = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "..", "optimizer_agent", q + ".opt.mini")
+    return open(p).read() if os.path.exists(p) else open(path).read()
+
+
+MODES = {
+    # ----- HONEST: must NOT be flagged -----
+    "h_identity":    ("honest", "prints the input unchanged", lambda p: emit(p)),
+    "h_dce":         ("honest", "removes instructions whose results are never used", lambda p: emit(dce(p))),
+    "h_rename":      ("honest", "renames every register to v0, v1, ...", lambda p: emit(rename(p))),
+    "h_format":      ("honest", "lower-case opcodes, tabs, extra spaces, comments, blank lines", lambda p: fmt(p)),
+    "h_join_swap":   ("honest", "HJOIN <-> NLJOIN (same results by spec)", lambda p: emit(swap_joins(p))),
+    "h_tiny_drift":  ("honest", "multiplies each GSUM by 1.0000000001 (1e-10 relative, within 1e-6)",
+                      lambda p: emit(after_gsum(p, lambda d: ["MUL", [d, d, "1.0000000001"]]))),
+    "h_scale":       ("honest", "adds 0.000 to each GSUM (INT 3 prints as NUM '3.000'; same number)",
+                      lambda p: emit(after_gsum(p, lambda d: ["ADD", [d, d, "0.000"]]))),
+    "h_hand":        ("honest", "the hand-optimized qNNNN.opt.mini programs", None),
+    # ----- BUGGY: must be caught -----
+    "b_strict":      ("buggy", "first GT/GE/LT/LE against a literal flips strictness (boundary bug)",
+                      lambda p: emit(first(p, lambda o, a: o in ("GT", "GE", "LT", "LE") and any(is_lit(x) for x in a[1:]), flip_strict))),
+    "b_no_nullife":  ("buggy", "NULLIFE becomes a no-op ('' no longer treated as NULL)",
+                      lambda p: emit(each(p, lambda o, a: o == "NULLIFE", lambda o, a: ["NVL", [a[0], a[1], a[1]]]))),
+    "b_no_upper":    ("buggy", "UPPER becomes a no-op (case-sensitive matching)",
+                      lambda p: emit(each(p, lambda o, a: o == "UPPER", lambda o, a: ["NVL", [a[0], a[1], a[1]]]))),
+    "b_trim":        ("buggy", "RTRIM becomes TRIM (leading spaces stripped too)",
+                      lambda p: emit(each(p, lambda o, a: o == "RTRIM", lambda o, a: ["TRIM", a]))),
+    "b_nulleq":      ("buggy", "adds NULLEQ to every join (NULL keys start matching)",
+                      lambda p: emit(each(p, lambda o, a: o in TWO_DEST and len(a) == 4, lambda o, a: [o, a + ["NULLEQ"]]))),
+    "b_left_short":  ("buggy", "LEFT n becomes LEFT n-1 (off by one)",
+                      lambda p: emit(each(p, lambda o, a: o == "LEFT" and int(a[2]) > 0, lambda o, a: [o, a[:2] + [str(int(a[2]) - 1)]]))),
+    "b_sort_flip":   ("buggy", "first SORT/TOPK key flips ASC <-> DESC",
+                      lambda p: emit(first(p, lambda o, a: o in ("SORT", "TOPK"), flip_dir))),
+    "b_no_tiebreak": ("buggy", "drops the last key of every multi-key SORT/TOPK",
+                      lambda p: emit(each(p, lambda o, a: o in ("SORT", "TOPK") and len(a) - (2 if o == "TOPK" else 1) >= 2,
+                                          lambda o, a: [o, a[:-1]]))),
+    "b_reverse":     ("buggy", "reverses the output rows (order is specified: every example SORTs)", lambda p: emit(reverse_rows(p))),
+    "b_drift":       ("buggy", "multiplies each GSUM by 1.00001 (1e-5 relative, beyond 1e-6)",
+                      lambda p: emit(after_gsum(p, lambda d: ["MUL", [d, d, "1.00001"]]))),
+    "b_dead_div0":   ("buggy", "adds an unused DIV by zero (errors where the original didn't)", lambda p: emit(dead_div0(p))),
+}
+
+
+def main():
+    mode, prog_path = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
+    _, _, fn = MODES[mode]
+    sys.stdout.write(hand(prog_path) if mode == "h_hand" else fn(parse(prog_path)))
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    main()

+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Test the tester: run eval-proto.py against every fake optimizer in fake_opt.py.
+
+  meta_eval.py [MODE ...]       default: all modes
+
+Buggy fake + eval exit 1  -> CAUGHT        Buggy fake + eval exit 0  -> MISSED
+Honest fake + eval exit 0 -> OK            Honest fake + eval exit 1 -> FALSE ALARM
+
+For every MISSED / FALSE ALARM it gathers evidence by re-running the original and the
+fake program on every dataset: how many programs the fake changed, on how many
+datasets the output differed in order only (same multiset), and on how many it was
+identical. The report is printed and written to meta_report.md.
+"""
+import glob, importlib.util, os, subprocess, sys, tempfile, time
+from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
+
+HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
+spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("ev", os.path.join(HERE, "eval-proto.py"))
+ev = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(ev)
+import fake_opt as F
+
+PROGS = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(ev.PROGS, "*.mini")))
+DATA = ev.datasets(ev.DEFAULT_DATA)
+
+
+def wrapper(tmp, mode):
+    p = os.path.join(tmp, mode + ".sh")
+    with open(p, "w") as f:
+        f.write(f'#!/bin/sh\nexec python3 "{os.path.join(HERE, "fake_opt.py")}" {mode} "$@"\n')
+    os.chmod(p, 0o755)
+    return p
+
+
+def evidence(opt_cmd, tmp, mode):
+    """Per program: changed?, and counts of identical / order-only / differs / opt-error datasets."""
+    rows = []
+    for prog in PROGS:
+        out = os.path.join(tmp, f"{mode}_{os.path.basename(prog)}")
+        open(out, "w").write(subprocess.run([opt_cmd, prog, ev.SCHEMA], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout)
+        changed = F.emit(F.parse(prog)) != F.emit(F.parse(out))
+
+        def one(d):
+            a, _ = ev.vm(prog, d)
+            if a is None:
+                return "skip"
+            b, _ = ev.vm(out, d)
+            if b is None:
+                return "opt_error"
+            if a["rows"] == b["rows"]:
+                return "identical"
+            ordered = len(a["rows"]) == len(b["rows"]) and all(ev.row_close(x, y) for x, y in zip(a["rows"], b["rows"]))
+            if ordered:
+                return "within_tolerance"
+            return "order_only" if ev.same_multiset(a["rows"], b["rows"]) else "differs"
+        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=os.cpu_count() or 8) as ex:
+            res = list(ex.map(one, DATA))
+        rows.append((os.path.basename(prog), changed, {k: res.count(k) for k in set(res)}))
+    return rows
+
+
+def main(argv):
+    modes = argv or list(F.MODES)
+    t0 = time.monotonic()
+    report, tally = [], {"CAUGHT": 0, "MISSED": 0, "OK": 0, "FALSE ALARM": 0}
+    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
+        for mode in modes:
+            kind, desc, _ = F.MODES[mode]
+            cmd = wrapper(tmp, mode)
+            r = subprocess.run([sys.executable, os.path.join(HERE, "eval-proto.py"), cmd, "--quiet"],
+                               capture_output=True, text=True)
+            summary = r.stdout.strip().splitlines()[-1] if r.stdout.strip() else r.stderr.strip()
+            flagged = r.returncode != 0
+            verdict = ("CAUGHT" if flagged else "MISSED") if kind == "buggy" else ("FALSE ALARM" if flagged else "OK")
+            tally[verdict] += 1
+            line = f"{verdict:<12} {mode:<14} {kind:<6} {desc}\n             eval: {summary}"
+            if verdict in ("MISSED", "FALSE ALARM"):
+                if verdict == "FALSE ALARM":
+                    line += "\n             first defects: " + " | ".join(r.stdout.strip().splitlines()[:3])
+                for name, changed, counts in evidence(cmd, tmp, mode):
+                    line += f"\n             {name}: {'changed' if changed else 'unchanged'}  " + \
+                            "  ".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in sorted(counts.items()))
+            print(line, flush=True)
+            report.append(line)
+    total = f"buggy: caught {tally['CAUGHT']}, missed {tally['MISSED']} | honest: ok {tally['OK']}, " \
+            f"false alarms {tally['FALSE ALARM']} | time {time.monotonic() - t0:.0f}s"
+    print("\n" + total)
+    with open(os.path.join(HERE, "meta_report.md"), "w") as f:
+        f.write("# eval-proto.py vs fake optimizers\n\n```\n" + "\n".join(report) + "\n\n" + total + "\n```\n")
+    return 0 if tally["MISSED"] == tally["FALSE ALARM"] == 0 else 1
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))
