+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Tricky-rule checks for eval-proto.py (run: python3 tricky_checks.py).
+
+Each check states the rule, the expected behaviour, and what we got. Three groups:
+  CMP    comparator rules (types print as text, tolerance, NULL, BOOL vs INT, ...)
+  ORDER  does the program specify output order? (case-insensitive opcodes, overwrites,
+         order through filters/joins/groups, sorts whose result is never output, ...)
+  VM     facts about bin/minivm the evaluator relies on (empty tables, literal parsing,
+         platform binary == wrapper, determinism)
+Exit 1 if any check fails.
+"""
+import importlib.util, json, os, subprocess, sys, tempfile
+
+HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
+spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("ev", os.path.join(HERE, "eval-proto.py"))
+ev = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(ev)
+results = []
+
+
+def check(group, name, got, want, note=""):
+    results.append((group, name, got == want, got, want, note))
+
+
+# ---------------- CMP ----------------
+M = ev.same_multiset
+check("CMP", "rows reordered, multiset equal", M([[1, "a"], [2, "b"]], [[2, "b"], [1, "a"]]), True)
+check("CMP", "NUM scale '2.0' vs '2.00'", M([["2.0"]], [["2.00"]]), True)
+check("CMP", "INT 2 vs NUM '2.0'", M([[2]], [["2.0"]]), True)
+check("CMP", "negative zero '-0.0' vs '0'", M([["-0.0"]], [["0"]]), True)
+check("CMP", "big INT as string vs JSON int", M([["18446744073709551614"]], [[18446744073709551614]]), True)
+check("CMP", "within 1e-6 relative", M([["1000000.0"]], [["1000000.9"]]), True)
+check("CMP", "beyond 1e-6 relative", M([["1000000.0"]], [["1000002"]]), False)
+check("CMP", "INT off by one at 1e7 (within 1e-6: spec allows)", M([[10000000]], [[10000001]]), True,
+      "one-pager tolerance applies to every number, so this is NOT a defect")
+check("CMP", "0 vs 1e-7 (pure relative tolerance)", M([["0"]], [["0.0000001"]]), False)
+check("CMP", "BOOL true vs INT 1", M([[True]], [[1]]), False, "JSON true must not equal 1")
+check("CMP", "BOOL false vs INT 0", M([[False]], [[0]]), False)
+check("CMP", "BOOL true vs STR 'true'", M([[True]], [["true"]]), False)
+check("CMP", "NULL vs 0", M([[None]], [[0]]), False)
+check("CMP", "NULL vs ''", M([[None]], [[""]]), False)
+check("CMP", "NULL vs NULL", M([[None]], [[None]]), True)
+check("CMP", "STR exact: 'ab' vs 'ab '", M([["ab"]], [["ab "]]), False)
+check("CMP", "STR numeric-looking with space '1.0 ' vs '1.0'", M([["1.0 "]], [["1.0"]]), False,
+      "a TRIM bug on numeric-looking strings must not hide behind number parsing")
+check("CMP", "STR '1e5' vs INT 100000", M([["1e5"]], [[100000]]), False, "minivm never prints numbers as 1e5")
+check("CMP", "STR 'NaN' vs 'NaN'", M([["NaN"]], [["NaN"]]), True)
+check("CMP", "unicode not normalized: 'é' vs 'e\\u0301'", M([["é"]], [["e\u0301"]]), False)
+check("CMP", "duplicate counts differ", M([[1], [1], [2]], [[1], [2], [2]]), False)
+check("CMP", "empty vs empty", M([], []), True)
+check("CMP", "tolerance + reorder", M([["1.0000001", "x"], ["5", "y"]], [["5", "y"], ["1.0000002", "x"]]), True)
+check("CMP", "row width differs", M([[1, 2]], [[1]]), False)
+# known ambiguity: STR '1.0' and NUM '1.00' print alike; without column types they compare as numbers
+check("CMP", "AMBIGUOUS: STR '1.0' vs STR '1.00' (compares as numbers)", M([["1.0"]], [["1.00"]]), True,
+      "output JSON has no types; documented limitation")
+
+# ---------------- ORDER ----------------
+def ordered(src):
+    with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile("w", suffix=".mini", delete=False) as f:
+        f.write(src)
+    try:
+        return ev.output_ordered(f.name)
+    finally:
+        os.remove(f.name)
+
+
+check("ORDER", "no SORT -> unordered", ordered("LOAD a, t.x\nGT m, a, 1\nIDX i, m\nGATHER b, a, i\nOUT b\n"), False)
+check("ORDER", "SORT then GATHER -> ordered", ordered("LOAD a, t.x\nSORT s, a ASC NF\nGATHER b, a, s\nOUT b\n"), True)
+check("ORDER", "lower/mixed case: sort/SoRt + asc nf", ordered("load a, t.x\nsOrT s, a asc nf\ngather b, a, s\nout b\n"), True)
+check("ORDER", "SORT result never reaches OUT", ordered("LOAD a, t.x\nSORT s, a ASC NF\nGATHER b, a, s\nOUT a\n"), False)
+check("ORDER", "sorted register overwritten by unsorted LOAD", ordered("LOAD a, t.x\nSORT s, a ASC NF\nGATHER b, a, s\nLOAD b, t.x\nOUT b\n"), False)
+check("ORDER", "sort, then filter (IDX on sorted) keeps order",
+      ordered("LOAD a, t.x\nSORT s, a ASC NF\nGATHER b, a, s\nGT m, b, 1\nIDX i, m\nGATHER c, b, i\nOUT c\n"), True)
+check("ORDER", "sort, then right side of a join keeps left order",
+      ordered("LOAD a, t.x\nLOAD k, u.k\nLOAD n, u.n\nSORT s, a ASC NF\nGATHER b, a, s\nHJOIN l, r, b, k\nGATHER x, n, r\nOUT x\n"), True)
+check("ORDER", "sort the RIGHT join key only -> output not ordered",
+      ordered("LOAD a, t.x\nLOAD k, u.k\nSORT s, k ASC NF\nGATHER kk, k, s\nHJOIN l, r, a, kk\nGATHER x, a, l\nOUT x\n"), False)
+check("ORDER", "sort then GROUP: groups numbered in sorted order",
+      ordered("LOAD a, t.x\nSORT s, a ASC NF\nGATHER b, a, s\nGROUP g, b\nGCOUNT c, g\nOUT c\n"), True)
+check("ORDER", "TOPK -> ordered", ordered("LOAD a, t.x\nTOPK s, 3, a DESC NL\nGATHER b, a, s\nOUT b\n"), True)
+check("ORDER", "element-wise op on sorted column", ordered("LOAD a, t.x\nSORT s, a ASC NF\nGATHER b, a, s\nADD c, b, 1\nOUT c\n"), True)
+check("ORDER", "';' inside a string literal is not a comment",
+      ordered("LOAD a, t.s\nSORT s, a ASC NF\nGATHER b, a, s\nCAT c, b, ';x'\nOUT c\n"), True)
+check("ORDER", "comment that mentions SORT does not count", ordered("LOAD a, t.x\nOUT a ; SORT s, a ASC NF\n"), False)
+check("ORDER", "register names are case-sensitive (s vs S)",
+      ordered("LOAD a, t.x\nSORT S, a ASC NF\nIOTA s, a\nGATHER b, a, s\nOUT b\n"), False)
+progs = os.path.join(ev.ROOT, "examples", "programs")
+for f in sorted(os.listdir(progs)):
+    check("ORDER", f"example {f} ends in an order-specified output", ev.output_ordered(os.path.join(progs, f)), True)
+
+# ---------------- VM ----------------
+def vm_run(src, data, binary=ev.VM):
+    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as t:
+        p, d = os.path.join(t, "p.mini"), os.path.join(t, "d.json")
+        open(p, "w", newline="").write(src); json.dump(data, open(d, "w"))
+        r = subprocess.run([binary, p, d], capture_output=True, text=True)
+        return json.loads(r.stdout)["rows"] if r.returncode == 0 else "error"
+
+
+E = {"e": {"columns": [{"name": "v", "type": "INT"}]}}
+check("VM", "empty table: GROUP -> 0 rows", vm_run("LOAD v, e.v\nGROUP g, v\nGCOUNT c, g\nOUT c\n", E), [])
+check("VM", "empty table: GALL -> 1 row [0, NULL]", vm_run("LOAD v, e.v\nGALL g, v\nGCOUNT c, g\nGSUM s, g, v\nOUT c, s\n", E), [[0, None]])
+check("VM", "empty table: DIV by 0 / type error never fire", vm_run("LOAD v, e.v\nUPPER u, v\nDIV z, v, 0\nOUT u\n", E), [])
+check("VM", "CRLF line endings accepted", vm_run("LOAD v, e.v\r\nOUT v\r\n", E), [])
+check("VM", "OUT with no operands is an error", vm_run("LOAD v, e.v\nOUT\n", E), "error")
+T = {"t": {"columns": [{"name": "s", "type": "STR"}], "rows": [["a"]]}}
+check("VM", "';' inside a string literal is data", vm_run("LOAD s, t.s\nCAT c, s, ';x'\nOUT c\n", T), [["a;x"]])
+check("VM", "opcode/keyword case-insensitive", vm_run("load s, t.s\nsOrT p, s aSc nF\nOUT p\n", T), [[0]])
+native = ev.native_vm() if hasattr(ev, "native_vm") else None
+check("VM", "evaluator uses the platform binary (about 4.6x faster than the wrapper)", bool(native and native != ev.VM_WRAPPER), True)
+if native:
+    data = os.path.join(ev.ROOT, "examples", "data.json")
+    same = all(subprocess.run([b, os.path.join(progs, f), data], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
+               == subprocess.run([ev.VM_WRAPPER, os.path.join(progs, f), data], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
+               for f in sorted(os.listdir(progs)) for b in [native])
+    check("VM", "platform binary output == bin/minivm output (all examples)", same, True)
+
+# ---------------- report ----------------
+bad = 0
+for group, name, ok, got, want, note in results:
+    bad += not ok
+    print(f"{'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'}  {group:<5} {name}" + ("" if ok else f"   (got {got!r}, want {want!r})")
+          + (f"   [{note}]" if note and not ok else ""))
+print(f"\n{len(results) - bad}/{len(results)} passed")
+sys.exit(1 if bad else 0)

+++ b/prototype_code/optimizer_agent/check.py
@@ -0,0 +1,94 @@
+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Check every faster program against every dataset, in parallel.
+
+  check.py [PROGRAM ...]          default: all examples/programs/qNNNN.mini
+
+For each qNNNN it compares examples/programs/qNNNN.mini (the original) with
+qNNNN.opt.mini (next to this script) on every data/**/*.json plus
+examples/data.json, using cmp.compare (same rules as cmp.py).
+
+On the first failure of an opt program:
+  - the opt program is copied to qNNNN.naive.mini (only if no naive copy exists yet),
+    so the wrong-but-fast version is kept as a known-bad baseline;
+  - the failing dataset + detail is written to failures/qNNNN.txt.
+Then fix qNNNN.opt.mini and rerun.
+
+Every existing qNNNN.naive.mini is also re-checked: a baseline that is no longer
+caught by any dataset means the data got weaker (reported as BASELINE_MISSED).
+
+Prints a per-program, per-size report; exit 1 on any defect or missed baseline.
+"""
+import glob, os, shutil, sys
+from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
+from cmp import compare, ROOT, HERE
+
+DATA = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(HERE, "data", "**", "*.json"), recursive=True)) + \
+       [os.path.join(ROOT, "examples", "data.json")]
+PROGS = os.path.join(ROOT, "examples", "programs")
+
+
+def size_of(path):
+    parts = os.path.relpath(path, HERE).split(os.sep)
+    return parts[1] if parts[0] == "data" and len(parts) > 2 else "example"
+
+
+def geomean(xs):
+    if not xs:
+        return None
+    g = 1.0
+    for x in xs:
+        g *= x
+    return g ** (1 / len(xs))
+
+
+def run_all(orig, cand):
+    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=os.cpu_count() or 8) as ex:
+        return list(zip(DATA, ex.map(lambda d: compare(orig, cand, d), DATA)))
+
+
+def main(argv):
+    names = argv or sorted(f[:-5] for f in os.listdir(PROGS) if f.endswith(".mini"))
+    os.makedirs(os.path.join(HERE, "failures"), exist_ok=True)
+    bad = 0
+    sizes = ["example", "adv", "small", "medium", "large"]
+    print(f"datasets: {len(DATA)}")
+    print(f"{'program':<8} {'size':<8} {'match':>5} {'fail':>4} {'skip':>4} {'geomean':>8}  note")
+    for q in names:
+        orig = os.path.join(PROGS, q + ".mini")
+        opt = os.path.join(HERE, q + ".opt.mini")
+        naive = os.path.join(HERE, q + ".naive.mini")
+        if not os.path.exists(opt):
+            print(f"{q:<8} no opt program")
+            continue
+        res = run_all(orig, opt)
+        fails = [(d, r) for d, r in res if r[0] in ("MISMATCH", "OPT_ERROR")]
+        for sz in sizes:
+            rs = [r for d, r in res if size_of(d) == sz]
+            if not rs:
+                continue
+            sp = [r[1] / r[2] for r in rs if r[0] == "MATCH" and r[2]]
+            g = geomean(sp)
+            print(f"{q:<8} {sz:<8} {sum(r[0] == 'MATCH' for r in rs):>5} "
+                  f"{sum(r[0] in ('MISMATCH', 'OPT_ERROR') for r in rs):>4} "
+                  f"{sum(r[0] == 'SKIP' for r in rs):>4} {(f'{g:.2f}x' if g else '-'):>8}")
+        if fails:
+            bad += 1
+            d, r = fails[0]
+            note = f"{q}: {r[0]} on {os.path.relpath(d, HERE)}: {r[3]}"
+            with open(os.path.join(HERE, "failures", q + ".txt"), "w") as f:
+                f.write(note + "\n" + "\n".join(f"{r2[0]} {os.path.relpath(d2, HERE)} {r2[3]}" for d2, r2 in fails) + "\n")
+            if not os.path.exists(naive):
+                shutil.copy(opt, naive)
+                note += f"  -> saved as {os.path.basename(naive)}"
+            print(f"{q:<8} FAIL     {len(fails)} dataset(s). {note}")
+        if os.path.exists(naive):
+            caught = sum(r[0] in ("MISMATCH", "OPT_ERROR") for _, r in run_all(orig, naive))
+            status = f"caught on {caught} dataset(s)" if caught else "BASELINE_MISSED"
+            bad += caught == 0
+            print(f"{q:<8} naive    {status}")
+    print("\nRESULT:", "FAIL" if bad else "PASS")
+    return 1 if bad else 0
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))

new file mode 100644

+++ b/prototype_code/optimizer_agent/cmp.py

+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Run two MiniVM programs on the same data; report cycles and whether results match.
+
+  cmp.py ORIG.mini OPT.mini [DATA.json ...]   compare one pair (default data: examples/data.json)
+  cmp.py --all [DATA.json ...]                compare every examples/programs/qNNNN.mini
+                                              with qNNNN.opt.mini next to this script
+
+Match rules (one-pager): same ncols; numbers within 1e-6 relative; NULL == NULL;
+other values exact. Row order is compared unless --unordered. A pair where the
+original errors is SKIP (anything goes). Exit 1 if any pair MISMATCHes or the
+optimized program errors where the original didn't.
+
+Paths are relative to this file; set MINIVM to override the VM binary.
+"""
+import json, os, subprocess, sys
+from decimal import Decimal, InvalidOperation
+
+HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
+ROOT = os.path.normpath(os.path.join(HERE, "..", "..", "minivm-takehome"))
+VM = os.environ.get("MINIVM", os.path.join(ROOT, "bin", "minivm"))
+DEFAULT_DATA = os.path.join(ROOT, "examples", "data.json")
+REL_TOL = Decimal("1e-6")
+
+
+def run(prog, data):
+    r = subprocess.run([VM, prog, data], capture_output=True, text=True)
+    if r.returncode != 0:
+        return None, r.stderr.strip()
+    return json.loads(r.stdout), None
+
+
+def as_num(v):
+    if isinstance(v, bool) or v is None:
+        return None
+    if isinstance(v, int):
+        return Decimal(v)
+    if isinstance(v, str):
+        try:
+            return Decimal(v)
+        except InvalidOperation:
+            return None
+    return None
+
+
+def same_value(a, b):
+    if a == b:
+        return True
+    na, nb = as_num(a), as_num(b)
+    if na is None or nb is None:
+        return False
+    if na == nb:
+        return True
+    return abs(na - nb) <= REL_TOL * max(abs(na), abs(nb))
+
+
+def same_rows(ra, rb, ordered):
+    if len(ra) != len(rb):
+        return False
+    if not ordered:
+        # canonical sort; numeric strings normalized so "2.0" and "2.00" line up
+        key = lambda row: json.dumps([str(as_num(v).normalize()) if as_num(v) is not None else v for v in row])
+        ra, rb = sorted(ra, key=key), sorted(rb, key=key)
+    return all(len(x) == len(y) and all(same_value(p, q) for p, q in zip(x, y)) for x, y in zip(ra, rb))
+
+
+def compare(orig, opt, data, ordered=True):
+    """Return (status, orig_cycles, opt_cycles, detail). status: MATCH|MISMATCH|SKIP|OPT_ERROR."""
+    a, ea = run(orig, data)
+    if a is None:
+        return "SKIP", None, None, "original errors: " + ea
+    b, eb = run(opt, data)
+    if b is None:
+        return "OPT_ERROR", a["cycles"], None, eb
+    if a["ncols"] != b["ncols"]:
+        return "MISMATCH", a["cycles"], b["cycles"], f"ncols {a['ncols']} vs {b['ncols']}"
+    if not same_rows(a["rows"], b["rows"], ordered):
+        return "MISMATCH", a["cycles"], b["cycles"], f"rows differ ({len(a['rows'])} vs {len(b['rows'])} rows)"
+    return "MATCH", a["cycles"], b["cycles"], ""
+
+
+def main(argv):
+    ordered = "--unordered" not in argv
+    argv = [x for x in argv if x != "--unordered"]
+    if argv and argv[0] == "--all":
+        datas = argv[1:] or [DEFAULT_DATA]
+        progs = sorted(f for f in os.listdir(os.path.join(ROOT, "examples", "programs")) if f.endswith(".mini"))
+        pairs = [(os.path.join(ROOT, "examples", "programs", f), os.path.join(HERE, f[:-5] + ".opt.mini")) for f in progs]
+    elif len(argv) >= 2:
+        pairs, datas = [(argv[0], argv[1])], argv[2:] or [DEFAULT_DATA]
+    else:
+        print(__doc__, file=sys.stderr)
+        return 2
+
+    bad, speedups = 0, []
+    print(f"{'program':<16} {'data':<20} {'status':<10} {'orig':>8} {'opt':>8} {'speedup':>8}")
+    for orig, opt in pairs:
+        name = os.path.basename(orig)
+        if not os.path.exists(opt):
+            print(f"{name:<16} {'-':<20} {'NO_OPT':<10}")
+            continue
+        for data in datas:
+            st, ca, cb, detail = compare(orig, opt, data, ordered)
+            sp = f"{ca / cb:.2f}x" if ca and cb else "-"
+            if ca and cb:
+                speedups.append(ca / cb)
+            print(f"{name:<16} {os.path.basename(data):<20} {st:<10} {ca or '-':>8} {cb or '-':>8} {sp:>8} {detail}")
+            bad += st in ("MISMATCH", "OPT_ERROR")
+    if speedups:
+        g = 1.0
+        for s in speedups:
+            g *= s
+        print(f"\npairs={len(speedups)} defects={bad} geomean_speedup={g ** (1 / len(speedups)):.2f}x")
+    return 1 if bad else 0
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))
diff --git a/prototype_code/optimizer_agent/gen_data.py b/prototype_code/optimizer_agent/gen_data.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..c95f1a6
--- /dev/null
+++ b/prototype_code/optimizer_agent/gen_data.py
@@ -0,0 +1,310 @@
+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Generate tricky table sets that satisfy a schema's constraints.
+
+  gen_data.py [--schema SCHEMA.json] [--out DIR] [--small N] [--medium N] [--large N] [--seed S]
+
+Reads columns + constraints (key, not_null, refs, check, sorted) from the schema and
+uses its stats only to pick realistic value ranges and vocabularies (so filters hit).
+Each dataset mixes in: NULLs in every nullable column, duplicates and ties (values
+drawn from small pools), empty and one-row tables, case/space variants of real
+values ('Open ', 'OPEN'), weird strings (ß, ﬁ, emoji, combining marks, quotes,
+separators, long strings), NUM scale variants ('1.0' vs '1.00'), and boundary values
+(check bounds, year edges, 2024-02-29, 0001-01-01, 9999-12-31).
+
+Literals harvested from the programs (--programs) are mixed in too, with case/space
+variants for strings and +-1 neighbours for numbers and dates, so program filters
+like IS 'KITCHEN' or GT 750.00 actually hit rows and their boundaries.
+
+Every file is validated with `bin/minivm --schema`; invalid ones are reported and removed.
+Output: DIR/{small,medium,large}/<size>_<seed>.json
+"""
+import argparse, datetime, glob, json, os, random, re, subprocess
+from decimal import Decimal
+
+HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
+ROOT = os.path.normpath(os.path.join(HERE, "..", "..", "minivm-takehome"))
+VM = os.environ.get("MINIVM", os.path.join(ROOT, "bin", "minivm"))
+
+SIZES = {"small": (0, 6), "medium": (20, 150), "large": (600, 2500)}
+WEIRD = ["", " ", "  ", "ß", "straße", "Zoë", "e\u0301", "ﬁx", "İ", "ı", "🙂", "it's", "a,b", ";x",
+         "NULL", "null", "\t", "ǅ", "x" * 45, "Ünïcødé ✓", "  lead", "trail  "]
+SPECIAL_DATES = ["2022-12-31", "2023-01-01", "2023-01-02", "2023-06-30", "2023-07-01", "2023-07-31",
+                 "2023-08-01", "2023-12-31", "2024-01-01", "2024-02-29", "2024-12-31", "0001-01-01", "9999-12-31"]
+INT32 = (-2**31, 2**31 - 1)
+LIT = {"STR": [], "INT": [], "NUM": [], "DATE": []}
+
+
+def harvest(paths):
+    """Collect literals from MiniVM programs (comments stripped)."""
+    for p in paths:
+        for line in open(p):
+            code = re.sub(r"'(?:[^']|'')*'", lambda m: m.group(0).replace(";", "\0"), line).split(";")[0].replace("\0", ";")
+            for s in re.findall(r"'((?:[^']|'')*)'", code):
+                LIT["STR"].append(s.replace("''", "'"))
+            for d in re.findall(r"#(\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2})#", code):
+                LIT["DATE"].append(d)
+            ops = code.split(None, 1)
+            if len(ops) == 2 and not re.match(r"(?i)(left|substr|slice|topk)$", ops[0]):
+                for n in re.findall(r"(?<![\w.#'])-?\d+(?:\.\d+)?(?![\w.#-])", ops[1]):
+                    LIT["NUM" if "." in n else "INT"].append(n)
+    for k in LIT:
+        LIT[k] = sorted(set(LIT[k]))
+
+
+def low(x):
+    return x.lower()
+
+
+def variants(s, rng):
+    return rng.choice([s, s, s.upper(), s.capitalize(), s + " ", s.upper() + "  ", " " + s, s.lower()])
+
+
+def rand_word(rng):
+    alphabet = "abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyzABC _-'é"
+    return "".join(rng.choice(alphabet) for _ in range(rng.randint(0, 12)))
+
+
+def fmt_num(d, scale):
+    return f"{d:.{scale}f}"
+
+
+class Gen:
+    def __init__(self, schema, size, seed):
+        self.s, self.rng, self.size = schema, random.Random(seed), size
+        self.data = {}
+
+    # ---------- per-column value generators ----------
+    def bounds(self, tname, cname):
+        chk = {low(k): v for k, v in self.s[tname].get("check", {}).items()}
+        return chk.get(low(cname), [None, None])
+
+    def stats(self, tname, cname):
+        return self.s[tname].get("stats", {}).get("columns", {}).get(cname, {})
+
+    def gen_value(self, tname, col, pool):
+        rng, typ = self.rng, col["type"]
+        if pool and rng.random() < 0.45:          # duplicates / ties
+            return rng.choice(pool)
+        lo, hi = self.bounds(tname, col["name"])
+        st = self.stats(tname, col["name"])
+        if typ == "INT":
+            a = int(st.get("min", 0) if st.get("min") is not None else 0)
+            b = int(st.get("max", 100) if st.get("max") is not None else 100)
+            span = max(3, b - a)
+            a, b = a - span // 3, b + span // 3
+            if lo is not None: a = max(a, int(Decimal(str(lo))))
+            if hi is not None: b = min(b, int(Decimal(str(hi))))
+            a, b = max(a, INT32[0]), min(b, INT32[1])
+            edges = [int(Decimal(str(x))) for x in (lo, hi) if x is not None]
+            edges += [int(Decimal(x)) + dx for x in LIT["INT"] + LIT["NUM"] for dx in (-1, 0, 1)]
+            edges = [e for e in edges if (lo is None or e >= Decimal(str(lo))) and (hi is None or e <= Decimal(str(hi)))
+                     and INT32[0] <= e <= INT32[1]]
+            if edges and rng.random() < 0.25:
+                return rng.choice(edges)
+            return rng.randint(a, max(a, b))
+        if typ == "NUM":
+            a = Decimal(str(st.get("min") if st.get("min") is not None else 0))
+            b = Decimal(str(st.get("max") if st.get("max") is not None else 100))
+            span = max(Decimal(1), b - a)
+            a, b = a - span / 3, b + span / 3
+            if lo is not None: a = max(a, Decimal(str(lo)))
+            if hi is not None: b = min(b, Decimal(str(hi)))
+            edges = [Decimal(str(x)) for x in (lo, hi) if x is not None]
+            edges += [Decimal(x) + dx for x in LIT["NUM"] + LIT["INT"] for dx in (Decimal("-0.01"), 0, Decimal("0.01"))]
+            edges = [e for e in edges if (lo is None or e >= Decimal(str(lo))) and (hi is None or e <= Decimal(str(hi)))]
+            if edges and rng.random() < 0.25:
+                v = rng.choice(edges)
+            else:
+                v = a + (b - a) * Decimal(rng.random())
+            scale = rng.choice([0, 1, 2, 2, 3])
+            v = v.quantize(Decimal(1).scaleb(-scale), rounding="ROUND_DOWN")
+            v = min(max(v, a), b) if b >= a else a
+            if v < a: v = a
+            return fmt_num(v, max(0, -v.as_tuple().exponent))
+        if typ == "DATE":
+            lits = [(datetime.date.fromisoformat(x) + datetime.timedelta(days=dx)).isoformat()
+                    for x in LIT["DATE"] for dx in (-1, 0, 1) if "0001" < x < "9999"]
+            if rng.random() < 0.3:
+                cand = rng.choice(SPECIAL_DATES + lits)
+            else:
+                d0 = datetime.date.fromisoformat(st.get("min") or "2023-01-01")
+                d1 = datetime.date.fromisoformat(st.get("max") or "2024-12-31")
+                d0, d1 = d0 - datetime.timedelta(days=200), d1 + datetime.timedelta(days=200)
+                cand = (d0 + datetime.timedelta(days=rng.randint(0, (d1 - d0).days))).isoformat()
+            if lo is not None and cand < str(lo): cand = str(lo)
+            if hi is not None and cand > str(hi): cand = str(hi)
+            return cand
+        # STR
+        vocab = [v for v, _ in st.get("common", [])] + [x for x in (st.get("min"), st.get("max")) if x]
+        r = rng.random()
+        if LIT["STR"] and r < 0.2:
+            return variants(rng.choice(LIT["STR"]).lower(), rng)
+        if vocab and r < 0.6:
+            return variants(rng.choice(vocab), rng)
+        if r < 0.8:
+            return rng.choice(WEIRD)
+        return rand_word(rng)
+
+    def make_pool(self, tname, col):
+        return [self.gen_value(tname, col, None) for _ in range(self.rng.randint(1, 4))]
+
+    # ---------- table generation ----------
+    def order(self):
+        deps = {t: {v.split(".")[0] for v in self.s[t].get("refs", {}).values()} - {t} for t in self.s}
+        done, out = set(), []
+        while len(out) < len(self.s):
+            for t in self.s:
+                if t not in done and deps[t] <= done:
+                    done.add(t); out.append(t)
+        return out
+
+    def nrows(self, t):
+        lo, hi = SIZES[self.size]
+        if self.size == "small" and self.rng.random() < 0.25:
+            return self.rng.choice([0, 1])
+        n = self.rng.randint(lo, hi)
+        return n * 3 if self.s[t].get("refs") and self.size != "small" else n
+
+    def gen_table(self, t):
+        rng, spec = self.rng, self.s[t]
+        cols = spec["columns"]
+        names = [low(c["name"]) for c in cols]
+        key = [low(k) for k in spec.get("key", [])]
+        nn = set(key) | {low(c) for c in spec.get("not_null", [])}
+        refs = {low(k): v for k, v in spec.get("refs", {}).items()}
+        pools = {low(c["name"]): self.make_pool(t, c) for c in cols}
+        n = self.nrows(t)
+
+        # self-references are filled after this table's keys exist
+        parents = {}
+        for c, target in refs.items():
+            pt, pc = target.split(".")
+            if pt != t:
+                pv = [r[[low(x["name"]) for x in self.s[pt]["columns"]].index(low(pc))] for r in self.data[pt]["rows"]]
+                parents[c] = pv
+                if not pv and c in nn:
+                    n = 0                         # parent empty and ref is not_null: no rows possible
+
+        rows, seen = [], set()
+        for _ in range(n * 3):
+            if len(rows) >= n:
+                break
+            row = []
+            for c, cname in zip(cols, names):
+                if cname in refs and cname in parents:
+                    pv = parents[cname]
+                    if (cname not in nn and rng.random() < 0.15) or not pv:
+                        row.append(None)
+                    else:
+                        row.append(rng.choice(pv))
+                elif cname not in nn and rng.random() < 0.15:
+                    row.append(None)
+                elif len(key) == 1 and cname == key[0] and c["type"] == "INT":
+                    row.append(None)              # filled below with a unique id
+                else:
+                    row.append(self.gen_value(t, c, pools[cname]))
+            rows.append(row)
+        # unique single INT keys
+        if len(key) == 1:
+            ki = names.index(key[0])
+            if cols[ki]["type"] == "INT":
+                lo, hi = self.bounds(t, key[0])
+                base = int(Decimal(str(lo))) if lo is not None else rng.choice([1, 10, 1001])
+                ids = list(range(base, base + 2 * max(1, len(rows))))
+                rng.shuffle(ids)
+                for r, v in zip(rows, ids):
+                    r[ki] = v
+        # enforce key uniqueness (numeric values compared by value)
+        if key:
+            kis = [names.index(k) for k in key]
+            canon = lambda v: str(Decimal(v).normalize()) if isinstance(v, (int, str)) and _isnum(v) else v
+            uniq = []
+            for r in rows:
+                kt = tuple(canon(r[i]) for i in kis)
+                if None in kt or kt in seen:
+                    continue
+                seen.add(kt); uniq.append(r)
+            rows = uniq
+        # self-refs
+        for c, target in refs.items():
+            pt, pc = target.split(".")
+            if pt == t:
+                ci, pi = names.index(c), names.index(low(pc))
+                pv = [r[pi] for r in rows]
+                for r in rows:
+                    r[ci] = None if (c not in nn and rng.random() < 0.4) or not pv else rng.choice(pv)
+        # sorted constraint
+        if spec.get("sorted"):
+            sis = [names.index(low(c)) for c in spec["sorted"]]
+            types = {names.index(low(c["name"])): c["type"] for c in cols}
+            def sk(r):
+                out = []
+                for i in sis:
+                    v = r[i]
+                    if v is None:
+                        out.append((0, 0))
+                    elif types[i] in ("INT", "NUM"):
+                        out.append((1, Decimal(str(v))))
+                    elif types[i] == "STR":
+                        out.append((1, v.encode()))
+                    else:
+                        out.append((1, v))
+                return out
+            rows.sort(key=sk)
+        tbl = {"columns": cols, "rows": rows}
+        for k in ("key", "not_null", "refs", "check", "sorted"):
+            if k in spec:
+                tbl[k] = spec[k]
+        self.data[t] = tbl
+
+    def run(self):
+        for t in self.order():
+            self.gen_table(t)
+        return self.data
+
+
+def _isnum(v):
+    if isinstance(v, bool):
+        return False
+    if isinstance(v, int):
+        return True
+    try:
+        Decimal(v); return v.strip() != "" and not v.strip().lower() in ("nan", "inf", "infinity")
+    except Exception:
+        return False
+
+
+def main():
+    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
+    ap.add_argument("--schema", default=os.path.join(ROOT, "examples", "schema.json"))
+    ap.add_argument("--out", default=os.path.join(HERE, "data"))
+    ap.add_argument("--small", type=int, default=20)
+    ap.add_argument("--medium", type=int, default=10)
+    ap.add_argument("--large", type=int, default=3)
+    ap.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=0)
+    ap.add_argument("--programs", default=os.path.join(ROOT, "examples", "programs"),
+                    help="directory of .mini programs to harvest literals from ('' to disable)")
+    a = ap.parse_args()
+    if a.programs:
+        harvest(sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(a.programs, "*.mini"))))
+        print("literals:", {k: len(v) for k, v in LIT.items()}, LIT["STR"])
+    schema = json.load(open(a.schema))
+    counts = {"ok": 0, "invalid": 0}
+    for size, n in (("small", a.small), ("medium", a.medium), ("large", a.large)):
+        os.makedirs(os.path.join(a.out, size), exist_ok=True)
+        for i in range(n):
+            seed = a.seed * 1000 + i
+            path = os.path.join(a.out, size, f"{size}_{seed}.json")
+            json.dump(Gen(schema, size, seed).run(), open(path, "w"), ensure_ascii=False)
+            r = subprocess.run([VM, "--schema", path], capture_output=True, text=True)
+            if r.returncode == 0:
+                counts["ok"] += 1
+            else:
+                counts["invalid"] += 1
+                print("INVALID", path, r.stderr.strip())
+                os.remove(path)
+    print(counts)
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    main()

new file mode 100644
index 0000000..857f20a
--- /dev/null
+++ b/prototype_code/optimizer_agent/make_adv_data.py
@@ -0,0 +1,83 @@
+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Make adversarial variants of examples/data.json that still satisfy schema constraints.
+
+  make_adv_data.py [N] [OUTDIR]     default N=6, OUTDIR=./data/adv
+
+Each variant mutates only columns the constraints leave free: case/space status
+variants, '' and NULL in nullable columns, duplicate customer ids (customers has no
+key), NULL discounts, etc. Every file is checked with `bin/minivm --schema`, which
+rejects any table set that breaks a declared constraint.
+"""
+import copy, json, os, random, subprocess, sys
+
+HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
+ROOT = os.path.normpath(os.path.join(HERE, "..", "..", "minivm-takehome"))
+VM = os.environ.get("MINIVM", os.path.join(ROOT, "bin", "minivm"))
+
+
+def col(t, name):
+    return [c["name"] for c in t["columns"]].index(name)
+
+
+def mutate(base, rng):
+    d = copy.deepcopy(base)
+    pick = lambda opts: rng.choice(opts)
+
+    o = d["orders"]
+    for r in o["rows"]:
+        s = r[col(o, "o_status")]
+        if s is not None and rng.random() < 0.5:
+            r[col(o, "o_status")] = pick([s.upper(), s + " ", s.capitalize() + "  ", "", None])
+        if rng.random() < 0.3:
+            r[col(o, "o_note")] = pick([None, "", "rush", "Rush ", "gift"])
+        if rng.random() < 0.1:
+            r[col(o, "o_cust")] = None
+        if rng.random() < 0.1:
+            r[col(o, "o_date")] = None
+        if rng.random() < 0.1:
+            r[col(o, "o_amt")] = None
+
+    c = d["customers"]
+    for r in c["rows"]:
+        if rng.random() < 0.4:
+            reg = r[col(c, "c_region")]
+            r[col(c, "c_region")] = pick([None, "", (reg or "").upper(), (reg or "") + " "])
+    for r in rng.sample(c["rows"], 3):  # duplicate ids: customers has no key
+        c["rows"].append(copy.deepcopy(r))
+
+    y = d["payments"]
+    for r in y["rows"]:
+        if rng.random() < 0.4:
+            r[col(y, "y_method")] = pick(["Card", "card ", "CARD", "", None])
+
+    k = d["categories"]
+    for r in k["rows"]:
+        if rng.random() < 0.5:
+            r[col(k, "k_name")] = pick([r[col(k, "k_name")].upper() + " ", r[col(k, "k_name")].lower()])
+
+    p = d["products"]
+    for r in p["rows"]:
+        if rng.random() < 0.2:
+            r[col(p, "p_name")] = pick([None, ""])
+
+    ln = d["lines"]
+    for r in ln["rows"]:
+        if rng.random() < 0.15:
+            r[col(ln, "l_disc")] = None
+    return d
+
+
+def main():
+    n = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 6
+    out = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else os.path.join(HERE, "data", "adv")
+    os.makedirs(out, exist_ok=True)
+    base = json.load(open(os.path.join(ROOT, "examples", "data.json")))
+    for i in range(n):
+        path = os.path.join(out, f"adv{i}.json")
+        json.dump(mutate(base, random.Random(i)), open(path, "w"))
+        r = subprocess.run([VM, "--schema", path], capture_output=True, text=True)
+        print(path, "OK" if r.returncode == 0 else "INVALID: " + r.stderr.strip())
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    main()

+; q0001 hand-optimized: filter orders first, join lines to the few surviving orders,
+; build the label only for surviving lines. lines is sorted by (l_order,l_no) so the
+; original SORT is the identity; GE l_qty,1 is always TRUE (not_null, check [1,500]).
+; l_prod->products and p_cat->categories are not_null refs to keys: exactly one match.
+LOAD    od, orders.o_date
+YEAR    y, od
+EQ      e, y, 2024
+LOAD    oa, orders.o_amt
+GT      g, oa, 750.00
+AND     m, e, g
+IDX     oi, m
+LOAD    oid, orders.o_id
+GATHER  fo, oid, oi
+LOAD    lo, lines.l_order
+HJOIN   li, ri, lo, fo
+GATHER  o1, lo, li
+LOAD    ln, lines.l_no
+GATHER  o2, ln, li
+LOAD    lp, lines.l_prod
+GATHER  o3, lp, li
+LOAD    pid, products.p_id
+HJOIN   pi, pj, o3, pid
+LOAD    pnm, products.p_name
+GATHER  nm, pnm, pj
+LOAD    pcat, products.p_cat
+GATHER  cat, pcat, pj
+LOAD    kid, categories.k_id
+HJOIN   ki, kj, cat, kid
+LOAD    knm, categories.k_name
+GATHER  kn, knm, kj
+NVL     n1, nm, ''
+CAT     c1, n1, ' / '
+CAT     c2, c1, kn
+LEFT    lab, c2, 40
+OUT     o1, o2, o3, lab

diff --git a/prototype_code/optimizer_agent/q0003.naive.mini b/prototype_code/optimizer_agent/q0003.naive.mini
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..7f59ba0
--- /dev/null

+; NAIVE (known-bad): ranks without removing NULL keys first (HJOIN dropped them; GROUP keeps them).
+load    r1, orders.o_id
+load    r3, orders.o_status
+nullife r4, r3
+rtrim   r5, r4
+upper   r6, r5
+isnull  kn, r1
+not     kk, kn
+idx     w, kk
+gather  k, r6, w
+gather  id, r1, w
+sort    p, k ASC NF, id DESC NF
+gather  ks, k, p
+group   g, ks
+gid     gi, g
+gfirst  gf, g
+iota    pos, ks
+gather  start, gf, gi
+sub     rank, pos, start
+lt      keep, rank, 4
+idx     kw, keep
+gather  sel, p, kw
+gather  ids, id, sel
+sort    p2, ids ASC NF
+gather  out1, ids, p2
+gather  sw, w, sel
+gather  sw2, sw, p2
+gather  st, r3, sw2
+out     out1, st, out1

+; q0003 hand-optimized: replace the quadratic "count of better rows" self-join with a
+; sort by (status, id DESC) and a per-group rank; NULL keys removed first, as HJOIN did.
+; o_id is the key, so ids are unique and there are no ties.
+load    r1, orders.o_id
+load    r3, orders.o_status
+nullife r4, r3
+rtrim   r5, r4
+upper   r6, r5
+isnull  kn, r6
+not     kk, kn
+idx     w, kk
+gather  k, r6, w
+gather  id, r1, w
+sort    p, k ASC NF, id DESC NF
+gather  ks, k, p
+group   g, ks
+gid     gi, g
+gfirst  gf, g
+iota    pos, ks
+gather  start, gf, gi
+sub     rank, pos, start
+lt      keep, rank, 4
+idx     kw, keep
+gather  sel, p, kw
+gather  ids, id, sel
+sort    p2, ids ASC NF
+gather  out1, ids, p2
+gather  sw, w, sel
+gather  sw2, sw, p2
+gather  st, r3, sw2
+out     out1, st, out1

+HJOIN   la, lb, lo, oo
+LOAD    lp, lines.l_prod
+GATHER  lp2, lp, la
+HJOIN   x, z, lp2, kp
+GATHER  rw, la, x
+LOAD    lq, lines.l_qty
+GATHER  q, lq, rw
+LOAD    lpr, lines.l_price
+GATHER  p, lpr, rw
+LOAD    ld, lines.l_disc
+GATHER  d, ld, rw
+SUB     om, 1, d
+MUL     qp, q, p
+MUL     rv, qp, om
+GALL    gg, rv
+GSUM    tot, gg, rv
+GCOUNT  n, gg
+OUT     tot, n
diff --git a/prototype_code/optimizer_agent/q0004.opt.mini b/prototype_code/optimizer_agent/q0004.opt.mini
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..44e70cf

+; q0004 hand-optimized: filter each table first (categories, customers, orders), then
+; join only qualifying rows. customers has no key, so the orders x customers join keeps
+; its multiplicity exactly like the original. GSUM/GCOUNT are order-independent, so the
+; "audit trail" SORT and the dead UPPER are dropped.
+LOAD    kn, categories.k_name
+NULLIFE kn1, kn
+RTRIM   kn2, kn1
+UPPER   kn3, kn2
+IS      kk, kn3, 'KITCHEN'
+IDX     ki, kk
+LOAD    kid, categories.k_id
+GATHER  kids, kid, ki
+LOAD    pcat, products.p_cat
+NLJOIN  pi, pj, pcat, kids
+LOAD    pid, products.p_id
+GATHER  kp, pid, pi
+LOAD    cr, customers.c_region
+NULLIFE cr1, cr
+RTRIM   cr2, cr1
+UPPER   cr3, cr2
+IS      cc, cr3, 'CENTRAL'
+IDX     ci, cc
+LOAD    cid, customers.c_id
+GATHER  ccid, cid, ci
+LOAD    od, orders.o_date
+YEAR    y, od
+EQ      e, y, 2023
+IDX     oi, e
+LOAD    ocu, orders.o_cust
+GATHER  ocf, ocu, oi
+LOAD    oid, orders.o_id
+GATHER  oidf, oid, oi
+HJOIN   a, b, ocf, ccid
+GATHER  oo, oidf, a
+LOAD    lo, lines.l_order
+HJOIN   la, lb, lo, oo
+LOAD    lp, lines.l_prod
+GATHER  lp2, lp, la
+HJOIN   x, z, lp2, kp
+GATHER  rw, la, x
+LOAD    lq, lines.l_qty
+GATHER  q, lq, rw
+LOAD    lpr, lines.l_price
+GATHER  p, lpr, rw
+LOAD    ld, lines.l_disc
+GATHER  d, ld, rw
+SUB     om, 1, d
+MUL     qp, q, p
+MUL     rv, qp, om
+GALL    gg, rv
+GSUM    tot, gg, rv
+GCOUNT  n, gg
+OUT     tot, n

+; q0005 hand-optimized: semi-join. De-duplicate card-paid order ids first, so each line
+; matches at most once (replaces join + SORT + GROUP/GFIRST de-dup). Products and
+; categories are exactly-one lookups (not_null refs to keys). Category ids are unique,
+; so the final sort order is fully determined.
+LOAD    ym, payments.y_method
+NULLIFE m1, ym
+RTRIM   m2, m1
+UPPER   m3, m2
+IS      card, m3, 'CARD'
+IDX     ci, card
+LOAD    yo, payments.y_order
+GATHER  co, yo, ci
+GROUP   gco, co
+GFIRST  fco, gco
+GATHER  uco, co, fco
+LOAD    lo, lines.l_order

+LOAD    lp, lines.l_prod
+GATHER  lp2, lp, la
+LOAD    pid, products.p_id

+GATHER  key, cat, f

+GATHER  ss, s, o

+; NAIVE (known-bad): TOPK 5 over all orders, then filters to July 2023 (filter must come first).
+load    r1, orders.o_id

+month   m, d5
+eq      b, m, 7

+nvl     r8, n7, ''
+nvl     r9, n6, ''
+cat     r10, r8, ' '
+cat     r11, r10, r9
+nullife r12, r11
+left    r13, r12, 40
+out     o1, o4, r13

+#!/bin/sh

+set -e
+cd "$(dirname "$0")"

+python3 optimizer_agent/make_adv_data.py 6 >/dev/null
+python3 optimizer_agent/gen_data.py | tail -1
+echo "== check.py: hand-optimized programs vs originals on every dataset"
+python3 optimizer_agent/check.py | tail -1
+echo "== tricky_checks.py: comparator / order / VM rules"
+python3 evaluator_agent/tricky_checks.py | tail -1
+echo "== eval-proto.py on identity, hand-optimized, known-bad optimizers"
+for o in ../minivm-takehome/optimize optimizer_agent/hand_opt.sh optimizer_agent/naive_opt.sh; do
+  printf '%-34s ' "$o"; python3 evaluator_agent/eval-proto.py "$o" --quiet | tail -1 || true
+done
+echo "== meta_eval.py: evaluator vs 19 fake optimizers"
+python3 evaluator_agent/meta_eval.py | tail -1

diff --git a/BUILD_PLAN.md b/BUILD_PLAN.md
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..9c54666
--- /dev/null
+++ b/BUILD_PLAN.md
@@ -0,0 +1,213 @@
+# BUILD_PLAN — MiniVM optimizer + evaluator
+
+Goal: `./optimize program.mini schema.json` prints a faster equivalent program (<1 s).
+`./evaluate OPTIMIZER` exits non-zero if the optimizer ever changes results (<2 min).
+**Correctness first: a wrong answer is worse than a slow one.**
+
+Numbers marked **[measured]** come from `bin/minivm` on this machine; **[est]** are estimates.
+
+---
+
+## 0. Facts we build on
+
+| Fact | Source |
+|---|---|
+| All 8 examples reproduce `examples/expected/*.json` rows and cycles exactly | [measured] |
+| `bin/minivm --schema examples/data.json` regenerates `examples/schema.json` exactly, so we can make schemas for our own data | [measured] |
+| One `bin/minivm` run takes ~42 ms (mostly process start); python start ~31 ms; 16 cores | [measured] |
+| Example cycles: q1 13766, q2 3617, q3 8562, q4 18442, q5 5518, q6 6338, q7 2596, q8 1524 | [measured] |
+| Hot spots: q4 GATHER 6672 + UPPER/RTRIM 5132; q1 CAT+LEFT 7124; q3/q6 self-HJOIN+GROUP ≈ 3200 each; redundant SORTs in q1/q4/q5 | [measured, prefix-diff profiler] |
+| Dead `DIV x,0` still errors, so dead-code removal can only remove errors (allowed: "if the input errors, anything goes") | [measured] |
+| NUM prints keep scale (`"2.0"` vs `"2.00"`); INT+NUM gives NUM; INT beyond ±2^63 prints as a string | [measured] |
+| HJOIN = NLJOIN results, ordered by (i, j); NULL keys never match unless `NULLEQ` | [measured] |
+| GROUP treats NULL = NULL, and `1` and `1.00` share a group; groups are numbered by first appearance | [measured] |
+| IDX drops FALSE and NULL; IF treats a NULL condition as FALSE; `IS` is never NULL | [measured] |
+| Type errors are per row, and never on empty or all-NULL columns, so whether a program fails depends on its data | [measured] |
+| Empty GALL: GCOUNT 0, GSUM/GMIN/GFIRST NULL; GATHER of a NULL index errors | [measured] |
+| VM limits: 20k instructions, 4M values per register, 64M total, ~300M work, 10k rows per table | docs |
+
+---
+
+## 1. Requirements → how we meet them → how we prove it
+
+| # | Requirement (one-pager) | How we meet it | Proof |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| R1 | `./optimize program.mini schema.json` prints a program | Python 3 stdlib only, no deps; on any internal failure it prints the input unchanged | runner checks exit 0 and that `bin/minivm` parses the output |
+| R2 | Same results on any data satisfying the schema constraints | rewrites are justified only by semantics plus **constraints** (never stats); each rewrite has a written safety rule (§3) | evaluator over many generated datasets (§4) plus mutant-optimizer recall test |
+| R3 | Order doesn't matter unless the program specifies it | the optimizer always **preserves exact order** (strictest); the evaluator compares order only when the original specifies it (§2 Q1) | rubric O2 |
+| R4 | Numbers within 1e-6 relative | the optimizer avoids changing arithmetic, so values are usually bit-identical; the comparator uses a relative tolerance | rubric O3 |
+| R5 | Input errors → anything goes | the evaluator skips (program, data) pairs where the original errors | rubric O5 |
+| R6 | ≤1 s per program | linear-ish passes and a hard internal deadline (0.8 s) that falls back to the best result so far | runner times every call; p100 reported |
+| R7 | Fewer cycles, "noticeably faster" on unseen programs | general passes, not example-specific hacks; measured on held-out generated programs | perf report: speedup per program and geomean |
+| R8 | `./evaluate OPTIMIZER` exits 0 or non-zero; any optimizer with the same CLI | black-box: calls `OPTIMIZER prog schema` as a subprocess | identity optimizer → exit 0; mutant optimizers → non-zero |
+| R9 | Evaluator ≤2 min, may use `bin/minivm` | parallel pool (16 workers), 100 s wall-clock budget, deterministic seed | runner reports wall time |
+| R10 | Submission: code, prompts, logs, raw sessions, git history, no binaries, models and spend, "few days" plan | §10 | checklist before sending |
+
+---
+
+## 2. Open questions → default we use
+
+| # | Question | Default |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q1 | When does the program "specify" order? | Order is specified iff the row positions reaching `OUT` come from a `SORT`/`TOPK` and no later reordering (join/group) loses that order. Otherwise compare as multisets. The **optimizer** preserves order regardless. |
+| Q2 | Is `"2.0"` vs `"2.00"` a difference? Or INT `2` vs NUM `"2.0"`? | Numbers are compared by value (rule R4), so no. Type is not compared for numbers; STR/DATE/BOOL/NULL must match exactly. |
+| Q3 | Does a NULL equal a NULL in output comparison? | Yes. NULL vs non-NULL is a defect. |
+| Q4 | Is a different `ncols` or column order a defect? | Yes, always. |
+| Q5 | Optimized program errors, times out (>1 s) or hits a VM limit where the original succeeds? | Defect. |
+| Q6 | Optimizer crashes or prints garbage? | Defect (counted as "optimizer failure"). |
+| Q7 | Can we rely on `stats`? | Only for **cost choices** (HJOIN vs NLJOIN, which side to filter), never for correctness. Stats describe one dataset. |
+| Q8 | "Faster" measured how? | `cycles` reported by `bin/minivm`, on the example data and on generated data. |
+| Q9 | Can the optimizer run `bin/minivm`? | Not required, and we don't depend on it (the grader may not provide it to `optimize`). |
+| Q10 | Hidden programs: same shape as the examples? | Assume "legacy" SQL-ish plans: wide GATHER fan-outs, late filters, self-joins, redundant sorts, dead columns. |
+
+---
+
+## 3. Optimizer design
+
+**Pipeline:** parse → SSA IR (rename registers, def-use, value metadata: source table, row domain, type, nullability, sortedness) → passes to a fixpoint → cost check → emit.
+**Global guard:** if the optimized program's estimated cost ≥ the original's, or any pass throws, emit the original.
+
+### Rewrites ranked by (gain ÷ risk)
+
+| Rank | Rewrite | Gain [est] | Risk | Safety rule |
+|---|---|---|---|---|
+| 1 | **Dead-code elimination** (unused LOAD/GATHER/string ops; e.g. q7 `upper r14`, `dow r15`) | 5–25% | very low | Removing instructions only removes possible errors. Never remove `OUT`. |
+| 2 | **GATHER composition and sinking**: `GATHER(GATHER(s,i),j)` → `GATHER(s, GATHER(i,j))`; carry *index vectors* through joins and filters and gather payload columns only at the end, only for columns that reach `OUT` | 20–50% on wide plans (q1, q4, q5) | low | Identity of index composition. Indices are in range by construction (same as the original). |
+| 3 | **CSE**: duplicate LOADs and identical ops (q2 loads `l_disc` twice) | small | very low | Pure ops are deterministic. |
+| 4 | **Late materialization of element-wise ops**: compute `f(x)` after filters (`f(GATHER(x,i)) = GATHER(f(x),i)`), only on surviving rows | 20–40% (q1, q4, q7 string ops) | low | Evaluating on a *subset* of rows can only remove errors. **Never** move an op onto rows it wasn't evaluated on, and never onto a join-expanded domain larger than the original (cost check). |
+| 5 | **Constraint rewrites**: `SORT` keys = prefix of `sorted` ASC NF on an unpermuted table → identity; predicate always TRUE by `not_null` + `check` (q1 `GE l_qty,1`) → drop it; `key` uniqueness ⇒ joins are at most 1:1, so a GROUP on key cols is identity | 5–15% | low-med | Uses only declared constraints. Must also prove the column isn't permuted or filtered in between (sortedness survives IDX/GATHER-by-ascending-index only). |
+| 6 | **Filter pushdown below joins** (q4 filters after 4 joins) | 30–70% on q4-like plans | med | Filtering the left side with IDX preserves the (i, j) output order. Applies only when the predicate depends on one side. Cast-free: same ops, fewer rows. |
+| 7 | **Sort reduction**: `SORT` + slice-first-n → `TOPK`; sort after filters, not before; drop sorts whose order is later destroyed | 5–20% | med | TOPK = first n of the same stable SORT (doc). Ties are preserved because both are stable. |
+| 8 | **Join algorithm choice** HJOIN ↔ NLJOIN by cost model | small-med | very low | Identical results by spec; only cost differs. Stats are fine here. |
+| 9 | **Idiom rewrites** (pattern-matched, each behind a precise matcher): self-join + GROUP + GMAX → `GROUP/GMAX/GID/GATHER` (q6); "count of better rows" top-n per group → sort + rank (q3); join-then-dedup semi-join → dedup the right side first (q5) | 2–10× on those programs | **high** | Must match NULL semantics exactly: HJOIN drops NULL keys but GROUP keeps them, so filter out NULL keys explicitly; GMAX "first of equals" printing scale. Only enabled after the evaluator passes with 0 defects on targeted stress data. |
+
+**Rule: never trade correctness for cycles.** Each rewrite ships with a unit test (before/after program pair) plus targeted adversarial data.
+
+**VM-limit safety:** no rewrite may increase any register's maximum length or the total HJOIN/GROUP input (work limit). This is checked by the cost estimator.
+
+---
+
+## 4. Evaluator design (`./evaluate OPTIMIZER`)
+
+1. **Corpus (seeded, deterministic).**
+   - The 8 example programs.
+   - **Generated programs**: a template generator over the example schema that emits legacy-style plans: scan/filter/project, 1–4 joins along `refs`, group-aggregate, sort/topk/slice, string transforms (NULLIFE/RTRIM/UPPER/CAT/LEFT), dates, arithmetic, with deliberate bloat (wide fan-outs, late filters, dead columns, self-joins).
+   - **Mutants of the examples** (swap predicates or literals, change sort keys or directions).
+   - Programs are type-correct by construction, and are kept only if the original runs without error on at least one dataset.
+2. **Data (per schema).** A constraint-respecting generator: unique `key`s, `refs` drawn from the parent key (plus NULLs where nullable), `check` bounds including boundaries, `sorted` order, `not_null`. It is adversarial: NULLs, ties, `'Open '`/`'OPEN'`/`'open'`, empty strings, empty tables, 1-row tables, duplicate join keys, NUM scale variants (`1.0` vs `1.00`), boundary dates. Every dataset is validated by `bin/minivm --schema` (it checks constraints), and its schema file is produced the same way.
+3. **Run.** For each program: call `OPTIMIZER prog.mini schema.json` once (1 s timeout). Then run the original and optimized programs on K datasets (K≈8–12).
+4. **Compare** with the rubric in §5. Skip pairs where the original errors.
+5. **Report** defects with a minimal repro (program, optimized program, dataset, diff) saved to `eval_out/`. Exit 1 if there are any defects (or optimizer failures), else 0.
+6. **Budget** [est from measurements]: ~60 programs × (1 optimizer call + 2×10 VM runs) ≈ 1,300 runs × 42 ms ÷ 16 workers ≈ 4–8 s, plus optimizer time ≤ 60 s worst case in parallel. A hard watchdog at 100 s stops new work, prints the report, and exits non-zero if any defect was found so far.
+
+**Evaluator self-test (meta-eval):**
+- The identity optimizer (`cat`) must give 0 false alarms.
+- A set of **known-bad mutant optimizers** must each be caught: drop a filter, flip `ASC`/`DESC`, drop `NULLEQ`, drop a tie-break sort key, replace `IS` with `EQ`, reorder unsorted output only (must *not* be flagged when order isn't specified), change NUM rounding, introduce DIV-by-zero on filtered rows.
+- Recall = caught ÷ mutants. Target 100%.
+
+---
+
+## 5. Eval rubric
+
+**Outputs (per program × dataset):**
+- **O1** `ncols` equal, and the optimized run succeeds whenever the original succeeds.
+- **O2** Rows equal as a list if order is specified (Q1), else as a multiset (sort both by canonical key).
+- **O3** Numbers (INT or NUM, parsed exactly via `Decimal`) satisfy `|a−b| ≤ 1e-6·max(|a|,|b|)` (`a==b` for 0). STR/DATE/BOOL are exact; NULL = NULL.
+- **O4** The optimized program doesn't exceed VM limits or the 1 s optimizer limit.
+- **O5** If the original errors, the pair is skipped (not a defect).
+
+**Programs (performance):**
+- **P1** Cycles of optimized vs original per dataset: speedup ratio, plus a geomean over the corpus.
+- **P2** Instruction count before and after.
+- **P3** Peak register size and VM-limit headroom (never worse than the original).
+- **P4** Optimizer wall time (max must be < 1 s).
+- **P5** Regression flag: optimized cycles > original cycles on any dataset (allowed but reported; target zero).
+
+**Evaluator:**
+- **E1** Defects caught on mutant optimizers (recall, target 100%).
+- **E2** False alarms on the identity and correct optimizers (target 0).
+- **E3** Wall time (< 120 s; target < 60 s).
+- **E4** Determinism: the same seed gives the same verdict.
+
+---
+
+## 6. Stopping criteria for the build ↔ evaluate loop
+
+- **Success, stop:** 0 defects on the full eval with 3 different seeds; E1 = 100% and E2 = 0; geomean speedup ≥ 2× on examples [target est]; all optimizer calls < 1 s; evaluator < 2 min.
+- **No more improvement, stop that rewrite:** two consecutive iterations where geomean cycles improve by < 3%, or a rewrite's gain doesn't justify its risk. Move to the next rank, or stop.
+- **Budget used up, stop:** user time hits 2h45m, or the AI token budget reaches its cap (we track per session). Ship the last green commit.
+- **Ask the user when:** a rewrite depends on an open-question interpretation (Q1/Q2); a defect can't be explained within 2 attempts; a fix requires dropping a rewrite that gives > 20% of the gain; or any destructive git action (history rewrite for binaries, §10).
+- **Hard rule:** any defect is a red build. Revert or disable the rewrite before doing anything else.
+
+---
+
+## 7. Failure modes
+
+| Failure | Detection | Fix / prevention |
+|---|---|---|
+| Rewrite changes NULL semantics (HJOIN vs GROUP NULL keys) | targeted NULL-heavy data; mutant tests | explicit NULL filters in idiom rewrites; per-rewrite unit tests |
+| Rewrite introduces an error (DIV by 0, type error, out-of-range GATHER, DATEADD overflow) on rows the original skipped | O1 failures on adversarial data | the "subset of evaluated rows" rule (§3 #4) |
+| Lost tie order (unstable reorder) | O2 on tie-heavy data | always preserve order; only stable sorts and IDX/ascending composition |
+| Relying on stats instead of constraints | data generator deliberately violates stats (different ranges, distincts) | lint: the correctness path never reads `stats` |
+| Order-insensitive comparison hides a real defect, or order-sensitive gives false alarms | meta-eval with "reorder-only" mutant | Q1 detector unit-tested on all 8 examples |
+| NUM scale or rounding differences | O3 tolerance; DIV/MUL results compared via Decimal | don't reassociate arithmetic |
+| Optimized program blows VM limits (join expansion) | O4; P3 report | cost check: never increase peak register size or HJOIN/GROUP input |
+| Optimizer > 1 s on big programs (20k instructions) | timing in runner; a stress program with 20k lines | linear passes, iteration cap, 0.8 s deadline → fall back |
+| Evaluator > 2 min | wall-time report | watchdog at 100 s; parallel pool; cap on corpus size |
+| Generator makes invalid data | `bin/minivm --schema` rejects it | regenerate; count rejections in the report |
+| Overfitting to 8 examples | held-out generated programs scored separately | rewrites must be general passes; idioms gated |
+
+---
+
+## 8. Test suite and runners
+
+**Easy** (one pass each): DCE-only, CSE-only, SORT on a sorted table, always-true predicate, HJOIN↔NLJOIN choice, empty tables, all-NULL columns.
+**Medium:** examples q7, q8, q2; filter pushdown through one join; SORT+SLICE→TOPK; string op after filter.
+**Complex:** q1, q4, q5 (multi-join fan-outs); q3, q6 (self-join idioms); generated 3–4-join plans; 20k-instruction stress program for timing.
+
+Layout:
+```
+tests/cases/{easy,medium,complex}/NAME.mini     # + optional NAME.expect_opt (golden)
+tests/data/                                     # generated datasets + schemas
+tools/gen_data.py  tools/gen_prog.py  tools/compare.py  tools/profile.py
+run_tests.sh   # unit + suite: each case × each dataset, original vs optimized
+run_eval.sh    # ./evaluate ./optimize + meta-eval (identity + mutants)
+```
+Every run prints a report: `pass/fail per case | cycles orig→opt (×speedup) | instructions | max opt time | defects | geomean`. It also appends a JSON line to `notes/runs.jsonl` for trend tracking.
+
+---
+
+## 9. Progression log
+
+`notes/PROGRESSION_LOG.md` gets one entry per iteration: *date · approach tried · result (defects, geomean, timing) · what we learned · keep/revert*. The first entries are the facts in §0 and the probe results.
+
+---
+
+## 10. Timeline (2–3 h of user time)
+
+| Time | Work | Checkpoint |
+|---|---|---|
+| 0:00–0:20 | Scaffolding: parser/IR/emitter (round-trip = identity), compare.py, data generator validated by `--schema` | CP1: identity optimizer round-trips all examples, eval = 0 defects |
+| 0:20–0:50 | Evaluator v1: corpus = examples + datasets, parallel, rubric, report; meta-eval mutants | CP2: identity 0 false alarms, mutants caught |
+| 0:50–1:40 | Optimizer passes 1–5 (DCE, GATHER sinking, CSE, late materialization, constraint rewrites), iterating with the eval | CP3: 0 defects, geomean ≥ 1.5× [target] |
+| 1:40–2:15 | Passes 6–8 (filter pushdown, sort reduction, join choice); program generator for held-out tests | CP4: 0 defects on 3 seeds |
+| 2:15–2:40 | Idioms (#9) only if green; otherwise skip | CP5 |
+| 2:40–3:00 | Submission packaging (§11) | done |
+
+**Cut order if late:** idiom rewrites (#9) → program generator (keep examples + mutants) → join choice → sort reduction. **Never cut:** the evaluator, meta-eval, or the fall-back-to-original guard.
+
+---
+
+## 11. Submission
+
+- **Git history from the start:** commit at every checkpoint, with messages that reference the progression log.
+- **No binaries — action needed.** The initial commit (already pushed to `sfc-gh-skodi/assessment_fde`) contains `minivm-takehome/bin/minivm-*` (5 binaries). We need to (a) add `minivm-takehome/bin/` to `.gitignore` and `git rm --cached` it, and (b) **purge it from history** (rewrite plus force-push). This needs explicit approval. The alternative is a fresh repo seeded without `bin/`. Also exclude the `bin/minivm` launcher script, since the one-pager names it explicitly.
+- **Raw AI session files:** copy the Cortex Code session files (`~/.snowflake/cortex/conversations/<project>/<session>.json` and `.history.jsonl`) into `ai-sessions/` unmodified.

+- **"Few days, unlimited compute" plan:**
+  - an equality-saturation (e-graph) optimizer with a calibrated cost model;
+  - a cost-based join-order search using stats;
+  - translation validation: an SMT or bounded-model check that each rewrite is equivalent under the constraints;
+  - a coverage-guided fuzzer for the evaluator, with test-case shrinking;
+  - a much larger generated corpus, run in parallel in the cloud;
+  - learning which idioms legacy programs use from a big corpus.

+'m starting a take-home assignment. Before we write any code, help me build a solid plan.
+
+## 1. Read first
+
+Problem statement: `../minivm-onepager.pdf`
+Assessment package: this folder. Start with `docs/README.md`, then read all of `docs/`, `examples/` and `bin/minivm`.
+Use `bin/minivm` to check facts. Don't guess.
+
+
+## 2. The goal
+
+Build `./optimize program.mini schema.json`. It prints a faster program that gives the same results on any data that fits the schema's constraints, in under 1 second.
+Build `./evaluate OPTIMIZER`. It exits non-zero if the optimizer ever changes results, and finishes in under 2 minutes.
+
+
+## 3. What the plan must cover
+
+**Requirements:** every rule in the one-pager, how we meet it, and how we prove it.
+**Open questions:** anything the one-pager leaves unclear, with the default we'll use for each.
+**Optimizer design:** the rewrites, ranked by speed gain vs. risk, plus the safety rules that keep results identical.
+**Evaluator design:** how it generates test programs and data, how it compares results, and how it stays within 2 minutes.
+**Eval rubric:** clear rules for comparing an original program with its optimized version:
+outputs: same rows, same order where it matters, numbers within 1e-6
+the programs themselves: cycles, instruction count, VM limits
+the evaluator: defects caught, false alarms
+
+**Stopping criteria:** when the build-and-test(orchestrator-evaluator) loop should stop. Cover success, no more improvement, budget used up, and when to ask me.
+**Failure modes:** what could go wrong, how we'd detect it, and how we'd fix or prevent it.
+**Test suite:** easy, medium and complex cases, so we can iterate quickly.
+**Runners:** scripts that run the test suite and print an eval and performance report at the end of every run.
+**Progression log:** a document that records every approach we try, what we learned, and the key takeaways.
+**Timeline:** fits 2/3 hours of my time, with checkpoints and what to cut if we run late.
+**Submission:**
+git history from the start
+no binaries
+raw AI session files
+models and token spend
+a "few days, unlimited compute" plan
+
+
+
+## 4. How to work
+
+Use subagents for research where it helps.
+Measure numbers with `bin/minivm`, or mark them as estimates.
+Write the plan to `BUILD_PLAN.md`. Don't build the optimizer or evaluator yet.
+Correctness comes first. A wrong answer is worse than a slow one.
+Feel free to save temp artifacts in notes folder 
+Do no save any binary files in the repo
+Do no overcomplicate, go step by step and save the relevant/important outputs

+# Example programs: what they compute, cost, waste
+
+How these were measured: `bin/minivm examples/programs/qNNNN.mini examples/data.json`. Every example matched `examples/expected/` rows and cycles exactly. Per-opcode cycles come from running the program cut off after each instruction and taking the difference in cycles.
+"Waste" is my reading of each program and has not been rewritten and measured yet. Items marked *(needs proof)* depend on a reasoning step that the evaluator must still confirm.
+
+| Program | Rows out | Instrs | Cycles | Biggest cost |
+|---|---|---|---|---|
+| q0001 | 4 | 89 | 13,766 | CAT+LEFT 7,124 (52%) |
+| q0002 | 7 | 41 | 3,617 | HJOIN 784, GROUP 670 |
+| q0003 | 12 | 32 | 8,562 | GROUP 1,888, HJOIN 1,346 |
+| q0004 | 1 | 121 | 18,442 | GATHER 6,672 (36%), UPPER 3,120 |
+| q0005 | 2 | 79 | 5,518 | GATHER 1,666, HJOIN 664 |
+| q0006 | 30 | 34 | 6,338 | GROUP 1,888, HJOIN 1,346 |
+| q0007 | 3 | 49 | 2,596 | CAT 650, LEFT 502 |
+| q0008 | 3 | 31 | 1,524 | RTRIM/UPPER 540 |
+
+## q0001: line export for big 2024 orders
+**Computes:** lines of orders dated 2024 with `o_amt > 750`, giving order, line number, product, and a 40-character "product / category" label, in (order, line) order.
+**Waste:**
+- The label (NVL, CAT, CAT, NULLIFE, LEFT) is built for every line before the orders filter, which keeps only 4 rows. CAT+LEFT cost 7,124 cycles.
+- `SORT r7` on (l_order, l_no) does nothing, because `lines` is declared `sorted` on exactly those columns (860 cycles plus 6 GATHERs).
+- 14 columns are gathered through 3 joins, but only 4 are output (GATHER 2,574).
+- `GE l_qty, 1` is always TRUE, because `l_qty` is `not_null` with check [1, 500]. The IDX+GATHER filter after it is pointless.
+- The orders filter comes last instead of first.
+
+## q0002: discounted-line rank within product
+**Computes:** for each discounted line of a 2023 order with `o_amt > 300`, how many discounted lines of the same product (across all orders) sold fewer units. Ordered by (order, line).
+**Waste:**
+- `l_disc` is loaded twice, and the second load (`t5`) is never used.
+- The orders filter is applied after the self-join, group and sum. Only the outer rows can be filtered early, because the count must still range over all discounted lines.
+- The quadratic self-HJOIN on `l_prod` followed by GROUP/GSUM (HJOIN 550, GROUP 670, GSUM 226) could become sort- or group-based counting.
+- The final SORT may be an identity, because the rows already follow lines order *(needs proof)*.
+
+## q0003: top 4 orders per status
+**Computes:** per normalized status (NULLIFE → RTRIM → UPPER), the 4 orders with the highest `o_id`, ordered by id. Outputs id, raw status, id. Orders with an empty or NULL status drop out, because HJOIN never matches NULL keys.
+**Waste:**
+- A quadratic self-HJOIN on status, then GROUP and GSUM over all pairs, counts the "better" rows for each order (GROUP 1,888, HJOIN 1,346). A sort by (status, id DESC) plus a per-group rank is linear-ish. The rewrite must keep dropping NULL keys.
+- Duplicate GATHERs: `r11` = `r9` and `r12` = `r10`.
+- Six element-wise ops run on the pair domain, about 318 cycles each.
+- `o_cust` is loaded and gathered but never output.
+
+## q0004: Kitchen revenue for central customers in 2023
+**Computes:** one row giving the sum of `qty*price*(1-disc)` and the line count, for Kitchen-category products sold to central-region customers in 2023.
+**Waste:**
+- It joins all 4 tables, carries every column (GATHER 6,672), and only filters at the end.
+- The string cleanup (NULLIFE/RTRIM/UPPER on category and region) runs on joined rows (UPPER+RTRIM about 5,100 cycles) instead of on the 4 categories and 12 customers.
+- `UPPER t82` is dead (1,108 cycles).
+- `SORT t83` (1,126 cycles) is useless before GALL/GSUM/GCOUNT. Per the docs, GSUM's result doesn't depend on row order.
+- The categories join uses NLJOIN.
+
+## q0005: card-paid revenue per category
+**Computes:** revenue per category name over lines of orders with at least one card payment, ordered by category id.
+**Waste:**
+- It joins card payments onto lines, then de-duplicates with SORT + GROUP + GFIRST. That is a semi-join, so the payments could be de-duplicated per order first.
+- `SORT r29` on (l_order, l_no) is probably an identity, because HJOIN output is ordered by line index and `lines` is sorted *(needs proof)*.
+- 9 to 11 columns are gathered through each step, but only category and revenue reach `OUT`.
+- The product and category joins use NLJOIN.
+
+## q0006: max quantity within each order's status
+**Computes:** for orders dated on or after 2023-01-01, the id, amount, and the largest `o_qty` among orders with the same normalized status, ordered by id. NULL or empty status rows drop out (HJOIN).
+**Waste:**
+- A quadratic self-HJOIN, then GROUP and GMAX (GROUP 1,888, HJOIN 1,346, GMAX 632), where GROUP + GMAX + GID + GATHER is linear. The rewrite must filter out NULL keys explicitly, because GROUP treats NULL = NULL.
+- `IOTA r28` with `GATHER r29` is an identity copy.
+- The date and status columns are gathered but unused. The final SORT by id is needed, because `orders` isn't declared sorted.
+
+## q0007: top 5 July-2023 orders by amount
+**Computes:** the 5 largest-amount orders dated July 2023, with ties broken by id. Outputs id, amount, and a 40-character "note status" label.
+**Waste:**
+- The label (NVL, CAT, LEFT) is built for all 30 orders, but only 5 are output (CAT 650, LEFT 502).
+- `UPPER r14` and `DOW r15` are dead.
+- It SORTs every order before filtering, where filtering first and then using TOPK 5 would do.
+- 8 columns are gathered, but 3 are output.
+
+## q0008: count and quantity per status
+**Computes:** order count and total `o_qty` per normalized status, for orders with `o_qty >= 5`, biggest count first, then by name.
+**Waste:**
+- 5 of the 7 loads are never used (only status and qty are), and all 7 are gathered after the filter.
+- Otherwise it's already tight, so expect only small gains.
+
+## Cross-cutting patterns (feed BUILD_PLAN §3)
+- Dead loads, gathers and ops appear in every example except q0002.
+- Every column is carried through every join or filter: q0001, q0004, q0005, q0007, q0008.
+- Filters come late: they run after joins, after string building, or after sorting (q0001, q0002, q0004, q0007).
+- Sorts are redundant: on a declared-`sorted` table (q0001, probably q0005), before an order-independent aggregate (q0004), or before a filter where TOPK would do (q0007).
+- Quadratic self-joins compute per-group statistics (q0002, q0003, q0006); this is the highest gain but also the highest NULL-semantics risk.
+- NLJOIN is used where HJOIN may be cheaper (q0004, q0005); the cost depends on input sizes.

@@ -0,0 +1,84 @@
+# Research: evaluating a MiniVM optimizer
+
+Scratch experiments: `/tmp/research_eval/`. "Verified" = I ran it on `bin/minivm`.
+
+## Findings
+
+**Industry practice (what maps to this problem)**
+- **Differential testing** (McKeeman '98; Csmith, PLDI'11): run the same input through two implementations and compare. For us: original vs optimized on the same VM. Csmith's lesson: generated programs must avoid UB (for us, programs that error) and print enough output for differences to show up. Programs that error or give empty output tell us nothing.
+- **Translation validation** (Pnueli '98; CompCert validates register allocation after the fact; Alive2 checks that LLVM IR rewrites are refinements, using SMT). Validate each optimizer *output*, never trust the optimizer. A full SMT check is out of scope in 2 min. Our version is a test-based check per program.
+- **STOKE** (superoptimizer): cheap tests filter candidates, then a formal check runs, and each counterexample becomes a new test (CEGIS loop). Takeaway: keep a growing set of "killer" datasets and run them first.
+- **Metamorphic testing**: EMI (PLDI'14) changes dead code under fixed inputs. SQLancer: **TLP** (a query's result = union of its results under `p`, `NOT p`, `p IS NULL`), **NoREC** (compare against a form the optimizer can't optimize), **PQS** (pick a row and check it appears in the result). For us: the original program *is* the non-optimizing reference. TLP-style 3-way NULL partitions are a good model for adversarial data.
+- **Test-case reduction**: C-Reduce / delta debugging, guided by an "interestingness" predicate. Shrink the dataset rows (children before parents, to keep `refs` valid) and then the program lines, while the diff still reproduces.
+- **Mutation testing of the oracle**: plant known faults in the optimizer and measure the kill rate. Also confirm that correct rewrites are *not* killed, so false positives are measured too.
+
+**Verified VM facts that matter for the evaluator**
+- The `bin/minivm` wrapper runs `xattr` on every call: **43 ms serial, about 86 runs/s on 16 workers**. Calling `bin/minivm-macos-aarch64` directly takes **8.8 ms, about 420 runs/s**. The parallel speedup is only about 5x, likely due to process-spawn overhead.
+- Output is deterministic: md5 matched over 3 runs. A 10k×10k `NLJOIN` producing a 2M-row output takes 425 ms and prints 13 MB of JSON (`HJOIN`: 188 ms).
+- `--schema` rejects data that breaks a constraint (duplicate key, out-of-order `sorted`, INT outside 32 bits) with exit 1. Use it as the data-generator gate. `sorted` is checked as `ASC NF`, so NULLs come first.
+- NUM keeps its scale: `GMIN` over `1.0`/`1.00` returns whichever comes first. `GSUM` scale = the largest scale among its inputs (`"2.00"`). `ADD x, 0.000` keeps `"1.0"`. `DIV 3,1` prints `"3"`, a NUM string with no point, so the type is not visible in the output.
+- An error gives exit 1 and nothing on stdout. Errors depend on the data: `DIV` by zero only on rows that are actually used.
+- Empty table: `GALL`+`GCOUNT` gives 1 row `[0,null,null]`. `GROUP` gives 0 rows. `UPPER('straße')`→`STRASSE`, so `LEFT∘UPPER ≠ UPPER∘LEFT`. `DATEADD 9999-12-31,1` is an error.
+- The schema's `stats` describe one dataset only. The optimizer must be correct for **any** data that meets the constraints.
+
+## Risks
+
+- **False alarm on order.** A program "specifies order" only if its OUT rows come, through GATHER, IDX or SLICE, from a `SORT`/`TOPK`. Rows tied on the sort keys keep their *input* order, and a correct join reorder can legally change that order. Comparing tied rows strictly would flag correct optimizers.
+- **Ties at a cut-off** (`SORT`+`SLICE`, `TOPK`, or `GFIRST` after a non-total sort for de-dup): a correct optimizer can return a different tied row. This is ambiguous in the spec. Choose lenient handling and document it.
+- **Type is not visible in output.** STR `"2.00"` and NUM `"2.00"` print the same. Numeric tolerance on strings that look like numbers can hide a real STR change. INT `3` vs NUM `"3"` must count as equal.
+- **Relative-only tolerance near zero.** `0` vs `1e-28`, e.g. a DIV-rounding residue, fails a strict `|a-b| ≤ 1e-6·max`. This is a spec-conformant flag, but it may look harsh.
+- **Optimizer timing under load.** Running 16 VMs in parallel inflates the optimizer's wall time, which can produce a false ">1 s" failure.
+- **Missed bugs (the evaluator wrongly passes a bad optimizer):**
+  - The data generator only produces data that looks like the stats.
+  - No NULLs in nullable `refs`/`check` columns.
+  - No duplicates in non-key join columns.
+  - No empty or 1-row tables.
+  - Programs whose output is always empty.
+- **Budget:** a slow optimizer (up to 1 s × N programs) dominates the run time, not the VM. Large join outputs make JSON parsing in Python the bottleneck.
+
+## Recommendations
+
+**Proving it WRONG within 2 min**
+- Resolve the native binary the same way the wrapper does, with a fallback to `bin/minivm`. That gives about 420 runs/s, so 90 s ≈ 35k VM runs. Timeouts: 5 s per VM run, 2 s per optimizer call (flag at more than 1 s).
+- **Corpus:** the 8 examples, their mutants, and type-inferred generated programs. Keep a program only if the original succeeds on at least 1 dataset and gives non-empty output on at least 1. Order the work as: killer datasets first, then examples, then generated programs (STOKE-style).
+- **Data:** run `--schema` on every generated dataset and require its columns and constraints to equal the target schema, ignoring stats. Give the optimizer the schema of dataset D0, then test it on D1..Dk, whose stats deliberately differ (nulls where D0 had none, values beyond D0's min/max, duplicates where D0 was distinct).
+- **Adversarial values:**
+  - NULL in every nullable column, including nullable FKs and `check` columns.
+  - Ties in sort keys; duplicate join keys.
+  - `''` vs NULL; `'Open '`/`'OPEN'`/`'open'`; `ß`/multibyte strings.
+  - `1.0`/`1.00`; zeros in divisor-like columns.
+  - Values exactly on the `check` bounds; dates `0001-01-01`, `9999-12-31`, Feb 29.
+  - Empty, 1-row and max-fan-out tables.
+- Call the optimizer once per program, all calls in parallel, and cache the output. Fan out VM runs on 16 workers. **Exit early** on the first confirmed defect: re-run once to rule out flakiness, save the repro to `eval_out/`, exit 1. A watchdog at about 100 s stops new work and exits with the verdict so far.
+- If time remains, shrink the repro with delta debugging (≤10 s cap).
+
+**Not flagging a CORRECT optimizer**
+- Skip a (program, dataset) pair when the original errors or hits a VM limit. It is a defect when the original succeeds and the optimized program errors, fails to parse, times out, or `ncols` differs.
+- **Order:** do a static dataflow check for whether order is specified.
+  - If order is unspecified, compare as multisets: sort both sides by a canonical key.
+  - If order is specified, compare row by row, but canonical-sort *inside* runs of rows tied on the sort keys. If the keys are not in OUT, recover them by running an instrumented original with the sort keys appended to OUT.
+  - Treat a diff at a TOPK/SLICE tie boundary as ambiguous: warn, don't fail.
+- **Values:**
+  - Exact match first.
+  - Both values numeric (JSON int, or a string matching `^-?\d+(\.\d+)?$`): compare as `Decimal` with `|a−b| ≤ 1e-6·max(|a|,|b|)`, so `2.0`=`2.00` and `3`=`"3"`.
+  - NULL only equals NULL. STR, DATE and BOOL must match exactly.
+  - Better: infer OUT column types statically so numeric tolerance is never applied to STR.
+- **Determinism:** fixed seed, sorted work lists, no wall-clock dependence in the verdict, only the deadline. Time the optimizer outside the VM fan-out, or re-time it alone before flagging ">1 s".
+- **Meta-eval:** `cat` (identity) and known-correct rewrites must give 0 alarms. Correct rewrites to test:
+  - reorder unsorted output
+  - change NUM scale
+  - TOPK ↔ SORT+SLICE
+  - INT→NUM with the same value
+  - swap the HJOIN/NLJOIN sides with a re-sort
+
+  Bad mutants must give 100% kill:
+  - drop a filter
+  - ASC↔DESC, NF↔NL
+  - drop NULLEQ; IS→EQ
+  - `NOT GT` → `LE` (wrong under NULL)
+  - move DIV before the filter
+  - swap LEFT and UPPER
+  - remove an inner join on a nullable FK
+  - GALL→GROUP (wrong on an empty table)
+  - TOPK n±1
+  - assume stats are constraints (drop an ISNULL check because nulls=0)
