+# Optimizer research: what pays, what bites, in what order
+
+Sources: one-pager, `docs/*`, `notes/examples.md`, BUILD_PLAN §3. **[M]** = measured with `bin/minivm` on `examples/data.json`
+(scratch files in `/tmp/research_opt/`); **[E]** = estimate from the cost table.
+
+## Findings (ranked by cycles saved)
+1. **Late materialization + filter-first (move filters up, run element-wise ops only on surviving rows).** Cost is per element plus
+   per string byte, so string ops (CAT/LEFT/UPPER/RTRIM) on wide row sets dominate. q7: run the filter, then TOPK 5, then build the label
+   on 3 rows: **2596 → 637 (4.1x) [M]**, identical rows. q1 CAT+LEFT is 7,124 of 13,766 cycles, but the filter keeps only 4 rows, so most of that goes away **[E]**.
+   q4 runs UPPER/RTRIM (about 5,100) on joined rows instead of on 4 categories and 12 customers **[E: ~25%]**.
+2. **Replace a quadratic self-join with a GROUP aggregate.** `HJOIN k,k` costs 2 per output pair (n² for each group) and then GROUPs the pairs.
+   q6 becomes `GROUP key → GMAX → GID → GATHER` with an explicit `NOT ISNULL(key)` filter: **6338 → 2091 (3.0x) [M]**, identical rows.
+   q3 and q2 (count of better rows, top-n per group) have the same shape: GROUP+HJOIN ≈ 3,200 of 8,562 in q3 **[M profile]**, about 2–3x **[E]**.
+3. **Dead-code elimination and GATHER sinking.** Every GATHER costs 1 per value, and legacy plans re-gather 7–14 columns after every join
+   or filter. q4 GATHER is 6,672 (36%) **[M profile]**. Carry index vectors, compose them (`GATHER(GATHER(s,i),j)` → `GATHER(s,GATHER(i,j))`),
+   and gather only columns that reach OUT: 20–50% on q1/q4/q5 **[E]**. Dead ops: q4 `UPPER t82` 1,108 **[M]**; q7 UPPER and DOW.
+4. **Push filters below joins.** HJOIN costs (n+m)·(6+bytes) + 2·pairs. Filtering a side first shrinks both terms and every
+   downstream GATHER. q4 joins 4 tables and filters last: 30–70% **[E]**.
+5. **Sort reduction.** SORT costs 2/row plus about n·log n comparisons at (2+bytes) each. Options: SORT+filter+SLICE → filter+TOPK (part of q7's 4.1x);
+   SORT on a `sorted` table → identity (q1, 860 + 6 GATHERs **[M profile]**); SORT before GSUM/GCOUNT/GALL is dead (q4, 1,126) **[M profile]**.
+6. **Constraint rewrites.** Cheap but small: drop an always-TRUE predicate (`l_qty` not_null + check [1,500] → `GE l_qty,1`, q1).
+   Join elimination when the FK is `not_null` + `refs` to a `key` and no right-side columns are used, so each left row matches exactly once **[E: one HJOIN + its GATHERs]**.
+7. **Choose the join algorithm.** NLJOIN costs n·m·(1+bytes). That is cheaper than HJOIN only when one side is tiny
+   (INT keys: n·m < 6(n+m), e.g. m ≤ 5). So q4/q5 NLJOIN against 4 categories may already be optimal. Pick using stats. Small gain **[E]**.
+8. **CSE** (q2 loads `l_disc` twice; q3 `r11`=`r9`): tiny but free.
+
+## Risks (concrete traps)
+- **NULL keys differ between HJOIN and GROUP.** HJOIN never matches NULL (unless `NULLEQ`); GROUP makes one NULL group **[M: n1/n2 probes]**.
+  `''` is not NULL and HJOIN matches it **[M]**. Any join→group rewrite must add `ISNULL` filtering, or the reverse.
+- **Introducing errors.** `DIV x,d` after `IDX d≠0` succeeds. Moving it before the filter errors with *division by zero* **[M: n3 vs n4]**.
+  The same applies to IDIV/MOD, DATEADD range, overflow (ADD/MUL/NEG/GSUM totals), NUM×INT>2^96, type errors on mixed NVL/IF columns,
+  and comparisons of STR with INT. Filter pushdown below a join evaluates the predicate on *unmatched* rows the original never saw.
+- **Row order and ties.** OUT order is the contract when the program sorts. HJOIN orders by (i, j), so swapping sides changes the order.
+  GROUP numbers groups by first appearance, so reordering its input changes the group order, which GFIRST row is picked, and which "first of equals" GMIN/GMAX returns.
+  SORT is stable, so dropping a tie-break key or substituting an unstable order changes the rows. `sorted` holds only for the *stored* order: it
+  survives IDX/ascending GATHER and the left side of a join, **not** the right side of HJOIN, GROUP output, or a SORT on other keys.
+- **NUM scale printing.** `1.0`≠`1.00` as text. GMAX keeps the first-of-equals scale (`"1.0"`). GSUM uses the max scale in the group (`"2.00"`).
+  `MUL v,1.000` re-scales (`"1.0000"`), and DIV keeps the dividend's scale **[M: n1, n5]**. Algebraic identities (x*1, x+0.0) change the printed text.
+  They are equal by value (1e-6 rule), but a strict grader might compare the strings, so avoid them.
+- **Unicode.** `UPPER('straße')`='STRASSE' changes length, so UPPER does not commute with LEFT/SUBSTR/LEN, and string bytes ≠ characters.
+- **Misusing constraints.** customers/regions have **no key**, so joins there can fan out. `refs` allows NULL FKs (inner join drops them) unless the FK is also `not_null`.
+  `check` without `not_null` doesn't make a predicate TRUE (NULL → IDX drops the row). Stats describe one dataset, so never base correctness on them.
+- **VM limits.** 4M values per register, 64M total, about 300M work, where HJOIN/GROUP use it up faster than their cycles. A rewrite that grows any
+  intermediate (join reordering, an op moved onto a join-expanded domain) can make the optimized program fail where the original passed.
+  The 20k-instruction limit and the 1 s optimizer limit rule out super-linear passes.
+- **Dead code that errors** may be removed (if the original errors, anything goes), but never *add* an op that may error.
+
+## Recommendations
+1. **Order:** DCE+CSE → GATHER composition/sinking → late materialization of element-wise ops → sort reduction (TOPK, dead SORT,
+   `sorted` identity) → filter pushdown below joins → constraint rewrites → join choice → idioms (self-join→GROUP, semi-join dedup).
+2. **Global safety rule:** an op may be evaluated only on a **subset** of the rows it ran on originally (never a superset), in the same
+   relative order. Otherwise the rewrite must prove the op is *total*: typed by the schema, no DIV/IDIV/MOD/DATEADD, no possible overflow.
+3. **Per rewrite:**
+   - DCE/CSE: always safe. Keep OUT and its operand order.
+   - GATHER composition: safe (indices come from in-range positions).
+   - Filter pushdown: only predicates that use one side and are total. Use IDX on the left side, then re-gather. Never reorder join sides.
+   - TOPK: `SORT; IDX; SLICE n` → `IDX; TOPK n` with the same keys (stable), only when the filter doesn't depend on sort position.
+   - Drop SORT: only if it is followed by order-insensitive aggregates (GSUM/GCOUNT/GCOUNTV/GALL, *not* GFIRST/GMIN/GMAX/GID order),
+     or if the keys are a prefix of `sorted` (ASC NF) on an index proven ascending.
+   - Self-join→GROUP: match the exact pattern (HJOIN k,k without NULLEQ → GROUP on left index → aggregate). Add a `NOT ISNULL(k)` filter
+     and keep row order so GMAX "first of equals" and GROUP numbering stay the same.
+4. **Gate behind the evaluator:** idioms and join elimination. Enable each only after 0 defects on NULL-, tie-, scale- and empty-table-heavy
+   generated data. **Skip:** arithmetic re-association/simplification, join reordering, anything that uses stats for correctness.
+5. **Guard:** if the estimated cost is not lower, or any intermediate would be larger than the original's (VM limits), emit the input program unchanged.

+# Research: working model for the build ↔ check loop
+
+Sources: `minivm-onepager.pdf`, `minivm-takehome/docs/{README,running,cycles}.md`, `BUILD_PLAN.md`, `notes/INITIAL_PROMPT.md`.
+
+```
+ human (CP gates, ambiguity, destructive git)
+   ^ escalate                         | approve
+ ORCHESTRATOR -- task card --> BUILDER -- diff + commit --> fast tier (regressions+examples)
+   ^   |                                                      | green
+   |   +-- spec only --> RED-TEAM (mutants, adversarial data)  v
+   |                         |                      CHECKER: ./evaluate ./optimize (3 seeds)
+   +---- verdict + repro <---+---- REVIEWER (diff vs §3 safety rule) <----+
+         green: keep + log   red: git revert, repro -> tests/regressions/
+```
+
+## Findings
+- Grading is on process (git history, raw sessions, prompts, logs), so the loop itself is a deliverable: every role's prompt and verdict must land in the repo.
+- The oracle is free: `bin/minivm` on the *original* program is ground truth, so the evaluator never needs to reimplement semantics. That makes black-box independence possible.
+- Real defects depend on the data (per-row type errors, NULL keys in HJOIN vs GROUP, ties, NUM scale, stats ≠ constraints). Evaluator recall comes mostly from the **data generator**, not the comparer.
+- The grader will run `./evaluate` on *their* wrong optimizers. These are likely subtle and depend on the data, so our meta-eval mutants have to look like that, not like trivial breakage.
+- Two traps hurt precision: legal changes that look wrong (row reorder when order isn't specified, HJOIN↔NLJOIN, `2.0` vs `2.00`, whatever the optimizer does when the original errors) and timing flakiness (1 s limit under a 16-worker load).
+- `BUILD_PLAN.md` §4/§6 already defines the mutant list, identity check and stopping rules. What's missing: role separation, held-out mutants, a regression corpus, determinism checks, and per-iteration logging.
+
+## Risks
+- **Self-grading:** the same agent writes a rewrite and the test data that "proves" it. Its blind spots end up in both.
+- **Overfitting:** an optimizer tuned to q1–q8, or an evaluator tuned to the mutants its own author wrote. Both get 100% at home and fail on hidden cases.
+- **Shared code:** if the evaluator imports the optimizer's parser/IR, one bug hides itself on both sides.
+- **Flaky verdicts:** seed-dependent or load-dependent results. The evaluator exits 1 on a correct optimizer, and the grader sees a false alarm.
+- **History damage:** `git reset`/squash on red builds erases the process the graders want to read. Binaries in history (BUILD_PLAN §11) are still an open item.
+- **Budget overrun:** a full eval with 3 seeds and the meta-eval on every small edit burns both wall time and tokens.
+
+## Recommendations
+**Roles and context (who sees what)**
+- *Orchestrator* (main session): owns BUILD_PLAN, writes one **task card** per iteration (`notes/tasks/NN.md`: goal, rewrite rank, safety rule, done-definition). It doesn't write optimizer code.
+- *Builder* subagent: gets the task card, `optimizer/`, docs. Output: diff, unit test, one commit `opt: <pass> (task NN)`.
+- *Red-team* subagent: gets **only** the one-pager, docs, rubric §5 and the CLI. It never sees `optimizer/` or the builder's rationale. It writes mutant optimizers and adversarial data/programs under `evaluator/`.
+- *Checker* = the `./evaluate` script, not an LLM. Verdicts are numbers and repro files, not opinions.
+- *Reviewer* subagent (fresh context): gets the diff, the safety rule it claims, and the doc pages for the touched opcodes. It answers: "name the dataset that breaks this". Run it for rank ≥5 rewrites only.
+
+**Handoff artifacts:** task card → commit SHA → `eval_out/report.json` (defects, recall, false alarms, geomean, wall time) → reviewer note → `PROGRESSION_LOG.md` entry. Agents talk through files, not chat summaries.
+
+**Cadence and git discipline**
+- One rewrite per iteration. Tier 1 (<15 s: regressions + 8 examples) on every edit. Tier 2 (full eval, 1 seed) before each commit. Tier 3 (3 seeds + meta-eval) at each CP.
+- Red build → `git revert` (keep history) or turn the pass off with a flag. Never amend or reset. Tag green checkpoints `cp1`…`cp5`. Ship the last tag.
+- Escalate to the human per BUILD_PLAN §6, plus: the red-team and the builder disagree on the rubric (Q1/Q2), or recall drops after an evaluator change.
+
+**Testing the tester**
+- *Seeded mutants:* about 12 optimizers that wrap `cat` + one sed-style mutation. Each one's failure needs specific data: drop `NULLEQ`, `IS`→`EQ`, drop the last sort key (ties), `ASC`→`DESC`, push a filter below a DIV (introduces an error), trust `stats.min` to drop a check, GATHER off-by-one on the last row, swap 1.0/1.00, drop a dead-looking instruction whose error matters (should *not* be flagged).
+- *Held-out mutants:* a second red-team session writes 4–6 mutants that the evaluator's builder never sees. Report recall on those separately. This is the honest number.
+- *Equivalent mutants (precision):* identity, an unsorted-output shuffle, HJOIN↔NLJOIN, a NUM rescale, and garbage output when the original errors. All must exit 0. Target 0 false alarms over 10 seeds.
+- *Recall/precision table* in each report: caught/total mutants (seeded and held-out), false alarms per run, median and max time-to-detect.
+- *Golden repros:* each defect found (from a mutant or the real optimizer) is shrunk to the smallest (program, data) and saved under `tests/regressions/`. They replay first on every run, and each one must still fail on its mutant.
+- *Determinism:* same seed gives an identical report hash. 10 seeds on the real optimizer give the same verdict. Run once with `-j1` and once with `-j16` to catch timeouts caused by load (allow a timing margin, e.g. re-time once before flagging a timeout).
+- *Independence:* the evaluator never imports `optimizer/` (enforce with a grep check in `run_eval.sh`). It has its own small parser for its program mutator. Only bin/minivm and the CLI cross the boundary.
+
+**Anti-overfitting**
+- Score the optimizer on generated held-out programs separately from q1–q8. Ban matching on literal register or table names.
+- Data generator: base its distributions on the constraints and deliberately ignore stats (wider ranges, other distinct counts).
+
+**Budget, logging, stopping**
+- Budget for each iteration: ≤15 min wall time and ≤1 token-budget unit (track with `/usage` per session in `notes/AI_USAGE.md`). If an iteration overruns twice, cut down per BUILD_PLAN §10.
+- `notes/runs.jsonl`: one line per eval run (sha, seed, defects, recall, false alarms, geomean, wall time). `PROGRESSION_LOG.md`: one entry per iteration (tried / result / learned / keep-revert).
+- Stop when the BUILD_PLAN §6 success criteria are met **and** held-out mutant recall is 100%, false alarms are 0 over 10 seeds, and the evaluator runs in under 60 s with `-j4` (the grader's machine may be smaller).
+- Save the raw session files of every subagent role (orchestrator, builder, red-team, reviewer) to `ai-sessions/`, and their prompts to `prompts/`.

+# MiniVM
+
+MiniVM is a toy column virtual machine. It runs straight-line programs whose
+instructions each operate on whole columns of values, and it reports how many
+cycles each run costs.
+
+## Example
+
+```text
+LOAD    a, orders.o_amt
+GT      m, a, 100.00
+IDX     i, m
+GATHER  big, a, i
+OUT     big
+```
+
+- `LOAD` reads the stored column `orders.o_amt` into register `a`.
+- `GT` sets `m` to a BOOL column: `a > 100.00` at each row.
+- `IDX` sets `i` to the positions where `m` is TRUE.
+- `GATHER` sets `big` to the values of `a` at the positions in `i`.
+- `OUT` makes `big` the program's result.
+
+Saved as `big.mini`, run it from the bundle root:
+
+```sh
+bin/minivm big.mini examples/data.json
+```
+
+which prints `{"ncols": 1, "rows": [...], "cycles": C}` as one line of JSON; on
+`examples/data.json` that is 26 rows and 136 cycles. See [Running](running.md).
+
+## Contents
+
+- [Language](language.md): program syntax: lines, comments, opcodes, registers, operands, literals.
+- [Values](values.md): types, NULL, the NUM range, columns and group sets, ordering and comparison.
+- [Running](running.md): program rules and limits, errors, the command line, the table-set JSON format.
+- [Cycles](cycles.md): the cost model: base cost, string bytes, comparison cost, the full cost table.
+- [Instructions](instructions/README.md): conventions shared by all instructions, and an index of every opcode.
+  - [Arithmetic](instructions/arithmetic.md): `ADD` `SUB` `MUL` `DIV` `IDIV` `MOD` `NEG` `WRAP` `TRUNC`.
+  - [Logic](instructions/logic.md): `EQ` `NE` `LT` `LE` `GT` `GE` `IS` `AND` `OR` `NOT` `ISNULL` `NVL` `IF` `NULLIF` `NULLIFE`.
+  - [Strings](instructions/strings.md): `UPPER` `RTRIM` `TRIM` `LEFT` `SUBSTR` `LEN` `CAT`.
+  - [Dates](instructions/dates.md): `YEAR` `MONTH` `DAY` `DOW` `DATEADD` `DATEDIFF`.
+  - [Columns](instructions/columns.md): `LOAD` `FILL` `IOTA` `IDX` `GATHER` `SLICE` `OUT`.
+  - [Sorting](instructions/sorting.md): `SORT` `TOPK`, and sort keys.
+  - [Joins](instructions/joins.md): `NLJOIN` `HJOIN`.
+  - [Grouping](instructions/grouping.md): `GROUP` `GALL` `GID` `GFIRST` `GCOUNT` `GCOUNTV` `GSUM` `GMIN` `GMAX`.

+[MiniVM docs](README.md) › Cycles
+
+# Cycles
+
+This page defines how many cycles a program costs. A program's cost is the sum
+of its instructions' costs, and `bin/minivm` reports it as `cycles`
+([Running](running.md#command-line)).
+
+## Base cost
+
+Every instruction costs 4, plus the cost given for it in the
+[cost table](#cost-table). `OUT` costs only the 4.
+
+A run is stopped with an error if it does too much work; see the
+[work limit](running.md#program-rules).
+
+## String bytes
+
+The string bytes of a value are the number of bytes in it if it is a STR, and
+0 otherwise (including `NULL`).
+
+An [element-wise instruction](instructions/README.md#element-wise-instructions)
+costs, for each element of its result, a base amount plus string bytes:
+
+| Instructions | Base per element |
+|---|---|
+| `DIV` `IDIV` `MOD` | 6 |
+| `TRUNC` `YEAR` `MONTH` `DAY` `DOW` `DATEADD` `DATEDIFF` | 2 |
+| every other element-wise instruction | 1 |
+
+| Instructions | String bytes per element |
+|---|---|
+| comparisons: `EQ` `NE` `LT` `LE` `GT` `GE` `IS` | the bytes of the shorter operand |
+| `LEN` | the bytes of its operand |
+| `ISNULL` `NVL` `IF` `NULLIF` `NULLIFE` | nothing |
+| every other element-wise instruction | 1 per byte of each string operand (literals included) and of its result |
+
+A literal operand counts at every element, like the value it stands for.
+
+## Comparisons
+
+`SORT` and `TOPK` compare rows on their sort keys
+([sort keys](instructions/sorting.md#sort-keys)). One comparison of two rows
+costs, for each key examined until one differs, 2 plus the bytes of the
+shorter string.
+
+For example, on a table `t` whose STR column `s` and INT column `n` hold the
+rows `('ab', 5)` and `('ab', 7)`, comparing the two rows on keys
+`s ASC NL, n ASC NL` costs 2 + 2 for the first key, which is equal, and 2 + 0
+for the second, which differs: 6 in all. Sorting the two rows takes that one
+comparison:
+
+```text
+LOAD s, t.s
+LOAD n, t.n
+SORT o, s ASC NL, n ASC NL
+OUT  o
+```
+
+costs (4 + 2) + (4 + 2) + (4 + 2 x 2 rows + 6) + 4 = 30 cycles.
+
+## Cost table
+
+Cycles beyond the base 4 of each instruction:
+
+| Instruction | Cycles |
+|---|---|
+| [`ADD`](instructions/arithmetic.md#add-sub-mul) [`SUB`](instructions/arithmetic.md#add-sub-mul) [`MUL`](instructions/arithmetic.md#add-sub-mul) [`NEG`](instructions/arithmetic.md#neg) [`WRAP`](instructions/arithmetic.md#wrap) | 1 per element, plus 1 per byte of each string operand (literals included) and of its result |
+| [`DIV`](instructions/arithmetic.md#div) [`IDIV`](instructions/arithmetic.md#idiv) [`MOD`](instructions/arithmetic.md#mod) | 6 per element, plus 1 per byte of each string operand (literals included) and of its result |
+| [`TRUNC`](instructions/arithmetic.md#trunc) | 2 per element, plus 1 per byte of each string operand (literals included) and of its result |
+| [`EQ`](instructions/logic.md#eq-ne-lt-le-gt-ge) [`NE`](instructions/logic.md#eq-ne-lt-le-gt-ge) [`LT`](instructions/logic.md#eq-ne-lt-le-gt-ge) [`LE`](instructions/logic.md#eq-ne-lt-le-gt-ge) [`GT`](instructions/logic.md#eq-ne-lt-le-gt-ge) [`GE`](instructions/logic.md#eq-ne-lt-le-gt-ge) [`IS`](instructions/logic.md#is) | 1 per element, plus the bytes of the shorter operand |
+| [`AND`](instructions/logic.md#and-or) [`OR`](instructions/logic.md#and-or) [`NOT`](instructions/logic.md#not) | 1 per element, plus 1 per byte of each string operand (literals included) and of its result |
+| [`ISNULL`](instructions/logic.md#isnull) [`NVL`](instructions/logic.md#nvl) [`IF`](instructions/logic.md#if) [`NULLIF`](instructions/logic.md#nullif) [`NULLIFE`](instructions/logic.md#nullife) | 1 per element |
+| [`UPPER`](instructions/strings.md#upper) [`RTRIM`](instructions/strings.md#rtrim-trim) [`TRIM`](instructions/strings.md#rtrim-trim) [`LEFT`](instructions/strings.md#left) [`SUBSTR`](instructions/strings.md#substr) [`CAT`](instructions/strings.md#cat) | 1 per element, plus 1 per byte of each string operand (literals included) and of its result |
+| [`LEN`](instructions/strings.md#len) | 1 per element, plus the bytes of its operand |
+| [`YEAR`](instructions/dates.md#year-month-day) [`MONTH`](instructions/dates.md#year-month-day) [`DAY`](instructions/dates.md#year-month-day) [`DOW`](instructions/dates.md#dow) [`DATEADD`](instructions/dates.md#dateadd) [`DATEDIFF`](instructions/dates.md#datediff) | 2 per element, plus 1 per byte of each string operand (literals included) and of its result |
+| [`LOAD`](instructions/columns.md#load) [`FILL`](instructions/columns.md#fill) [`IOTA`](instructions/columns.md#iota) [`GATHER`](instructions/columns.md#gather) [`SLICE`](instructions/columns.md#slice) | 1 per value produced |
+| [`IDX`](instructions/columns.md#idx) | 1 per input value |
+| [`SORT`](instructions/sorting.md#sort) | 2 per row, plus every [comparison](#comparisons) of a stable top-down merge sort |
+| [`TOPK`](instructions/sorting.md#topk) | 2 per row, plus every [comparison](#comparisons) of a bounded insertion buffer (each row is compared with the current `n`-th, then placed by binary search), plus 1 per buffered row shifted |
+| [`NLJOIN`](instructions/joins.md#nljoin-hjoin) | per pair of rows: 1 plus the bytes of the shorter string key; plus 2 per output pair |
+| [`HJOIN`](instructions/joins.md#nljoin-hjoin) | per row of either side: 6 plus its key's string bytes; plus 2 per output pair |
+| [`GROUP`](instructions/grouping.md#group) | per row: 6 plus the string bytes of its keys |
+| [`GALL`](instructions/grouping.md#gall) [`GID`](instructions/grouping.md#gid) [`GFIRST`](instructions/grouping.md#gfirst) | 1 per row |
+| [`GCOUNT`](instructions/grouping.md#gcount) [`GCOUNTV`](instructions/grouping.md#gcountv) [`GSUM`](instructions/grouping.md#gsum) | 2 per row |
+| [`GMIN`](instructions/grouping.md#gmin-gmax) [`GMAX`](instructions/grouping.md#gmin-gmax) | 2 per row, plus the bytes of each non-NULL string |
+| [`OUT`](instructions/columns.md#out) | nothing |
+
+## Example
+
+On a table `t` whose STR column `s` holds `'ab'`, `'cde'`, `NULL`:
+
+```text
+LOAD  x, t.s
+UPPER u, x
+EQ    e, u, 'AB'
+OUT   e
+```
+
+| Instruction | Cost | Cycles |
+|---|---|---|
+| `LOAD x, t.s` | 4 + 3 values | 7 |
+| `UPPER u, x` | 4 + (1 + 2 + 2) + (1 + 3 + 3) + (1 + 0 + 0) | 17 |
+| `EQ e, u, 'AB'` | 4 + (1 + 2) + (1 + 2) + (1 + 0) | 11 |
+| `OUT e` | 4 | 4 |
+| total | | 39 |
+
+`UPPER` pays for the bytes of its operand and of its result; `EQ` pays for the
+shorter of `u` and the literal `'AB'` at each row. `bin/minivm` prints
+`{"ncols":1,"rows":[[true],[false],[null]],"cycles":39}`.

+[MiniVM docs](../README.md) › Instructions
+
+# Instructions
+
+This page states the conventions shared by all instructions and indexes every opcode. Cycle costs are summarized in [Cycles](../cycles.md).
+
+## Notation
+
+Each instruction is shown as `OP` followed by its operands:
+
+- `d` is the destination register (`li`, `ri` for joins, `g` for group sets).
+- `a`, `b`, `c`, `s`, `x`, `y` are source operands: `c` is a condition, `s` usually a string or source column, `x` and `y` dates.
+- `n`, `start` and `len` are integer literals; `lit` is a literal.
+- `i` is a column of positions, `g` a group set, `key` a [sort key](sorting.md#sort-keys), `table.column` a stored column.
+
+Where an element-wise instruction takes a register operand, a literal may be used instead; it stands for that value at every row ([literals](../language.md#literals)).
+
+Positions are 0-based INT values.
+
+An operand that expects a column can't name a group set, and the reverse; either
+is an error, as is reading a register that hasn't been set.
+
+## Element-wise instructions
+
+The instructions on the [arithmetic](arithmetic.md), [logic](logic.md), [strings](strings.md) and [dates](dates.md) pages compute one result value per row:
+
+- All register operands must have the same length, which is the result's length.
+- At least one operand must be a register.
+- Unless noted, the result is `NULL` when any operand is `NULL`. That comes first: `DIV` of NULL by 0 is NULL, not division by zero, and `ADD` of NULL and a STR is NULL, not a type error.
+- Types are checked row by row: an operand of the wrong type is an error only at a row where the instruction uses it, so whether a program fails can depend on its data. An empty column, or one of NULLs, never gives a type error.
+
+Given a table `t` whose INT column `x` holds `1`, `NULL`, `3`:
+
+```text
+LOAD  x, t.x
+ADD   y, x, 10      ; the literal 10 stands for 10 at every row
+OUT   y
+```
+
+prints `{"ncols":1,"rows":[[11],[null],[13]],"cycles":18}`.
+
+## Index
+
+### Arithmetic
+
+| Instruction | Summary |
+|---|---|
+| [`ADD`](arithmetic.md#add-sub-mul) | sum |
+| [`SUB`](arithmetic.md#add-sub-mul) | difference |
+| [`MUL`](arithmetic.md#add-sub-mul) | product |
+| [`DIV`](arithmetic.md#div) | NUM quotient |
+| [`IDIV`](arithmetic.md#idiv) | INT quotient truncated toward zero |
+| [`MOD`](arithmetic.md#mod) | INT remainder with the sign of `a` |
+| [`NEG`](arithmetic.md#neg) | negation |
+| [`WRAP`](arithmetic.md#wrap) | INT wrapped to 32-bit two's complement |
+| [`TRUNC`](arithmetic.md#trunc) | NUM truncated toward zero to INT |
+
+### Logic
+
+| Instruction | Summary |
+|---|---|
+| [`EQ`](logic.md#eq-ne-lt-le-gt-ge) | equal |
+| [`NE`](logic.md#eq-ne-lt-le-gt-ge) | not equal |
+| [`LT`](logic.md#eq-ne-lt-le-gt-ge) | less than |
+| [`LE`](logic.md#eq-ne-lt-le-gt-ge) | less than or equal |
+| [`GT`](logic.md#eq-ne-lt-le-gt-ge) | greater than |
+| [`GE`](logic.md#eq-ne-lt-le-gt-ge) | greater than or equal |
+| [`IS`](logic.md#is) | equal, or both NULL; never NULL |
+| [`AND`](logic.md#and-or) | three-valued conjunction |
+| [`OR`](logic.md#and-or) | three-valued disjunction |
+| [`NOT`](logic.md#not) | BOOL negation |
+| [`ISNULL`](logic.md#isnull) | whether the value is NULL |
+| [`NVL`](logic.md#nvl) | `a`, or `b` when `a` is NULL |
+| [`IF`](logic.md#if) | `a` where `c` is TRUE, else `b` |
+| [`NULLIF`](logic.md#nullif) | NULL where `a IS b`, else `a` |
+| [`NULLIFE`](logic.md#nullife) | NULL where `a` is the empty string, else `a` |
+
+### Strings
+
+| Instruction | Summary |
+|---|---|
+| [`UPPER`](strings.md#upper) | upper case |
+| [`RTRIM`](strings.md#rtrim-trim) | without trailing spaces |
+| [`TRIM`](strings.md#rtrim-trim) | without leading and trailing spaces |
+| [`LEFT`](strings.md#left) | first `n` characters |
+| [`SUBSTR`](strings.md#substr) | `len` characters from 1-based `start` |
+| [`LEN`](strings.md#len) | number of characters |
+| [`CAT`](strings.md#cat) | concatenation |
+

+
+| Instruction | Summary |

+

+
+| Instruction | Summary |
+|---|---|
+| [`LOAD`](columns.md#load) | a stored column |

+| [`IDX`](columns.md#idx) | positions where a BOOL column is TRUE |
+| [`GATHER`](columns.md#gather) | `d[k] = s[i[k]]` |
+| [`SLICE`](columns.md#slice) | the first `n` values |
+| [`OUT`](columns.md#out) | the program's result |
+
+### Sorting
+
+| Instruction | Summary |
+|---|---|

+Sort keys are described in [Sort keys](sorting.md#sort-keys).
+
+### Joins

+
+### Grouping
+
+| Instruction | Summary |
+|---|---|

+| [`GFIRST`](grouping.md#gfirst) | position of each group's first row |
+| [`GCOUNT`](grouping.md#gcount) | rows per group |
+| [`GCOUNTV`](grouping.md#gcountv) | non-NULL values per group |
+| [`GSUM`](grouping.md#gsum) | sum of non-NULL values per group |
+| [`GMIN`](grouping.md#gmin-gmax) | least non-NULL value per group |
+| [`GMAX`](grouping.md#gmin-gmax) | greatest non-NULL value per group |

+[MiniVM docs](../README.md) › [Instructions](README.md) › Arithmetic
+
+# Arithmetic
+
+Element-wise arithmetic on INT and NUM values. All instructions on this page follow the [element-wise rules](README.md#element-wise-instructions): the result is `NULL` when any operand is `NULL`. Their operands and results are numbers, so they pay no [string bytes](../cycles.md#string-bytes).
+
+| Instruction | Result |
+|---|---|
+| [`ADD d, a, b`](#add-sub-mul) / `SUB` / `MUL` | INT if both are INT, else NUM |
+| [`DIV d, a, b`](#div) | NUM quotient |
+| [`IDIV d, a, b`](#idiv) | INT quotient truncated toward zero |
+| [`MOD d, a, b`](#mod) | INT remainder with the sign of `a` |
+| [`NEG d, a`](#neg) | `-a` |
+| [`WRAP d, a`](#wrap) | INT wrapped to 32-bit two's complement |
+| [`TRUNC d, a`](#trunc) | INT |
+
+## `ADD`, `SUB`, `MUL`
+
+```text
+ADD d, a, b
+SUB d, a, b
+MUL d, a, b
+```
+
+`a + b`, `a - b` and `a * b`. The result is INT if both operands are INT, else NUM, rounded as in [NUM range](../values.md#num-range). A result outside the INT or NUM range is the error `numeric overflow`, as is an INT operand above 2^96 - 1 in magnitude when the other operand is NUM.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element.
+
+## `DIV`
+
+```text
+DIV d, a, b
+```
+
+The NUM quotient `a / b`, rounded as in [NUM range](../values.md#num-range), even when both operands are INT. It is an error if `b` is 0, and `numeric overflow` if the quotient is outside the NUM range or an INT operand is above 2^96 - 1 in magnitude.
+
+**Cycles:** 6 per element.
+
+## `IDIV`
+
+```text
+IDIV d, a, b
+```
+
+The INT quotient `a / b`, truncated toward zero. Both operands must be INT. It is an error if `b` is 0, or if the quotient is outside the INT range.
+
+**Cycles:** 6 per element.
+
+## `MOD`
+
+```text
+MOD d, a, b
+```
+
+The INT remainder of `a / b`, with the sign of `a`. Both operands must be INT. It is an error if `b` is 0.
+
+**Cycles:** 6 per element.
+
+## `NEG`
+
+```text
+NEG d, a
+```
+
+`-a`. A result outside the INT or NUM range is an error.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element.
+
+## `WRAP`
+
+```text
+WRAP d, a
+```
+
+INT `a` wrapped to 32-bit two's complement.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element.
+
+## `TRUNC`
+
+```text
+TRUNC d, a
+```
+
+INT: a NUM `a` truncated toward zero; an INT `a` is unchanged. Any other type is an error.
+
+**Cycles:** 2 per element.

+[MiniVM docs](../README.md) › [Instructions](README.md) › Columns
+
+# Column instructions
+
+These instructions load table columns, build columns, select and reorder values by position, and produce the program's result. They are not element-wise. Positions are 0-based INT values ([notation](README.md#notation)).
+
+| Instruction | Result |
+|---|---|
+| [`LOAD d, table.column`](#load) | the column as stored |
+| [`FILL d, lit, s`](#fill) | `lit` repeated once per value of `s` |
+| [`IOTA d, s`](#iota) | positions `0 .. len(s)-1` |
+| [`IDX d, s`](#idx) | positions where `s` is TRUE |
+| [`GATHER d, s, i`](#gather) | `d[k] = s[i[k]]` |
+| [`SLICE d, s, n`](#slice) | the first `n` values of `s` |
+| [`OUT s, ...`](#out) | the program's result |
+
+## `LOAD`
+
+```text
+LOAD d, table.column
+```
+
+Sets `d` to the column `column` of table `table` as stored. An empty string stays `''`; it is not NULL.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per value produced.
+
+## `FILL`
+
+```text
+FILL d, lit, s
+```
+
+Sets `d` to the literal `lit` repeated once per value of `s`. `lit` must be a literal.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per value produced.
+
+## `IOTA`
+
+```text
+IOTA d, s
+```
+
+Sets `d` to the positions `0 .. len(s)-1` of `s`.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per value produced.
+
+## `IDX`
+
+```text
+IDX d, s
+```
+
+Sets `d` to the positions, in order, where the BOOL column `s` is TRUE. FALSE and NULL positions are left out.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per input value.
+
+## `GATHER`
+
+```text
+GATHER d, s, i
+```
+
+Sets `d[k] = s[i[k]]` for every position `k` of `i`; `d` has the length of `i`. Every index must be an INT in range for `s`, or the instruction is an error. Use it with the positions from [`IDX`](#idx), [`SORT`](sorting.md#sort), [`TOPK`](sorting.md#topk), the [joins](joins.md#nljoin-hjoin) and [`GFIRST`](grouping.md#gfirst) to filter, reorder, pair up or pick rows.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per value produced.
+
+## `SLICE`
+
+```text
+SLICE d, s, n
+```
+
+Sets `d` to the first `n` values of `s` (all of `s` if it has fewer). `n` is an integer literal.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per value produced.
+
+## `OUT`
+
+```text
+OUT s, ...
+```
+
+Produces the program's result: one column per operand, all the same length. `OUT` must be the last instruction and the program's only `OUT` ([program rules](../running.md#program-rules)).
+
+**Cycles:** nothing beyond the base cost.

+[MiniVM docs](../README.md) › [Instructions](README.md) › Dates
+
+# Dates
+
+Element-wise operations on DATE values. All instructions on this page follow the [element-wise rules](README.md#element-wise-instructions): the result is `NULL` when any operand is `NULL`. Their operands and results are not strings, so they pay no [string bytes](../cycles.md#string-bytes).
+
+| Instruction | Result |
+|---|---|
+| [`YEAR` `MONTH` `DAY` `d, x`](#year-month-day) | date parts |
+| [`DOW d, x`](#dow) | day of week, 1 = Monday ... 7 = Sunday |
+| [`DATEADD d, x, n`](#dateadd) | date `x` plus `n` days |
+| [`DATEDIFF d, x, y`](#datediff) | INT days from `y` to `x` |
+
+## `YEAR`, `MONTH`, `DAY`
+
+```text
+YEAR  d, x
+MONTH d, x
+DAY   d, x
+```
+
+INT: the year, month or day of month of date `x`.
+
+**Cycles:** 2 per element.
+
+## `DOW`
+
+```text
+DOW d, x
+```
+
+INT: the day of the week of date `x`, 1 = Monday ... 7 = Sunday.
+
+**Cycles:** 2 per element.
+
+## `DATEADD`
+
+```text
+DATEADD d, x, n
+```
+
+The date `x` plus `n` days. It is an error if the result falls outside years 1..9999.
+
+**Cycles:** 2 per element.
+
+## `DATEDIFF`
+
+```text
+DATEDIFF d, x, y
+```
+
+INT: the number of days from `y` to `x`.
+
+**Cycles:** 2 per element.

+[MiniVM docs](../README.md) › [Instructions](README.md) › Grouping
+
+# Grouping instructions
+
+`GROUP` and `GALL` divide rows into groups and store them as a group set; the other instructions on this page read a group set and return columns. Positions are 0-based INT values ([notation](README.md#notation)).
+
+| Instruction | Result |
+|---|---|
+| [`GROUP g, k, ...`](#group) | group set: rows with equal keys share a group |
+| [`GALL g, s`](#gall) | group set: one group holding every row of `s` |
+| [`GID d, g`](#gid) | group number of each row |
+| [`GFIRST d, g`](#gfirst) | position of each group's first row |
+| [`GCOUNT d, g`](#gcount) | rows per group |
+| [`GCOUNTV d, g, s`](#gcountv) | non-NULL values of `s` per group |
+| [`GSUM d, g, s`](#gsum) | sum of non-NULL values per group |
+| [`GMIN d, g, s` / `GMAX`](#gmin-gmax) | least / greatest non-NULL value per group |
+
+## Group sets
+
+A register holds either a column or a group set ([values](../values.md#columns-and-group-sets)). A group set assigns each of its rows a group number; groups are numbered from 0 in order of first appearance. [`GROUP`](#group) and [`GALL`](#gall) produce a group set. [`GID`](#gid) returns a column with one value per row; the aggregates [`GFIRST`](#gfirst) through [`GMAX`](#gmin-gmax) return a column with one value per group, the value for group `k` at position `k`. The `s` operand of an aggregate is a column over the same rows as the group set; a different length is an error.
+
+Aggregate columns of the same group set line up with each other. To label each group with its key, gather the key at [`GFIRST`](#gfirst). Orders per status:
+
+```text
+LOAD   st, orders.o_status
+GROUP  g, st
+GFIRST f, g
+GATHER key, st, f
+GCOUNT n, g
+OUT    key, n
+```
+
+On `examples/data.json` this prints `[["open",7],["closed",12],["shipped",11]]`.
+
+## `GROUP`
+
+```text
+GROUP g, k, ...
+```
+
+Sets `g` to a group set over the rows of the key columns `k, ...`: rows with equal keys share a group, and NULL equals NULL. All key columns must have the same length. Keys are compared as in [Ordering](../values.md#ordering): INT and NUM mix (`1` and `1.00` share a group), and a key column holding values that can't be compared (e.g. STR and INT) is an error.
+
+**Cycles:** per row: 6 plus the string bytes of its keys ([rules](../cycles.md#string-bytes)).
+
+## `GALL`
+
+```text
+GALL g, s
+```
+
+Sets `g` to a group set with one group holding every row of `s`, even when `s` is empty.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per row.
+
+## `GID`
+
+```text
+GID d, g
+```
+
+Sets `d` to the group number of each row of `g`.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per row.
+
+## `GFIRST`
+
+```text
+GFIRST d, g
+```
+
+Sets `d` to the position of each group's first row. The result is NULL for the group of an empty `GALL`.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per row.
+
+## `GCOUNT`
+
+```text
+GCOUNT d, g
+```
+
+Sets `d` to the number of rows in each group.
+
+**Cycles:** 2 per row.
+
+## `GCOUNTV`
+
+```text
+GCOUNTV d, g, s
+```
+
+Sets `d` to the number of non-NULL values of `s` in each group.
+
+**Cycles:** 2 per row.
+
+## `GSUM`
+
+```text
+GSUM d, g, s
+```
+
+Sets `d` to the sum of the non-NULL values of `s` in each group: INT when all are INT, else NUM. The result is NULL for a group with no non-NULL values. A NUM sum is computed exactly, with as many digits after the point as the value with the most, and rounded once as in [NUM range](../values.md#num-range), so it doesn't depend on the order of the rows. Only each group's total is checked: a total outside the INT or NUM range is the error `numeric overflow`, whatever the running sums along the way.
+
+**Cycles:** 2 per row.
+
+## `GMIN`, `GMAX`
+
+```text
+GMIN d, g, s
+GMAX d, g, s
+```
+
+Sets `d` to the least (`GMIN`) or greatest (`GMAX`) non-NULL value of `s` in each group, the first of equals: of `1`, `1.0` and `1.00`, whichever comes first. Values are compared as in [Ordering](../values.md#ordering). The result is NULL for a group with no non-NULL values.
+
+**Cycles:** 2 per row, plus the bytes of each non-NULL string ([rules](../cycles.md#string-bytes)).

new file mode 100644
index 0000000..25e0f3c
--- /dev/null
+++ b/minivm-takehome/docs/instructions/joins.md
@@ -0,0 +1,40 @@
+[MiniVM docs](../README.md) › [Instructions](README.md) › Joins
+
+# Join instructions
+
+`NLJOIN` and `HJOIN` pair up the rows of two key columns whose keys are equal and return the pairs as two position columns. Positions are 0-based INT values ([notation](README.md#notation)).
+
+| Instruction | Result |
+|---|---|
+| [`NLJOIN li, ri, lk, rk [, NULLEQ]`](#nljoin-hjoin) | positions of all pairs with `lk[i] = rk[j]` |
+| [`HJOIN li, ri, lk, rk [, NULLEQ]`](#nljoin-hjoin) | same result as `NLJOIN` |
+
+## `NLJOIN`, `HJOIN`
+
+```text
+NLJOIN li, ri, lk, rk [, NULLEQ]
+HJOIN  li, ri, lk, rk [, NULLEQ]
+```
+
+Finds all pairs `(i, j)` with `lk[i] = rk[j]`, ordered by `i` then `j`. Sets `li` to the positions into `lk` and `ri` to the positions into `rk`, one entry per pair; if `li` and `ri` are the same register, it holds the positions into `rk`. Keys are compared as in [Ordering](../values.md#ordering): a non-NULL left key that can't be compared with a non-NULL right key (e.g. STR with INT) is an error. A NULL key matches no key, unless the optional `NULLEQ` flag is given, in which case NULL keys match each other. `HJOIN` gives the same result as `NLJOIN`, errors included; only the cost differs.
+
+**Cycles (`NLJOIN`):** per pair of rows: 1 plus the bytes of the shorter string key ([rules](../cycles.md#string-bytes)); plus 2 per output pair.
+
+**Cycles (`HJOIN`):** per row of either side: 6 plus its key's string bytes ([rules](../cycles.md#string-bytes)); plus 2 per output pair.
+
+### Example
+
+[`GATHER`](columns.md#gather) through `li` picks columns of the left rows and through `ri` columns of the right rows, giving one row per pair. Customers with their region's name:
+
+```text
+LOAD   reg, customers.c_region
+LOAD   code, regions.r_code
+HJOIN  li, ri, reg, code
+LOAD   cname, customers.c_name
+LOAD   rname, regions.r_name
+GATHER c, cname, li
+GATHER r, rname, ri
+OUT    c, r
+```
+
+On `examples/data.json` this prints `[["Cy","Southern"],["Dee","West Coast"],["Eve","Southern"], ...]`.

+[MiniVM docs](../README.md) › [Instructions](README.md) › Logic
+
+# Logic
+
+Element-wise comparisons, BOOL logic and NULL handling. All instructions on this page follow the [element-wise rules](README.md#element-wise-instructions); where an instruction's NULL behavior differs from the default (the result is `NULL` when any operand is `NULL`), its section says so.
+
+| Instruction | Result |
+|---|---|
+| [`EQ` `NE` `LT` `LE` `GT` `GE` `d, a, b`](#eq-ne-lt-le-gt-ge) | BOOL comparison |
+| [`IS d, a, b`](#is) | BOOL, never NULL: equal, or both NULL |
+| [`AND d, a, b`](#and-or) / `OR` | three-valued logic |
+| [`NOT d, a`](#not) | BOOL negation |
+| [`ISNULL d, a`](#isnull) | BOOL, never NULL |
+| [`NVL d, a, b`](#nvl) | `a`, or `b` when `a` is NULL |
+| [`IF d, c, a, b`](#if) | `a` where `c` is TRUE, else `b` |
+| [`NULLIF d, a, b`](#nullif) | NULL where `a IS b`, else `a` |
+| [`NULLIFE d, a`](#nullife) | NULL where `a` is the empty string, else `a` |
+
+## `EQ`, `NE`, `LT`, `LE`, `GT`, `GE`
+
+```text
+EQ d, a, b
+NE d, a, b
+LT d, a, b
+LE d, a, b
+GT d, a, b
+GE d, a, b
+```
+
+BOOL comparison of `a` with `b`: `a = b`, `a <> b`, `a < b`, `a <= b`, `a > b`, `a >= b`. Values are ordered as described in [Ordering](../values.md#ordering); comparing other mixes of types is an error.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element, plus the bytes of the shorter operand ([rules](../cycles.md#string-bytes)).
+
+## `IS`
+
+```text
+IS d, a, b
+```
+
+BOOL, never NULL: TRUE where `a` and `b` are equal, or both NULL, and FALSE where just one is NULL. Values are ordered as described in [Ordering](../values.md#ordering); comparing two non-NULL values of other mixes of types is an error.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element, plus the bytes of the shorter operand ([rules](../cycles.md#string-bytes)).
+
+## `AND`, `OR`
+
+```text
+AND d, a, b
+OR  d, a, b
+```
+
+Three-valued logic on BOOL values; a non-BOOL operand is an error: `FALSE AND NULL` is `FALSE`, and `TRUE OR NULL` is `TRUE`; otherwise a NULL operand gives NULL.
+
+| `a` | `b` | `AND` | `OR` |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| `TRUE` | `TRUE` | `TRUE` | `TRUE` |
+| `TRUE` | `FALSE` | `FALSE` | `TRUE` |
+| `FALSE` | `FALSE` | `FALSE` | `FALSE` |
+| `TRUE` | `NULL` | `NULL` | `TRUE` |
+| `FALSE` | `NULL` | `FALSE` | `NULL` |
+| `NULL` | `NULL` | `NULL` | `NULL` |
+
+Both instructions are symmetric in `a` and `b`.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element.
+
+## `NOT`
+
+```text
+NOT d, a
+```
+
+BOOL negation of `a`, which must be BOOL.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element.
+
+## `ISNULL`
+
+```text
+ISNULL d, a
+```
+
+BOOL, never NULL: TRUE where `a` is NULL.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element; no string bytes.
+
+## `NVL`
+
+```text
+NVL d, a, b
+```
+
+`a`, or `b` where `a` is NULL. `a` and `b` may have different types.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element; no string bytes.
+
+## `IF`
+
+```text
+IF d, c, a, b
+```
+
+`a` where `c` is TRUE, else `b` (also where `c` is NULL). `c` must be BOOL; `a` and `b` may have any types, and a NULL `a` or `b` is passed through.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element; no string bytes.
+
+## `NULLIF`
+
+```text
+NULLIF d, a, b
+```
+
+NULL where `a IS b` (see [`IS`](#is)), else `a`.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element; no string bytes.
+
+## `NULLIFE`
+
+```text
+NULLIFE d, a
+```
+
+NULL where `a` is the empty string, else `a`, which must be STR.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element; no string bytes.

index 0000000..b8f56e1

+++ b/minivm-takehome/docs/instructions/sorting.md
@@ -0,0 +1,53 @@
+[MiniVM docs](../README.md) › [Instructions](README.md) › Sorting
+
+# Sorting instructions
+
+`SORT` and `TOPK` order the rows of one or more key columns and return positions, which [`GATHER`](columns.md#gather) applies to any column of the same rows. Positions are 0-based INT values ([notation](README.md#notation)).
+
+| Instruction | Result |
+|---|---|
+| [`SORT d, key, ...`](#sort) | positions of the rows in stable sorted order |
+| [`TOPK d, n, key, ...`](#topk) | the first `n` positions of the same order |
+
+## `SORT`
+
+```text
+SORT d, key, ...
+```
+
+Sets `d` to the positions of the rows in stable sorted order by the [sort keys](#sort-keys).
+
+**Cycles:** 2 per row, plus every comparison of a stable top-down merge sort ([comparison cost](../cycles.md#comparisons)).
+
+## `TOPK`
+
+```text
+TOPK d, n, key, ...
+```
+
+Sets `d` to the first `n` positions of the order `SORT` would produce for the same keys (all of them if there are fewer rows). `n` is an integer literal.
+
+**Cycles:** 2 per row, plus every comparison of a bounded insertion buffer (each row is compared with the current `n`-th, then placed by binary search) ([comparison cost](../cycles.md#comparisons)), plus 1 per buffered row shifted.
+
+## Sort keys
+
+```text
+reg ASC|DESC NF|NL
+```
+
+A sort key is a register followed by a direction and a NULL placement, separated by spaces or tabs; all three parts are required. All key columns must have the same length. Rows are ordered by the first key, then by each following key among rows the earlier keys leave equal.
+
+- `ASC` orders values from least to greatest, `DESC` reverses the order of values. Values are compared as in [Ordering](../values.md#ordering).
+- `NF` puts NULLs first and `NL` puts them last, whatever the direction.
+- Ties keep their input order.
+
+Customers by credit, highest first with NULLs last, then by name:
+
+```text
+LOAD   name, customers.c_name
+LOAD   credit, customers.c_credit
+SORT   p, credit DESC NL, name ASC NF
+GATHER n, name, p
+GATHER c, credit, p
+OUT    n, c
+```

index 0000000..56c9104

+[MiniVM docs](../README.md) › [Instructions](README.md) › Strings
+
+# Strings
+
+Element-wise operations on STR values. All instructions on this page follow the [element-wise rules](README.md#element-wise-instructions): the result is `NULL` when any operand is `NULL`. Lengths and positions count characters; string-byte costs count bytes ([rules](../cycles.md#string-bytes)).
+
+| Instruction | Result |
+|---|---|
+| [`UPPER d, s`](#upper) | upper case (Unicode) |
+| [`RTRIM d, s`](#rtrim-trim) / [`TRIM d, s`](#rtrim-trim) | without trailing / leading and trailing spaces |
+| [`LEFT d, s, n`](#left) | first `n` characters |
+| [`SUBSTR d, s, start, len`](#substr) | `len` characters from 1-based `start` |
+| [`LEN d, s`](#len) | number of characters |
+| [`CAT d, a, b`](#cat) | concatenation |
+
+## `UPPER`
+
+```text
+UPPER d, s
+```
+
+`s` in upper case, by Unicode's full case mapping, which can change the number of characters: `UPPER('straße')` is `'STRASSE'`.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element, plus 1 per byte of `s` and of the result ([rules](../cycles.md#string-bytes)).
+
+## `RTRIM`, `TRIM`
+
+```text
+RTRIM d, s
+TRIM  d, s
+```
+
+`s` without trailing spaces (`RTRIM`), or without leading and trailing spaces (`TRIM`). Only `' '` counts as a space.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element, plus 1 per byte of `s` and of the result ([rules](../cycles.md#string-bytes)).
+
+## `LEFT`
+
+```text
+LEFT d, s, n
+```
+
+The first `n` characters of `s` (all of `s` if it is shorter). `n` is a non-negative [integer literal](../language.md#integer-operands).
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element, plus 1 per byte of `s` and of the result ([rules](../cycles.md#string-bytes)).
+
+## `SUBSTR`
+
+```text
+SUBSTR d, s, start, len
+```
+
+`len` characters of `s`, starting at the 1-based position `start` (fewer if `s` ends first). `start` (at least 1) and `len` are [integer literals](../language.md#integer-operands).
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element, plus 1 per byte of `s` and of the result ([rules](../cycles.md#string-bytes)).
+
+## `LEN`
+
+```text
+LEN d, s
+```
+
+INT: the number of characters in `s`.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element, plus 1 per byte of `s` ([rules](../cycles.md#string-bytes)).
+
+## `CAT`
+
+```text
+CAT d, a, b
+```
+
+The concatenation of `a` and `b`.
+
+**Cycles:** 1 per element, plus 1 per byte of `a`, of `b` (literals included) and of the result ([rules](../cycles.md#string-bytes)).
+
+## Example
+
+Given a table `t` whose STR column `s` holds `' ab '`, `''` and `'Zoë'`:
+
+```text
+LOAD  s, t.s
+TRIM  u, s
+CAT   c, u, '!'
+LEN   l, c
+OUT   c, l
+```
+
+prints `{"ncols":2,"rows":[["ab!",3],["!",1],["Zoë!",4]],"cycles":73}`. `'Zoë!'` has 4 characters but 5 bytes: `CAT` costs 1 + 4 + 1 + 5 = 11 cycles on that row, and `LEN` costs 1 + 5.

+[MiniVM docs](README.md) › Language
+
+# Language
+
+This page gives the syntax of MiniVM programs: lines, comments, opcodes,
+registers, operands and literals.
+
+## Programs
+
+A program is straight-line: its instructions run once each, in order. Each
+line holds one instruction. A `;` starts a comment, which runs to the end of
+the line; a line may hold only a comment, or be blank. Spaces and tabs are
+interchangeable: they separate the opcode from its operands, and may indent a
+line or surround an operand.
+
+```text
+; Ids and amounts of orders over 100.
+load   id, orders.o_id
+load   amt, orders.o_amt
+gt     m, amt, 100      ; NULL where o_amt is NULL
+idx    i, m
+gather a, id, i
+gather b, amt, i
+out    a, b
+```
+
+The program must end with its only `OUT`; see [Program rules](running.md#program-rules).
+
+## Instructions
+
+An instruction is an opcode followed by comma-separated operands:
+
+```text
+OP dest, operand, ...
+```
+
+Opcodes are case-insensitive: `gt`, `Gt` and `GT` are the same instruction.
+So are the keywords `NULL`, `TRUE`, `FALSE`, `ASC`, `DESC`, `NF`, `NL` and
+`NULLEQ`.
+Each opcode's operands are given on its page in the
+[instruction reference](instructions/README.md).
+
+## Registers
+
+Register names are identifiers, such as `r1`, `amt` or `_t0`: a letter or `_`,
+followed by letters, digits and `_`. Register names are case-sensitive, and a
+keyword can't be a register name. A register holds a column or a group set
+([Values](values.md#columns-and-group-sets)) and can be overwritten.
+
+## Operands
+
+An operand is one of:
+
+| Operand | Example | Used by |
+|---|---|---|
+| register | `amt` | every instruction |
+| literal | `100.00` | element-wise instructions, `FILL`, integer operands such as `LEFT`'s `n` |
+| `table.column` | `orders.o_amt` | [`LOAD`](instructions/columns.md#load) |
+| sort key | `amt DESC NL` | [`SORT`, `TOPK`](instructions/sorting.md#sort-keys) |
+| `NULLEQ` | `NULLEQ` | [`NLJOIN`, `HJOIN`](instructions/joins.md#nljoin-hjoin) |
+
+## Literals
+
+| Literal | Type | Notes |
+|---|---|---|
+| `42`, `-7` | INT | must fit in 128 bits |
+| `12.50` | NUM | must be exact, in the [NUM range](values.md#num-range) |
+| `'it''s'` | STR | a quote inside the string is written twice |
+| `#2024-01-31#` | DATE | years 0001 to 9999 |
+| `NULL` | NULL | |
+| `TRUE`, `FALSE` | BOOL | |
+
+A literal stands for that value at every row.
+
+## Integer operands
+
+Some operands must be integer literals rather than registers: `n` and `len`
+(`LEFT`, `SUBSTR`, `SLICE`, `TOPK`) are non-negative, and `SUBSTR`'s `start` is
+at least 1. They may be arbitrarily large; a count larger than the input means
+all of it.

new file mode 100644
index 0000000..5360735
--- /dev/null
+++ b/minivm-takehome/docs/running.md

+[MiniVM docs](README.md) › Running
+
+# Running
+
+This page gives the rules every program must follow, what happens on an error,
+how to run a program with `bin/minivm`, and the JSON format of the tables it
+reads.
+
+## Program rules
+
+The last instruction must be the program's only [`OUT`](instructions/columns.md#out).
+
+| Limit | Value |
+|---|---|
+| instructions per program | at most 20,000 |
+| values held by one register | at most 4,000,000 (a join's output counts) |
+| values held by all registers together | at most 64,000,000; a long string counts as several values (about 1 per 32 bytes) |
+| work per run | roughly 300,000,000 [cycles](cycles.md); see below |
+| rows per table | at most 10,000 |
+| program file | at most 4 MiB |
+| table-set file | at most 32 MiB and 2,000,000 JSON values |
+
+The work limit stops runaway programs. It counts a run's cycles, but `HJOIN`
+and `GROUP` and copying long strings use it up faster than their cycles
+suggest, so such programs stop somewhat sooner. Exceeding it, like any other
+limit, is an error. It has no effect on the cycles reported for a run that
+finishes.
+
+## Errors
+
+Any error stops the program. No result is printed; the error goes to stderr
+and `bin/minivm` exits with status 1.
+
+## Command line
+
+```sh
+bin/minivm PROGRAM.mini TABLES.json
+bin/minivm --schema TABLES.json
+```
+
+runs the program in `PROGRAM.mini` on the [table set](#table-sets) in
+`TABLES.json`. On success it prints one line of JSON to stdout and exits 0:
+
+```json
+{"ncols": N, "rows": [...], "cycles": C}
+```
+
+- `ncols` is the number of `OUT` operands.
+- `rows` is the result, one array of `ncols` values per row.
+  `NULL` is `null` and a BOOL is `true` or `false`. An INT is a JSON integer,
+  or a string of its digits outside -2^63 .. 2^63-1. A NUM is a string that keeps
+  its digits after the point (`"250.00"`), a STR a string, and a DATE a string
+  `"YYYY-MM-DD"`.
+- `cycles` is the program's cost ([Cycles](cycles.md)).
+
+| Exit status | Meaning |
+|---|---|
+| 0 | success; the result is on stdout |
+| 1 | error; the message is on stderr |
+| 2 | wrong number of arguments; a usage line is on stderr |
+
+With the program from [Language](language.md#programs) saved as
+`over100.mini` and the [example table set](#example) as `orders.json`:
+
+```sh
+$ bin/minivm over100.mini orders.json
+{"ncols":2,"rows":[[1,"250.00"]],"cycles":42}
+```
+
+`bin/minivm --schema TABLES.json` prints the table set's [schema file](#schema-files)
+as one line of JSON instead of running a program.
+
+A program that compares a DATE with an INT stops with an error:
+
+```text
+LOAD d, orders.o_date
+GT   m, d, 5
+OUT  m
+```
+
+```sh
+$ bin/minivm bad.mini orders.json
+error: line 2: GT: expected a number, got DATE
+```
+
+## Table sets
+
+A table set is a JSON object mapping each table name to its columns and rows:
+
+```json
+{"<table>": {"columns": [{"name": "c", "type": "INT|NUM|STR|DATE"}], "rows": [[...], ...]}}
+```
+
+- Table and column names are identifiers (letters, digits and `_`, not starting
+  with a digit), compared without regard to case. Duplicate names are an error.
+- `columns` lists each column's `name` and `type`, one of `INT`, `NUM`, `STR`, `DATE`.
+- `rows` lists each row as an array of values, one per column, in column order.
+  A table without `rows` is empty.
+- A table may also declare [constraints](#constraints).
+- Any key other than those shown or listed there is an error, as is a repeated key.
+- A table holds at most 10,000 rows.
+- INT values are JSON integers that fit in 32 bits.
+- NUM values may be JSON numbers or strings. They must be exact, in the
+  [NUM range](values.md#num-range).
+- Dates are strings `"YYYY-MM-DD"`, years 0001 to 9999.
+- JSON `null` is `NULL`.
+
+A program reads a stored column with `LOAD d, table.column` ([`LOAD`](instructions/columns.md#load)).
+
+### Constraints
+
+A table may declare what its rows guarantee. `bin/minivm` checks every declared
+constraint when it reads the table set, and a table set that breaks one is an error.
+
+| Key | Example | Guarantee |
+|---|---|---|
+| `key` | `["l_order", "l_no"]` | the columns are never `NULL`, and no two rows agree on all of them |
+| `not_null` | `["p_price"]` | the columns are never `NULL` |
+| `refs` | `{"l_prod": "products.p_id"}` | every non-NULL value of the column is a value of the other column, which must be its table's whole `key` and have the same type (INT and NUM may refer to each other) |
+| `check` | `{"l_qty": [1, 500]}` | every non-NULL value lies between the bounds, inclusive; `null` leaves a side open; INT, NUM and DATE columns only |
+| `sorted` | `["l_order", "l_no"]` | the rows are stored in ascending order of the columns, as `ASC NF` sorts them |
+
+Values are compared as in [Ordering](values.md#ordering), so NUM keys `1.0` and
+`1.00` are the same key. Column names in constraints are compared without regard
+to case, and naming a column twice in one constraint is an error. A table may refer to itself.
+
+```json
+{
+  "orders": {"columns": [{"name": "o_id", "type": "INT"}], "key": ["o_id"], "rows": [[1], [2]]},
+  "lines": {
+    "columns": [{"name": "l_order", "type": "INT"}, {"name": "l_qty", "type": "INT"}],
+    "refs": {"l_order": "orders.o_id"}, "check": {"l_qty": [1, null]}, "sorted": ["l_order"],
+    "rows": [[1, 3], [1, 1], [2, 5]]
+  }
+}
+```
+
+### Example
+
+```json
+{
+  "orders": {
+    "columns": [
+      {"name": "o_id",     "type": "INT"},
+      {"name": "o_amt",    "type": "NUM"},
+      {"name": "o_date",   "type": "DATE"},
+      {"name": "o_status", "type": "STR"}
+    ],
+    "rows": [
+      [1, 250.00,   "2024-01-31", "shipped"],
+      [2, "99.5",   "2024-02-01", ""],
+      [3, null,     "2024-02-03", null]
+    ]
+  }
+}
+```
+
+## Schema files
+
+`bin/minivm --schema TABLES.json` describes a table set without its rows: each table's
+columns and [constraints](#constraints), as in the table set, plus a `stats` object
+summarizing its rows:
+
+```json
+{"<table>": {"columns": [...], "key": [...], ..., "stats": {"rows": N, "columns": {"<column>": {"nulls": N, "distinct": N, "min": v, "max": v, "common": [[v, N], ...], ...}}}}}
+```
+
+The constraints hold for every table set with this schema; the stats describe only this one.
+
+| Stats field | Meaning |
+|---|---|
+| `rows` | rows in the table |
+| `nulls` | `NULL` values in the column |
+| `distinct` | distinct non-NULL values, compared as in [Ordering](values.md#ordering) |
+| `min`, `max` | least and greatest non-NULL value; `null` if there is none |
+| `avg_bytes` | STR only: mean bytes of the non-NULL values, rounded to 2 decimal places; 0 if there are none |
+| `common` | up to 8 values that occur more than once, most frequent first (ties in value order), with their counts |
+| `histogram` | INT, NUM and DATE only, if any value is non-NULL: 11 bounds of 10 equal-count buckets of the sorted non-NULL values, from `min` to `max` |
diff --git a/minivm-takehome/docs/values.md b/minivm-takehome/docs/values.md
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..6039777
--- /dev/null
+++ b/minivm-takehome/docs/values.md
@@ -0,0 +1,76 @@
+[MiniVM docs](README.md) › Values
+
+# Values
+
+This page describes MiniVM's values and types, what a register holds, and how
+values are ordered and compared.
+
+## Types
+
+| Type | Values | Literal |
+|---|---|---|
+| INT | exact 128-bit integer; a result outside -2^127 .. 2^127-1 is an error | `42` |
+| NUM | decimal: see [NUM range](#num-range) | `12.50` |
+| STR | string | `'it''s'` |
+| DATE | calendar date | `#2024-01-31#` |
+| BOOL | `FALSE` or `TRUE` | `TRUE` |
+
+`NULL` is a value of its own, written `NULL`. Unless an instruction says
+otherwise, an element-wise result is `NULL` when any operand is `NULL`
+([element-wise instructions](instructions/README.md#element-wise-instructions)).
+
+Literal syntax is in [Literals](language.md#literals). Stored columns have
+type INT, NUM, STR or DATE ([table sets](running.md#table-sets)).
+
+## NUM range
+
+A NUM is below 1e28 in magnitude and has at most 28 digits after the point,
+and its digits without the point, read as an integer, are at most
+2^96 - 1 = 79228162514264337593543950335: 28 or 29 significant digits.
+A NUM keeps its digits after the point, so `1.0` and `1.00` are equal but
+print differently.
+
+A NUM literal or stored value must be such a number exactly; trailing zeros
+after the point are dropped as far as needed. A NUM result is rounded half to
+even to the most digits after the point that fit, and a result of magnitude
+1e28 or more is the error `numeric overflow`. Where an INT meets a NUM in
+arithmetic, and in `DIV`, an INT operand above 2^96 - 1 in magnitude is that
+error too, even if the result would fit.
+
+## Columns and group sets
+
+A register holds either:
+
+- a **column**: a vector of values; or
+- a **group set**: a grouping of rows, made by
+  [`GROUP`](instructions/grouping.md#group) or [`GALL`](instructions/grouping.md#gall)
+  and read by the other [grouping instructions](instructions/grouping.md).
+
+Registers can be overwritten.
+
+## Ordering
+
+These instructions compare values:
+
+- [`EQ`, `NE`, `LT`, `LE`, `GT`, `GE`](instructions/logic.md#eq-ne-lt-le-gt-ge) and [`IS`](instructions/logic.md#is)
+- [`SORT`](instructions/sorting.md#sort) and [`TOPK`](instructions/sorting.md#topk)
+- the joins [`NLJOIN`, `HJOIN`](instructions/joins.md#nljoin-hjoin)
+- [`GMIN`, `GMAX`](instructions/grouping.md#gmin-gmax)
+
+They order values as follows:
+
+| Values | Order |
+|---|---|
+| INT and NUM | by numeric value, exactly at any magnitude; INT and NUM mix |
+| STR | by bytes |
+| DATE | by date |
+| BOOL | `FALSE < TRUE` |
+
+Comparing any other mix of types, such as STR with INT or DATE with NUM, is an
+error.
+
+String comparison is exact: there is no case folding, and trailing spaces
+count, so `'ab'` and `'ab '` differ, as do `'ab'` and `'AB'`.
+
+How each instruction treats `NULL` is given on its page; for sorts, see
+[sort keys](instructions/sorting.md#sort-keys).

diff --git a/minivm-takehome/examples/programs/q0001.mini b/minivm-takehome/examples/programs/q0001.mini
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..1d677aa
--- /dev/null
+++ b/minivm-takehome/examples/programs/q0001.mini
@@ -0,0 +1,91 @@
+; Lines of orders with o_date in 2024 and o_amt above 750.00: order, line number and l_prod and a product-and-category label (40 wide), by order and line number.
+; line export for the warehouse (FIN-876)
+LOAD r1, lines.l_order
+LOAD r2, lines.l_no
+LOAD r3, lines.l_prod
+LOAD r4, lines.l_qty
+LOAD r5, lines.l_price
+LOAD r6, lines.l_disc
+SORT r7, r1 ASC NF, r2 ASC NF      ; keep the line order stable for the export
+GATHER r8, r1, r7
+GATHER r9, r2, r7
+GATHER r10, r3, r7
+GATHER r11, r4, r7
+GATHER r12, r5, r7
+GATHER r13, r6, r7
+LOAD r14, products.p_id
+LOAD r15, products.p_name
+LOAD r16, products.p_cat
+HJOIN r17, r18, r10, r14
+GATHER r19, r8, r17
+GATHER r20, r9, r17
+GATHER r21, r10, r17
+GATHER r22, r11, r17
+GATHER r23, r12, r17
+GATHER r24, r13, r17
+GATHER r25, r14, r18
+GATHER r26, r15, r18
+GATHER r27, r16, r18
+LOAD r28, categories.k_id
+LOAD r29, categories.k_name
+HJOIN r30, r31, r27, r28
+GATHER r32, r19, r30
+GATHER r33, r20, r30
+GATHER r34, r21, r30
+GATHER r35, r22, r30
+GATHER r36, r23, r30
+GATHER r37, r24, r30
+GATHER r38, r25, r30
+GATHER r39, r26, r30
+GATHER r40, r27, r30
+GATHER r41, r28, r31
+GATHER r42, r29, r31
+NVL r43, r39, ''
+NVL r44, r42, ''
+CAT r45, r43, ' / '
+CAT r46, r45, r44
+NULLIFE r47, r46
+LEFT r48, r47, 40                  ; sheet column width
+LOAD r49, orders.o_id
+LOAD r50, orders.o_date
+LOAD r51, orders.o_amt
+LOAD r52, orders.o_status
+YEAR r53, r50
+EQ r54, r53, 2024
+GT r55, r51, 750.00
+AND r56, r54, r55
+IDX r57, r56
+GATHER r58, r49, r57
+GATHER r59, r50, r57
+HJOIN r60, r61, r32, r58
+GATHER r62, r32, r60
+GATHER r63, r33, r60
+GATHER r64, r34, r60
+GATHER r65, r35, r60
+GATHER r66, r36, r60
+GATHER r67, r37, r60
+GATHER r68, r38, r60
+GATHER r69, r39, r60
+GATHER r70, r40, r60

+; For each discounted line of an order with o_date in 2023 and o_amt above 300.00: how many discounted lines of the same product sold fewer units.
+load t0_l_o, lines.l_order
+load t1_l_n, lines.l_no
+load t2_l_p, lines.l_prod
+load t3_l_q, lines.l_qty
+load t4_l_d, lines.l_disc
+load t5_l_d, lines.l_disc          ; discount comparison, dropped from the sheet in 2021
+load t6_o_i, orders.o_id
+load t7_o_d, orders.o_date
+load t8_o_a, orders.o_amt
+gt t9_pf, t4_l_d, 0.0
+idx t10_pw, t9_pf
+gather t0_l_o, t0_l_o, t10_pw
+gather t1_l_n, t1_l_n, t10_pw
+gather t2_l_p, t2_l_p, t10_pw
+gather t3_l_q, t3_l_q, t10_pw
+hjoin t11_a, t12_b, t2_l_p, t2_l_p ; every pair with the same key (a row pairs with itself)
+gather t13_va, t3_l_q, t11_a
+gather t14_vb, t3_l_q, t12_b
+lt t15_hit, t14_vb, t13_va
+if t16_one, t15_hit, 1, 0
+group t17_g, t11_a
+gsum t18_cnt, t17_g, t16_one
+gfirst t19_f, t17_g
+gather t20_pos, t11_a, t19_f
+year t21_fa_, t7_o_d
+eq t22_fa, t21_fa_, 2023
+gt t23_fb, t8_o_a, 300.00
+and t19_f, t22_fa, t23_fb
+idx t24_ow, t19_f
+gather t25_moi, t6_o_i, t24_ow
+gather t26_lo_, t0_l_o, t20_pos
+gather t27_ln_, t1_l_n, t20_pos
+hjoin t28_c, t29_d, t26_lo_, t25_moi
+gather t30_r1, t26_lo_, t28_c
+gather t31_r2, t27_ln_, t28_c
+gather t32_r3, t18_cnt, t28_c
+sort t33_s, t30_r1 ASC NF, t31_r2 ASC NF
+gather t34_o1, t30_r1, t33_s
+gather t35_o2, t31_r2, t33_s
+gather t36_o3, t32_r3, t33_s
+out t34_o1, t35_o2, t36_o3

+; The 4 orders with the latest order id of each status (ignoring case and trailing blanks) (ties by lower id), by order id.
+; top-n per group, ported from the old SQL (count of better rows) (WH-1307)
+; runs every night before the warehouse sync
+load    r1, orders.o_id
+load    r2, orders.o_cust
+load    r3, orders.o_status
+nullife r4, r3
+rtrim   r5, r4
+upper   r6, r5
+hjoin   r7, r8, r6, r6             ; every pair of orders in the same group
+gather  r9, r1, r7
+gather  r10, r1, r8
+gather  r11, r1, r7
+gather  r12, r1, r8
+gt      r13, r10, r9
+eq      r14, r10, r9
+lt      r15, r12, r11
+and     r16, r14, r15              ; equal value: lower id wins
+or      r17, r13, r16
+if      r18, r17, 1, 0
+group   r19, r7
+gsum    r20, r19, r18
+gfirst  r21, r19
+gather  r22, r7, r21
+lt      r23, r20, 4
+idx     r24, r23
+gather  r25, r22, r24
+gather  r26, r1, r25
+gather  r27, r2, r25
+gather  r28, r3, r25
+sort    r29, r26 ASC NF
+gather  r30, r26, r29
+gather  r31, r27, r29
+gather  r32, r28, r29
+out     r30, r32, r30

+; Revenue per category for orders with at least one card payment, by category id.
+; finance wants it per category id (OPS-792)
+; ported from the old reporting job, kept as close to the original as possible
+LOAD r1, lines.l_order
+LOAD r2, lines.l_no
+LOAD r3, lines.l_prod
+LOAD r4, lines.l_qty
+LOAD r5, lines.l_price
+LOAD r6, lines.l_disc
+LOAD r7, payments.y_order
+LOAD r8, payments.y_method
+LOAD r9, payments.y_amt
+NULLIFE r10, r8
+RTRIM r11, r10
+UPPER r12, r11
+IS r13, r12, 'CARD'
+IDX r14, r13
+GATHER r15, r7, r14
+GATHER r16, r8, r14
+GATHER r17, r9, r14
+HJOIN r18, r19, r1, r15
+GATHER r20, r1, r18
+GATHER r21, r2, r18
+GATHER r22, r3, r18
+GATHER r23, r4, r18
+GATHER r24, r5, r18
+GATHER r25, r6, r18
+GATHER r26, r15, r19
+GATHER r27, r16, r19
+GATHER r28, r17, r19               ; payments joined straight onto lines, de-duplicated below
+SORT r29, r20 ASC NF, r21 ASC NF   ; line order for the de-dup
+GATHER r30, r20, r29
+GATHER r31, r21, r29
+GATHER r32, r22, r29
+GATHER r33, r23, r29
+GATHER r34, r24, r29
+GATHER r35, r25, r29
+GATHER r36, r26, r29
+GATHER r37, r27, r29
+GATHER r38, r28, r29
+GROUP r39, r30, r31                ; one row per line even when paid several times
+GFIRST r40, r39
+GATHER r41, r30, r40
+GATHER r42, r31, r40
+GATHER r43, r32, r40
+GATHER r44, r33, r40
+GATHER r45, r34, r40
+GATHER r46, r35, r40
+GATHER r47, r36, r40
+GATHER r48, r37, r40
+GATHER r49, r38, r40
+LOAD r50, products.p_id
+LOAD r51, products.p_cat
+NLJOIN r52, r53, r43, r50
+GATHER r54, r41, r52
+GATHER r55, r42, r52
+GATHER r56, r43, r52
+GATHER r57, r44, r52
+GATHER r58, r45, r52
+GATHER r59, r46, r52
+GATHER r60, r47, r52
+GATHER r61, r48, r52
+GATHER r62, r49, r52
+GATHER r63, r50, r53
+GATHER r64, r51, r53
+SUB r65, 1, r59
+MUL r66, r57, r58
+MUL r67, r66, r65
+LOAD r68, categories.k_id
+LOAD r69, categories.k_name
+GROUP r70, r64
+GFIRST r71, r70
+GATHER r72, r64, r71
+GSUM r73, r70, r67
+NLJOIN r74, r75, r72, r68
+GATHER r76, r72, r74
+GATHER r77, r69, r75
+GATHER r78, r73, r74
+SORT r79, r76 ASC NF
+GATHER r80, r77, r79

+; Each order with its status (ignoring case and trailing blanks)'s largest o_qty (among orders with o_date at least #2023-01-01#), by id.
+; correlated lookup ported from the old SQL (one row per group member)
+LOAD    r1, orders.o_id
+LOAD    r2, orders.o_status
+LOAD    r3, orders.o_qty
+LOAD    r4, orders.o_amt
+LOAD    r5, orders.o_date
+GE      r6, r5, #2023-01-01#
+IDX     r7, r6
+GATHER  r8, r1, r7
+GATHER  r9, r2, r7
+GATHER  r10, r3, r7
+GATHER  r11, r4, r7
+GATHER  r12, r5, r7
+NULLIFE r13, r9
+RTRIM   r14, r13
+UPPER   r15, r14
+HJOIN   r16, r17, r15, r15         ; each row against every row of its group
+GATHER  r18, r10, r17
+GROUP   r19, r16
+GMAX    r20, r19, r18
+GFIRST  r21, r19
+GATHER  r22, r16, r21
+GATHER  r23, r8, r22
+GATHER  r24, r9, r22
+GATHER  r25, r10, r22
+GATHER  r26, r11, r22
+GATHER  r27, r12, r22
+IOTA    r28, r20
+GATHER  r29, r20, r28
+SORT    r30, r23 ASC NF
+GATHER  r31, r23, r30
+GATHER  r32, r25, r30
+GATHER  r33, r26, r30
+GATHER  r34, r29, r30
+OUT     r31, r33, r34

+; The 5 orders with the largest amount, among those with o_date in 2023-07, ties by order id.
+; bulk orders report
+load	r1, orders.o_id
+load	r2, orders.o_cust
+load	r3, orders.o_date
+load	r4, orders.o_amt
+load	r5, orders.o_qty
+load	r6, orders.o_status
+load	r7, orders.o_note
+nvl	r8, r7, ''
+nvl	r9, r6, ''
+cat	r10, r8, ' '
+cat	r11, r10, r9
+nullife	r12, r11
+left	r13, r12, 40                  ; sheet column width
+upper	r14, r7                      ; display copy
+dow	r15, r3                        ; weekday split (removed)
+sort	r16, r4 DESC NL, r1 ASC NF
+gather	r17, r1, r16
+gather	r18, r2, r16
+gather	r19, r3, r16
+gather	r20, r4, r16
+gather	r21, r5, r16
+gather	r22, r6, r16
+gather	r23, r7, r16
+gather	r24, r13, r16
+year	r25, r19
+eq	r26, r25, 2023
+month	r27, r19
+eq	r28, r27, 7
+and	r29, r26, r28
+idx	r30, r29
+gather	r31, r17, r30
+gather	r32, r18, r30
+gather	r33, r19, r30
+gather	r34, r20, r30
+gather	r35, r21, r30
+gather	r36, r22, r30
+gather	r37, r23, r30
+gather	r38, r24, r30
+iota	r39, r31
+slice	r39, r39, 5
+gather	r40, r31, r39
+gather	r41, r32, r39
+gather	r42, r33, r39
+gather	r43, r34, r39
+gather	r44, r35, r39
+gather	r45, r36, r39
+gather	r46, r37, r39
+gather	r47, r38, r39
+out	r40, r43, r47

@@ -0,0 +1,32 @@
+; Order count, total quantity per order status (ignoring case and trailing blanks), for orders with o_qty at least 5; biggest first, then by name (first 10 characters).
+LOAD    o_id, orders.o_id
+LOAD    o_cust, orders.o_cust
+LOAD    o_date, orders.o_date
+LOAD    o_amt, orders.o_amt
+LOAD    o_qty, orders.o_qty
+LOAD    o_status, orders.o_status
+LOAD    o_note, orders.o_note
+GE      f, o_qty, 5
+IDX     w_w, f
+GATHER  w_o_id, o_id, w_w
+GATHER  w_o_cust, o_cust, w_w
+GATHER  w_o_date, o_date, w_w
+GATHER  w_o_amt, o_amt, w_w
+GATHER  w_o_qty, o_qty, w_w
+GATHER  w_o_status, o_status, w_w
+GATHER  w_o_note, o_note, w_w
+NULLIFE xk, w_o_status
+RTRIM   k_t, xk
+UPPER   k, k_t
+GROUP   g, k
+GMIN    rep, g, xk                 ; one spelling per group
+GCOUNT  a0, g
+GSUM    a1, g, w_o_qty
+LEFT    sk_l, rep, 10
+RTRIM   sk_t, sk_l
+UPPER   sk, sk_t
+SORT    q, a0 DESC NL, sk ASC NF
+GATHER  o_rep, rep, q
+GATHER  o_a0, a0, q
